# Verified offsets: history

This file keeps `VERIFIED_OFFSETS.md` word for word as it stood before each restructuring: on 2026-10-22 (end of session 40, first section below), before it became a dictionary, and on 2026-11-10 (session 66, second section, under the heading `# Verified offsets and formats (as of 2026-11-10, session 66, before the second restructuring)`), before its findings were grouped by topic. Each keeps every row and lead with its full text, evidence and corrections; findings F320 to F480 and about 140 leads are only in the second. `VERIFIED_OFFSETS.md` keeps one line per fact; look an address, field, finding (F number) or lead (L number) up here for the full story.

---

| 'It's all over' on Mickey's white screen; Game Over update state 8 | The white screen's callback `exe+0x3FCC50` with option 8 fades to black (`exe+0x156CE0(fade, 0, 0)`), calls `exe+0x3FD2B0(obj)`, opens the **normal Continue / Load menu** (`exe+0x2E4910(*(obj+0x18), obj+0x50)`; the same callback `exe+0x3FCBE0`, Continue = 2) and sets state 8, Mickey byte +0x14 still 1. The update's state table (`exe+0x3FD100`: 0 `3FCEE9`, 1 `3FCEF8`, 2 `3FCF00`, 3 `3FCFF7`, 4 `3FD006`, 5 `3FD080`, 6 `3FCFDA`, 7 `3FD0EE`, 8 `3FD0F8`) has state 8 = `return 0` (the Game Over ends), but the game doesn't step the object while that menu is up: it stays at state 8. Calling the update ourselves at state 8 ended it before the menu showed (black screen with the loading heart); in a game whose white menu never opened (the Q6 loser) the task (`exe+0x3FCA90`, which just calls the update and ends on 0) stepped it on the next frame anyway, also ended. Why the winner's isn't stepped (not checked): the white menu's open `exe+0x2E4C20` does more than the Continue / Load open `exe+0x2E4910` (= `exe+0x31D860` + `exe+0x31D850(9)`): it also calls `exe+0x2EB810` and `exe+0x2E4950`. Continue (`exe+0x3FCBE0(2)`) never touches the object: `exe+0x155E10`, `exe+0x3A0520`, `exe+0x152990` start the reload; `exe+0x1532D0` (called in option 8) is an empty `ret` | struct / flow | Disasm (tools/disasm.py 3FCC50, 3FCEC0, table read from the exe) + bench `mickey_coop --end over` 2026-10-18 01:17 (host kh2coop_7104: state (8, 1) for 10 s and the Continue / Load screen, host Continue worked; friend kh2coop_14964: object gone, black) | 2026-10-18 |

Given another buffer and size, the pool works there: the handles of a buffer anywhere in memory are added to the base table `exe+0x2B0D720` by themselves (10-15 of 64 slots in use with ours) | function | Bench, session 34: our hook (`src/dll/fx_pool.cpp`) passed a 4096 KB VirtualAlloc buffer at each load, the pool's start/end gave 4096 KB; Data Roxas (`test_census.py data_roxas --friend-attacks`): the friend's game reached 1670 KB in use (the old pool holds 512 KB) with no crash, effects shown | 2026-10-13 |

| Live effects and their particles: `*(exe+0xB1D988)` | The effect update `exe+0x2BD190` walks the live effect list from `*(exe+0xB1D988)` (a pointer; next = handle at +0x40, decoded with the `exe+0x2B0D720` table like actor handles, `exe+0x4AD3F0`). An effect (0x58 bytes, built by `exe+0x2BD510`): +0x8 kind, +0xC flags, +0x10 owner (`actor+0x80` when an actor started it through `exe+0x3B6670` -> `exe+0x2C6B30`), +0x20 effect data, +0x30 its particle system, +0x38 / +0x3C fade. The system: +0x58 time, +0x68 group count, +0x88 groups (0x18 each: +0 group data, +8 first particle, +0x10 next spawn time, +0x14 u16 live particles); `exe+0x2C28E0(system, on)` steps it and calls `exe+0x4B0FD0`, where `exe+0x4AFF20` spawns particles from the effect pool (data+0x20 + (data+0x26) x 8 bytes each). An actor's effect starts are kept at `actor+0x80` (+0x18 newest, +0x20 oldest; 0x78-byte records: +0x40 the effect, +0x48 its entry (u16 effect id at +2), +0x50 the actor it is attached to, +0x58 f32 age (+1 per frame, `exe+0x2C5BE0`), +0x70 flags (bit 0 = looping, from the entry), +0x74 next (handle)). Functions that stop an effect (Ghidra; which one fades not checked): `exe+0x2BE8F0(effect)`, `exe+0x2BE6A0(effect)`, also `exe+0x2C7300(record)` (used when the attached actor goes). Xemnas's looping lasers end on their own once they are stepped (row 'Effects pause off-screen') | data / fn | Two-copy bench 2026-10-13 (session 31, `bench.fx_census`, `test_extra_copy.py xemnas --fx-census`, read from outside every 1 s): the walk found the effects the game's own trace logs (Xemnas's ids 28-40, `KH2COOP_FX_TRACE=1`), their particles add up to ~75% of the pool's bytes in use (the rest = the effects' own parts), ages climb 60 per second | 2026-10-13 |
| Effects pause off-screen: `exe+0x2C28E0(system, offscreen)` | The effect update `exe+0x2BD190` asks `exe+0x1702C0(view, box)` for each effect (box from the system's +0xA0 data, `exe+0x2C1FC0`); it returns 1 when the box is outside the camera's view (`exe+0x186130`, clip test of 8 corners) or behind one of the occluders (`exe+0xABD480` count, 5 planes each from `exe+0xABD4D0`, `exe+0x185F00`). The step `exe+0x2C28E0(system, offscreen)` (only caller `exe+0x2BD3AB`; prologue `48 89 5C 24 08 57 48 83 EC 30`) sets system+0xA8 = offscreen and, when offscreen and the data's byte +0xF8 is set, returns without stepping: the effect doesn't age, so a looping one can't end. Final Xemnas's looping lasers (ids 28-40) are never stopped by his AI script (`KH2COOP_FX_STOP_TRACE=1`: 3-10 starts per id every 10 s, 0 stops through `exe+0x41E1C0` / `exe+0x41E270`); they end once stepped. In co-op the boss can fight the other player away from this game's camera, so they paused and piled up. Fix (downed.cpp, `KH2COOP_FX_OFFSCREEN_RUN=1`, default): in co-op the step gets offscreen = 0 for effects whose data pauses them | fn / behaviour | Two-copy bench 2026-10-13 (session 31): census (`test_extra_copy.py xemnas --fx-census`) of the long-lived lasers in the overflowing game: all with +0xA8 = 1, time +0x58 stuck at 4096, +0xB0 at 0; the ones that ended had +0xA8 = 0. With the fix 3 runs, no crash: host pool peaks 99 / 120 / 99 KB (lasers live 0-0.1 s on average), two reached the grab in both games (the third landed no hit on Xemnas, so no grab); before it most runs that night overflowed (480-507 KB). Normal fights don't use more of the pool with it (`test_census.py --pool --cast`, 90 s): Twin Lords 147 / 148 KB with it, 156 / 160 KB without; Borough 35 / 25 KB with, 25 / 25 KB without | 2026-10-13 |
| Final Xemnas effect pool with the extra copy | With the extra copy the host's effect pool can climb ~16 KB/s once Xemnas's HP is dropped to 200 and overflow (crash `exe+0x4B00DA`); the friend's game stays normal, and without the copy it never happened. The host's Xemnas then never plays `re_b_ex170-40`. **More often with the bench's god mode** (`KH2COOP_GOD=1`, a hit lands as -1 HP then the HP is put back): 4 crashes in 5 runs, ~118 s into the run. **Also without god mode**: with the HP kept full by the test writing the stat slots (`test_extra_copy.py xemnas --hp`) one run peaked at 282 KB at ~110 s and was back at ~20 KB 10 s later (240+ s, no crash), but a run with `KH2COOP_NET_SIM=150,30,2` crashed at 222 s (peak 502 KB). Without the copy (GOD=1): peak 115-148 KB, no crash, the grab came. Cause not found yet: both games start the same Xemnas effects (row `exe+0x42D540`: ids 28-35 on Xemnas, 8 per volley; none on the copy) and play the same Xemnas motions at the same moments (204 → 215 → 205/206 → 207, `xemnas --motions`); the climb starts with 215/205, while his volleys hit the copy in the host's game every ~2 s (3-5 × -4, each blocked as an HP change on the copy, so the copy never reacts there); the host's particle spawns (`exe+0x4AFF20`) pile up | behaviour | Two-copy bench 2026-10-12 10:01-11:27 (session 28), tools/test_extra_copy.py xemnas with PoolWatch (pool sampled every 0.5 s); runs: copy+GOD 5 (4 crashes), PUPPET=0+GOD 2, copy+GOD=0 1 (Game Over at ~123 s, peak 163 KB), copy+GOD=0+--hp 1, copy+GOD=0+--hp+NET_SIM 2 (both crashed, 222 s and 169 s), copy+GOD+--motions 1 (crash), copy+GOD+`PUPPET_COLLIDE=0` 1 (crash; the lasers still hit the copy, 38 blocked HP changes, so that setting doesn't take the copy out of the hits), copy+GOD+`FORWARD_HITS=0` 1 (crash at 177 s: hits on the copy not blocked by the mod, they went through the game's HP function with god mode's -1; so the mod's blocked-hit path isn't the cause). **Session 31 (2026-10-13): the hits on the copy are not the cause:** with the research setting `KH2COOP_TEST_COPY_TEAM=0` (puppet.cpp: the copy's team `+0x4DC` held at 0 every frame, both games) Xemnas's lasers never hit the copy in the two runs that crashed (0 HP changes on it, against 107-150 per run without it), and the host's game still crashed at `exe+0x4B00DA` in 2 of 3 runs (pool peaks 507 and 506 KB at 119 s and 179 s; the third peaked at 204 KB and reached the grab); the friend's game 85-167 KB. Same night without the setting: 1 crash in 2 runs (496 KB at 182 s; the other 275 KB). **What piles up (census, session 31):** not more particles per effect but more live effects: Xemnas's looping laser effects (ids 28-40, one effect data, attached to Xemnas himself, 3-4 starts per id every 10 s in both games) live 10 s on average (median 11 s, up to 19 s) in the game that overflows, so ~11 of each id are alive together (236 effects at the peak); in the other game they live 0.6 s on average. Their ages keep climbing, so they are not frozen; nothing stops them. Without the copy (`KH2COOP_PUPPET=0`, 1 run): median 0 s, mean 1 s on the host, pool 125 KB. **Not the nearest-player swap:** with `KH2COOP_AI_NEAREST_PLAYER=0` (both games) 2 runs still overflowed, one of them in the **friend's** game (first time; its lasers median 7 s). **Cause (session 31):** those lasers were paused off-screen (row 'Effects pause off-screen'): the game whose camera didn't see Xemnas (he was after the other player) kept them all; fixed by letting such effects run off-screen in co-op | 2026-10-12, 2026-10-13 |

| Companion heal choice: 'needs help' `actor+0xF78` (think `exe+0x1B53B0`), Cure `exe+0x1CC1A0`, HP item `exe+0x1CC970`, item use `exe+0x1B7D90` | The companions' type handler table `exe+0x5B3880`: +0x10 = `exe+0x1B0020` (jumps to `exe+0x3C3660`, the per-actor update; puppet.cpp `HookFriendAi` and world sync's companion skip use it), +0x20 = `exe+0x1B0040` (jumps to `exe+0x1B15F0`, the brain), which calls the think callback at `actor+0xE00` (constructor `exe+0x1B06E0`: `exe+0x1B53B0`; `exe+0x1CB050` for one special type). In a fight the think sets `actor+0xF78` = who needs help: the player (entry 0 of the companion table `exe+0xAD6A18`, filled from `exe+0x2A105D0` by the party AI tick `exe+0x1B5E30`), else itself (type `actor+0xE08` < 0xE), else the other companion: the first at HP <= max / 4 (`exe+0x3C1D30(stats)`). Then it tries the emergency actions first (`exe+0x1B3A30`: the helper `actor+0xDF8`'s action entries flagged 0x10, then 0x20), else the normal pick `exe+0x1B1D40`. The current action is the record at `actor+0xEE8` (handler at +8), its target the handle at `actor+0xBF8`. The heal actions test three candidates only: the companion itself, `exe+0x1B4B70(0)` (table entry 0, the player) and the other companion (`exe+0x1B4AF0`), reading +0xF78 through `exe+0x1B4940`: `exe+0x1CC1A0` (flags 0x10) = **Cure**, `exe+0x1CC970` (flags 0x18; `exe+0x1B3310(actor, f)` = HP < max x f; items listed at `exe+0x7248B8`) = an **HP item** (Potion, id 1), used through `exe+0x1B7D90(companion, item, target, u32)`. (`exe+0x1CCE90` tests MP: +0x180 < +0x184 x f, MP since verified, row 'Stat block' 2026-10-21; probably the companion's MP-low action, not checked live.) So the extra Sora copy is never a heal candidate: it isn't in the table, and its stat block is our own Sora's | fn / struct | Two-copy bench 2026-10-13 (session 32, `test_extra_copy.py heal`, `KH2COOP_HEAL_TRACE=1`, save 20 in the Twin Lords fight, god mode for the party and the enemies, test command lowhp): host's Sora held at 5/36: Donald 'needs help our Sora', action 0x1CC1A0, then 'our Sora +38 from a Cure'; Aladdin and Donald action 0x1CC970, 'uses item 1 on our Sora', then 'item 1 lands', +22 / +15; host down: Donald's Cure got it up at once (downed rule, 'a Cure (+38) reached our Sora'). Friend's Sora at 5/36 for 40 s, then down for 36 s: the host's Donald and Aladdin never had anyone in 'needs help' (0 of ~4,500 thinks each), no heal on the copy | 2026-10-13 |

| Cutscene scripts (events) | A room's `ard/<lang>/<ww><rr>.ard` (BAR) holds its cutscenes as entries of type **0x16**, named by event number (TT 02/04 Sandlot: 109-112, 192, 308/309, 317, 712, 903, 904, 308v). Each is a list of commands `{u16 size, u16 type, data}` ended by size 0, layout as in OpenKH `OpenKh.Kh2/Ard/Event.cs`: type 0x01 SetActor `{s16 object, s16 actor id, cstring name}` makes the scene's own actors; 0x02 SeqActorPosition (pos, rotation in degrees, actor id @+0x20, frame @+0x22); 0x2C SeqPlayAnimation (frames, actor id @+0x0A, cstring path @+0x0E, e.g. `anm_ex/EEX00016A`); 0x1A SeqActorLeave. **The cutscene's Sora is its own actor, not the field Sora:** object 571 `ACTOR_SORA` (and 572 `ACTOR_SORA_H` for close-ups), Keyblade as a separate actor 573 `SORA_WEAPON_R` playing the same animation from `anm_*_r/`; Donald 92 / Goofy 93 are event actors too, so the field party (and our Sora copy) isn't in the scene. 581 of the 951 events in `ard/us` have a Sora actor; actor ids go up to 73 in one event. The game's language has its own folder (`ard/sp` for Spanish, 289 files) | data | Parsed this PC's OpenKH extraction 2026-10-09 (session 20, scratchpad `evtdump.py` / `evtsurvey.py`): all 951 `ard/us` events read cleanly; TT 02/04 event 904 = the Sandlot scene with Seifer's gang (user screenshot) with ACTOR_SORA as actor 0, its Keyblade actor 3 and 16, ACTOR_SORA_H actor 12. Not checked at runtime: how the game runs these commands | 2026-10-09 |
| Model files on PC | A model is `obj/<name>.mdlx` (+ `.mset` motions, `.a.us` effects) inside the game's archives (OpenKH extraction: `openkh\data\kh2\obj`); **the HD (remastered) textures are stored per model file name** as `remastered/obj/<name>.mdlx/-0.dds, -1.dds…`, so a model under a new file name needs its texture folder copied too. Sora has 43 model files: P_EX100 (normal), forms _BTLF Valor, _MAGF Wisdom, _KH1F Limit, _TRIF Master, _ULTF Final, _HTLF Anti (+_HTLF_BTL), world costumes _NM, _XM, _TR, _WI (each with the 6 forms), and _AL_CARPET, _LAST, _MEMO, _NPC, _SIDECAR, _TR_LIGHTCYCLE, _WM; normal Sora's Keyblade family W_EX010* has 106 model files. Texture-only mods in KH PC Patch Manager format (`.kh2pcpatch` = ZIP of `<archive>/remastered/obj/<name>.mdlx/-N.dds`) write into the game's own archives; Sora's HD textures sit in `kh2_sixth`, the H_EX500 family (probably Sora's cutscene model) in `kh2_fifth`. A texture swap applies to every actor whose model has that file name | files | Listing of this PC's OpenKH extraction; contents of the "Dirp Sora" patch (6 textures for P_EX100, same count as the original, 1024 px instead of 2048) (2026-10-08) | 2026-10-08 |

| A third party gauge: the HUD's friend gauge built once more | The HUD is built by `exe+0x16C3A0` (from `exe+0x168B30`) and taken down by `exe+0x16C2B0` (from `exe+0x168B10`) **at every room load**, not once per game. Every gauge part is a 2D element in the HUD system's list (`*(exe+0xABCD20)` +8 = first element; element +8 = next element's handle, decoded by `exe+0x4AD270`; +0x10 flags: 0x2 hidden and 0x100 paused (set per group by `exe+0x166BD0` / `exe+0x167310`, cleared by `exe+0x167470` / `exe+0x167200`), 0x40 = no actor (`exe+0x167020` / `exe+0x167070`); +0x14 = group), and each element gets a HUD task that calls its vtable +8 every frame (`exe+0x1667D0`), so a gauge built and set up the game's way is updated and drawn with no further call. Groups: 3 = player gauge, 4 / 5 = friend gauges 0 / 1, **6 = the boss HP bar** (`dk::GAUGE_ENEMY`, `exe+0x182DD0`). `dk::GAUGE_FRIEND` (0x1710 bytes, ctor `exe+0x16B5A0`, vtable `exe+0x5B2158`: +8 update `exe+0x182730`, +0x18 destructor `exe+0x16BE30` (bit 0 = free through the HUD allocator; it doesn't unlink its elements, the HUD teardown drops the whole list), +0x28 refresh `exe+0x182400`, +0x48 HP changed `exe+0x182360`). Set-up `exe+0x181D20(gauge, actor, index)` passes a hard-coded 1 (`mov r9d, 1` at `exe+0x181D41`) as `gauge+0x34`: group = +0x34 + 3 + index, flag word = +0x34 + index (`exe+0x1687F0` test / `exe+0x168AE0` clear), list entry its refresh reads = index + 1 (getter `exe+0x1689A0`); it moves the gauge up by its height x index (`gauge+0x370+0x1C4` = -96 for index 2: one gauge = 48). Friend gauge 1 slides down to gauge 0's place while list entry 1 is empty (its update, index 1 only). The face picture goes to its own place in the emulated GS memory per index: TBP 0x3500 + the sum of the first index+1 entries of `exe+0x71AE10` {0x200, 0x24, 0x24, 0}, CBP 0x2800 + `exe+0x71AE20` {0x10, 0x10, 0x10, 0}. **Built as a third gauge for the Sora copy (party_hud.cpp):** our memory, our vtable copy with +8 ours, set-up at index 2 with the base patched to 2 for the call (group 7, flag word 4, list entry 3, answered by hooks on the getter and the flag test / clear), the HUD bits copied from friend gauge 1, destroyed (no free) before the game's teardown | data / fn / behaviour | Two-copy bench 2026-10-13 (session 30, `tools/test_extra_copy.py hud` / `hud2` / `summon` / `downed` / `alone`, `tools/test_census.py bc` / `twins`): the copy's gauge drawn above Goofy and Donald in both games with its own face (no other HUD picture damaged), its ring following the other player's HP (host at 30/60: half ring on the friend's) and the red KO face when they're down; Valor Form and a summon moved the copy into the freed entry and back; the boss HP bar shown beside it; a HUD teardown + build at every room load, Game Over, the friend leaving and rejoining: no crash | 2026-10-13 |
| Enemy HP bar: what moves it | `dk::GAUGE_ENEMY` (0x4B30 bytes, vtable `exe+0x5B21E0`: +8 update `exe+0x1835F0`), built with the HUD at every room load, pointer `exe+0xABD260` (getter `exe+0x16C360`): +0x4B08 = HP shown, +0x4B0C = max, +0x4B10 = the HP before the last hit (the red part), +0x4B28 = on; 200 HP per bar (more = the stacked boss bars). Its functions (Ghidra): show `exe+0x183520(hp, max)`, set `exe+0x183220(gauge, hp)` (jumps), hit `exe+0x1832A0(gauge, hp)` (drops, lost part in red), hide `exe+0x1833E0`. Which enemy it shows: the target record at `*(exe+0x2A24E90)`: +4 the actor's handle, +0x28 the gauge while shown, +0x30 the stat channel; `exe+0x3DD440` shows it, reading HP and max from the stat block (`*(actor+0x5C0)` + channel x 0xC) only then. After that **only the game's stat change moves it**: `exe+0x3D2EB0(actor, delta, channel, flag)` (twin `exe+0x3D2C50`) changes the stat (`exe+0x3C0860`), then calls `exe+0x3DCC10(actor, channel, new)` for a loss or `exe+0x3DCBB0(actor, channel, new)` for a gain (both do nothing unless actor and channel are the bar's; prologue `48 89 5C 24 08 48 89 74 24 10 57 48 83 EC 20 48 8B 05`), and the death (actor vtable +0xB0) at 0 on channel 0. HP written straight into the stat block (world sync's mirrored enemy HP on the friend) didn't move the bar (TODO 1.21); world_sync.cpp now calls `exe+0x3DCC10` / `exe+0x3DCBB0` after each mirrored HP write. Normal enemies show the bar while locked on (R1) | data / fn / behaviour | Two-copy bench 2026-10-13 (session 31, `tools/test_census.py twins --bar-watch`, sampling the target record, the gauge and the enemy's HP every 0.1 s): before the fix the friend's bar showed the enemy's real HP 55% of the time (stuck up to 4.3 s), the host's 100%; with the calls 100% on both (friend's bar up 40 s, one Lord 374 -> 259 followed), and `borough --lock-on`: Shadows' bars 100% on both | 2026-10-13 |
| Face layout (SEQD, entry "face" type 0x19 in `obj/P_EX100.a.us` etc.) | Used in place from memory (`exe+0x192EB0`/`exe+0x196010` add the header offsets to its start, so a copy elsewhere works). Header u32: +0x08 sprite count / +0x0C offset (0x2C each: texture rect), +0x10 part count / +0x14 offset (0x14 each: s32 left, top, right, bottom = the on-screen quad around the anchor, u32 sprite), +0x18 group count / +0x1C offset (u16 first part, u16 count), +0x20 animation count / +0x24 offset (0x90 each; scale floats 1.0 at +0x48..+0x5C), +0x28 animation group count / +0x2C offset (**0x24 each**). The face widget picks animation group 3 for the normal state, 0 for low HP, 2 for KO (`exe+0x17EB30`/`exe+0x17EBA0`). Sora's quads are 70×95 (player gauge size), Goofy's 47×47; scaling Sora's part quads to 50% in a copy of the layout handed to the friend widget (caller `exe+0x17E9AC` of `exe+0x3B57B0`) draws his face inside the ring | data | Files parsed (Sora's and Goofy's layouts are both 0x4EC bytes, 4 groups × 0x24 end exactly there); two-copy bench 2026-10-09: with a 0x18 group size the copy cut off group 3 and the normal face was missing (the low-HP face showed), with 0x24 the half-size face shows in the normal and low-HP states on both copies | 2026-10-09 |

| Cutscene command handlers get (command, task) | The command walker `exe+0x2CB5D0` calls every Seq handler with the command in rcx and **its own task in rdx** (`mov rdx, rsi; call ...`, e.g. `0x2CB751` → SeqPlayAnimation `exe+0x2D2160`). The walker runs as a fiber: a handler whose start frame hasn't come yet waits in `exe+0x2C87F0(frame)`, which yields through `exe+0x150060(task)` (writes `task+0x70` → +0x1C, then `exe+0x135470` SwitchToFiber). **A hook on a handler must pass rdx through**, else the first wait crashes at `exe+0x150064` (`89 50 1C`, rax = 0) | calling convention | Two-copy bench 2026-10-10 (session 21): our SeqPlayAnimation hook forwarded only rcx; both copies crashed at `exe+0x150064` with rdx = 0, 0.6 s into TT scene 706 (stack 0x2C881A ← 0x2D2172 ← our hook ← walker 0x2CB75C); forwarding (rcx, rdx) fixed it: 706 and 707 played on both copies with the hook installed | 2026-10-10 |

Readable from outside the game | fn | Read-only probe 2026-10-10 (scratchpad s21 `sceneprobe.py`): decoded every handle in the cutscene actor table to actors with sane positions/angles that moved as the scene played | 2026-10-10 |

Animation slots: 0x40 rows of 0x38 bytes {object, id, handle, ..., +0x10 animation, +0x18 f32 elapsed (+1 per frame), +0x20/+0x22 s16 loop frames, +0x24 base position, +0x34 u8 'take the base position from the actor' flag}; per-frame updater `exe+0x2D1C80`. In a story scene the field copy is gone, so it took the twin (or the scene's own Sora / other player-class scene actors) as "the copy": held it idle every frame (writes `actor+0x670` from our hook, called from `exe+0x3BF6C4`) and its idle SetMotion cleared the twin's motion pointer (`actor+0x170` → 0 at `exe+0x3C8AB2`, one frame after creation). In the next scene in 02/09 it drove scene actors with the peer's pose | cause | Debug-register write watch on the twin's and Sora's `+0x170` / `+0x670` (trace build, `KH2COOP_TWIN_TRACE=1`, bench 2026-10-10 session 22, both copies): the twin's only extra writer was that hook, every frame from the frame after its creation, while the log said `puppet: Sora copy found (actor exe+0x2539C50)` = the twin. | Walk-in event areas (type-3 spawn groups) | a walk-in story area is a spawn group of **type 3** (descriptor byte 0; doors are type 1, enemies type 2). GroupCheck `exe+0x3FF000(group, pos)` calls GroupTrigger `exe+0x3FE320(group, box)` for each box holding Sora; GroupTrigger switches on type-1: type 1 → `exe+0x3FFB40` (door), type 2 → spawner `exe+0x3FE6F0`, **type 3 → `exe+0x3AABE0(descriptor +0x1C settings number, descriptor +2 group id)` and sets group+4 bit 0 (done)**. `exe+0x3AABE0(n, group)` is a general 'fire trigger n' (also called by event ends with 0x4C); for most n it ends in `exe+0x3F60D0(n, group)`, which picks the area settings entry from **`exe+0x2AE6ED0` (16 × 0x60: +0 id, +4 group or -1, +0x10 event name as 4 chars, +0x14, +0x38 byte, +0x3C jump data)**, applies it (`exe+0x3F61A0`) and either jumps (`exe+0x3A46E0` → RequestTransition at `exe+0x3A49D9`, no event) or plays the event with `exe+0x3AC350(event, +0x14, jump)` **in place** (no load) and makes the jump after it. Spawn group table `exe+0x2A10010`: entry +8 = the group object (+4 flags, +8 descriptor, +0x10 first box). Room file: in 02/19 event program 1 (`evt` script, AreaSettings #9: story flag 0x8A3, event '802', then a jump type 2) the type-3 group **45** (`e_01`, box centre (-318, -480, 237), half-size (499, 500, 1237)) fires settings 9 = the Pete scene. | fn / data | Live 2026-10-10 14:55 (session 24, two copies, logging build): the friend's Sora walked toward the tower in 02/19 (programs 2/0/1) and its log showed `cutscene 'tt_event_802' starts in 0x02/0x19 programs 2/0/1; from ? <- 0x3AC465 <- 0x3F6163 <- 0x3FE3C1 <- 0x3FF153 <- 0x3A5063` (GroupTrigger's type-3 case → settings runner → cutscene start, no RequestTransition); the host's game, standing outside the area, logged nothing and stayed in the field (screenshot) | 2026-10-10 |

| One-frame displacement `actor+0xA48` | f32 x, y, z added to the actor's move for one frame, like a push, and cleared by the move: adding to it before the per-entity update moves the actor that much the same frame through the terrain collision (a wall stops it), without changing its motion. Call chain (Ghidra): the per-entity update `exe+0x3BFD30` calls the move `exe+0x3B89A0(actor)`: it saves the position to `+0xA18`, sums velocity `+0xB98` × dt, `exe+0x3B7CA0` (motion), `+0xA58` × dt × `+0xA64`, the carried displacement `+0x690` and the force fields of `exe+0x3F9130` into the move vector, and passes it to `exe+0x3B9090(actor, move, +0xA48 copy, platform carry)`. `exe+0x3B9090` adds every other actor's push (`exe+0x40AD70` per pair of colliding actors from the entity list) to the `+0xA48` term, runs move + pushes through the terrain collision (`exe+0x3BA750`, `exe+0x16FEC0` on `+0x6D0`/`+0x6E0`, result `+0x700`) and writes the position `+0x670` (the per-frame writer seen at `exe+0x3B98A7`). `+0xA48..+0xA67` are zeroed at the end of `exe+0x3B89A0` every frame. So `+0xA48` (x, y, z) is a one-frame displacement treated like a push: our spawn step adds to it. | f32[3] | Two-copy bench 2026-10-10 (session 22, `spawn_step.cpp`, scratchpad s22 `fine.py` frame probe): up to 20 units a frame added to x/z from our per-entity update hook moved Sora 20 units that frame (idle motion kept); the friend's Sora against a railing in 02/06 stayed at z 410 while the x part slid along it; the other game saw the move through our position | 2026-10-10 |

| Save container: second container on our own file (co-op save, `exe+0x79CB10` swap) | Verified live 2026-10-10 (session 25, single-copy benches, DLL with coop_save.cpp): `exe+0x439064` = the game's operator new(size); a container is 0x178 bytes; `exe+0xFEF80(mem, *(i32*)exe+0x79CC2C slot size = 69568, *(i32*)exe+0x79CD34 slot count = 100, name)` opens a file. The path comes from `exe+0x103E40(name, dir out = container+0x40, file out = container+0x144)`: dir = `<Documents>\My Games\<name's folder>\<Steam ID>` (no trailing backslash; format strings `%s\My Games` exe+0x585E48, `\%llu` exe+0x585E64), file = `\<name's file>` (e.g. `\KHIIFM_WW.png`); full path = `%s%s` (exe+0x5859B8). Our hook on 0x103E40 (prologue `48 89 5C 24 20 55 56 57 48 81 EC 60 01 00 00`) wrote another folder while our FEF80 call ran: the container opened our copy of the Steam save (status +0x3C = 0, header +0x00..0x0F = the real one's and = the first 16 bytes of the data at *(+0x10)). With a name, a missing file gives status -1 (no fresh file). Swapping the pointer at `exe+0x79CB10` before the title's LOAD row code runs: the save list showed the swapped container and the picked save loaded from it (TT 02/02). Writer `exe+0x100810` (prologue `48 8B C4 56 57 41 56 48 81 EC B0 05 00 00`) called twice per in-game save, on the container in `exe+0x79CB10` (result 6991872 = bytes written); readers exe+0xFF8B0, 0xFFB00, 0x1002A0, 0x100340 read the pointer each call (Ghidra), exe+0x100720 frees it (only caller 0x14EB00). The save point menu has a 'Title Screen' item (back to the title in the same game). | fn, data | Described in the Meaning column | 2026-10-10 |

| Save data write `exe+0x100340` + save checksum (co-op saves without the Sora copy, TODO 5.19) | `exe+0x100340(i32 fd, void* data, u32 size)` (prologue `40 53 48 83 EC 20`) writes one memory-card file into the container in `exe+0x79CB10`; called twice per in-game save: first a 0x400-byte file, then the KH2 save data. The data passed the second time is a staging copy on the heap, not the live save at `exe+0x9A98B0`: magic `KH2J` (u32 0x4A32484B) at +0, version 0x3A at +4, checksum (u32) at +8, size 0x10FC0. Checksum = the game's CRC: `exe+0x325DC0` sets the state to 0xFFFFFFFF, `exe+0x325D70(buf, len)` adds bytes as `crc = table[(crc >> 24) ^ b] ^ (crc << 8)` and returns ~crc; table = the u32* at `exe+0xBEF898` (256 entries, filled at run time; the standard CRC-32 variants don't match); taken over bytes 0-7, then 0xC to the end; `exe+0x2ECC00` checks it on load. `exe+0x1523A0` initializes the live save, `exe+0x152360` returns its magic | fn, data | Bench 2026-10-10 (session 25, coop_save.cpp hook on 0x100340): our checksum of the game's buffer matched the stored one (one save, no 'checksum check failed' line); that save at HB Merlin's House during co-op logged 'the Sora copy left out of this save: world 0x04 00 00 02 12 -> 00 01 02 12'; after a game restart the slot showed normally in the list (not corrupted), loaded with HB's entry `00 01 02 12` (no repair needed), our patch applied as usual and the menu opened (Sora, Sora, Goofy, Stock). Two-PC test 2026-10-10 (you + friend): co-op saves on both PCs went through it ('the Sora copy left out'), loaded after restarting both games, menu opened | 2026-10-10 |
| Cutscene Sora model files = the field Sora's | `obj/ACTOR_SORA.mdlx` (object 571) holds a model entry (type 0x04) and texture entry (type 0x07) **byte-identical to `P_EX100.mdlx`'s** (field Sora, object 84); `ACTOR_SORA_H.mdlx` (572) likewise to `H_EX500.mdlx` (264). | What some script actions change in the actor | `trap_obj_set_flag(a, n)` / `trap_obj_reset_flag(a, n)` = bit n of `+0x120` (0 and 9 seen). `trap_obj_pattern_enable(a, n)` = bit n of `+0xD10`. `trap_btlobj_set_sheet(a, ...)` sets `+0x5C0` (the stat block pointer). `method_obj_disable_collision(a, 0)` sets `+0x5AC` = 1. `trap_sysobj_motion_start(a, motion, x)` / `trap_sysobj_motion_change` write `+0x170..+0x19C` (motion ids at `+0x180`/`+0x184`, `+0x18C`, clock `+0x19C`). `trap_obj_act_start` writes `+0x4A8`/`+0x4B0` (pointers), `+0x4BC` and `+0x5B8`. `trap_obj_set_movement(a, vec, n)` writes `+0x14`/`+0x1C`. `trap_obj_fly(a, ...)` writes `+0xC` and `+0x2C`. ## Leads (not yet verified)
- **The shared desperation move now starts together, but the friend's copy of it never ends: its Xemnas's HP is frozen (two-PC run 2026-10-22 ~22:03-22:06, DLL `b29d9bb91ec4`, host log kh2coop_18596, friend log kh2coop_14828; the friend's clock ~1 s behind):** host scene 're_b_ex170-60' 22:03:38.606, friend 'our B_EX170_LAST (bound to host#1) runs its own brain' 22:03:37.592 and its '-60' 22:03:37.659 (TODO 1.111 works: 'the host's captured motion ... not played: its source plays a shared move on our own Sora'). Both games then played '-61', '-70', '-71', '-72' within ~1 s of each other, the Sora in motions 254-256 (the grab), 275, 263, 273, 274. Host: during '-72' (Sora motion 274, Xemnas 229) Xemnas's HP went 98 -> 1 at ~22:05:00 (the move's last hit), then his script ran `trap_obj_detach` (22:05:04.5), `trap_obj_hook_stop(our Sora)` (22:05:06.5) and the host's Sora was free (motion 0). Friend: its Xemnas's HP stayed **98** to the end; its Sora stayed in motion 274 from 22:04:48 until the room changed after Xemnas died (22:05:57); no hook_stop, so the shared move never ended. The friend's own last hit (97) went to the host as a claim (host 22:05:00.254 'friend hit our#1 ... for 97 while it holds a player: no damage (at 1 HP)'); in the friend's game it was blocked: world_sync.cpp `WorldSyncBlocksDamage` returns true for every bound enemy on the friend, also one running its own brain, and `ApplyEnemy` (the host's HP) is skipped while it runs its own brain (`OwnBrain`), so its HP can't move at all. Likely: the script waits for that HP to drop before it lets go.
- **The friend's game crashed in a script call 1.3 s after the 1.99 guard ended Data Final Xemnas's thread on the host's copy (two-PC run 2026-10-22, friend log kh2coop_25076, host log kh2coop_18596, DLL `b29d9bb91ec4`; the friend's clock ~1 s behind):** friend 22:00:46.900 'a script thread of B_EX170_LAST_LV99 holds the host's Sora copy in our game: ended without running' (the first time the guard ever ended one; in the 20:48 run, kh2coop_25216, the same kind of thread ran because that Xemnas had its own brain for the shared move). At 22:00:48.218 an access violation at `exe+0x1A8E67`, writing address 0: `exe+0x1A8E60(dst, src)` copies 16 bytes and has no stack frame, and the return address on top of the stack is `kh2coop.dll+0x30468`, just after `call rax` in world_census.cpp `Called` (the fast path `t.real(args)`). So a script call's own code (reached through our pass-through) copied a vector into a null pointer; the source `rdx` = `exe+0x260B9D0`, in the range where the host's log lists the script threads (`exe+0x26019E0`-`0x260AED0`). Then a stack overflow in the crash handling (clr.dll). Xemnas was at ~1,880 HP (no 1-HP rule involved; that rule only runs in the host's game). Which call it was is not known: the friend's game logs no script calls (`KH2COOP_TRAP_LOG` off). Link between the ended thread and the crash: timing only, not proven.
- **The game's "can't die yet" rule for enemies (Ghidra 2026-10-22, for TODO 1.112; matches the host log kh2coop_10244):** a hit reaches the stat change through `exe+0x3D3BA0(actor, hit)`: damage = hit +0x28, stat channel = hit byte +0x25. If `current - damage <= min` (stat block +8), it asks the class check (`exe+0x4AD270(*actor)` vtable +0xA8; base `exe+0x3D2A20`, YS::BTLOBJ table `exe+0x5C6250` +0xA8) and, when that says no, cuts the damage to `current - min - 1` (0 if below 1), so the enemy stays at min + 1. Then `exe+0x3D3790(actor, -damage, channel, flag)` calls class +0xE8, which ends in the stat change `exe+0x3D2EB0`. The base check reads flags = `(actor+0xD10 | actor+0xD14) & ~actor+0xD18`: **bit 9 = can't die, whatever the hit**; bit 10 = can't die from an HP hit unless the attack can finish (attack record `exe+0x4AD270(hit+0x20)` byte +0x12 bit 5, `exe+0x3CEDE0`); bit 11 (or a non-HP channel) = can't die from an attack that can't finish unless the attacker has +0x6C8 bit 7 (`exe+0x3CEE50`); also +0x6C8 bit 7 with +0x18C bit 14 and hit byte +0x26 == 0. Most enemies start with +0xD10 = 0x8404 (bits 2, 10, 15). The script call `trap_obj_pattern_enable(a, n)` sets bit n of +0xD10: both Final Xemnas (`B_EX170_LAST`, `B_EX170_LAST_LV99`) set **bit 9** (+0xD10 `06 94` -> `06 96`, host logs), as do the Data bosses `B_EX110_LV99`, `B_EX120_HB_LV99`, `B_EX130_LV99`, `B_EX140_LV99`, `B_EX150_LV99` and `B_EX380`. Live match: in kh2coop_10244 at 21:43:10.423 the host's own hit on Data Final Xemnas at 7 HP came through as -6 (-> 1) where its others were -13 / -17; 66 ms later the friend's claim (9 damage, through `exe+0x3D2EB0`, which has no such check) took him 1 -> 0 and the fight ended ('re_deadboss-3a') before his desperation move. Not checked: whether some boss classes replace the +0xA8 check (only the base table points at `exe+0x3D2A20`).

- **How a Drive loads its files and builds the form body (Ghidra 2026-10-21, TODO 1.24 research):** the form change `exe+0x40CE60(player, form)` makes a 0x38-byte task (`exe+0x40CAA0`; current one at `exe+0x2AE9FA8`; +0 player, +8 form, +0xC the form's object id from `exe+0x405190(form)` = u16 at `exe+0x3E2E40()` + 0x16 + 2 x form) whose body `exe+0x40D000` (entry thunk `exe+0x40CF10`): drops the files of the previous form (`exe+0x39CCA0(5000)`: resource entries of group 5000), asks for the new form's files (`exe+0x3E0630(form)` -> `exe+0x3E0660(objId, 5000, 4)`: the object's model, motions, weapons (`exe+0x3E0CC0`) and linked objects into the resource list), waits for them (`exe+0x39D460(task)`), waits for the transform motion, then builds the body: 0xE18 bytes, object entry `exe+0x3DFEB0(objId)`, player-form constructor `exe+0x405030(mem, entry, form, ...)` (calls `exe+0x3A7A40`, sets the form player `exe+0x2AE9A28`), moves state over (`exe+0x3BD2E0`, `exe+0x3AB070`, ...), and ends the old body with its class slot +0x38. So only one form's files are kept (group 5000), which is why a form body can't be made in a game not in that form.
- **The Drive's glow stays on the copy (confirmed and fixed 22:38: table row 'The Drive's shine on the copy'; the rest here is still a lead) (Ghidra 2026-10-21 + bench screenshots `formlook_1_*_B`, `formlook_3_*_B` 22:2x, TODO 1.24):** in the game not in that form, the copy in a form is a white glowing shape with sparkles at 1.2 s, 3.8 s and later (the attack shot); the real form body in its own game has no glow. Lead: the effect list `exe+0xB63608` (groups: +0x18 first effect, +0x20 last, +0x28 next group handle; effects: +0x40 instance, +0x48 definition (flags +0x10), **+0x50 owner actor**, +0x60 sound id, +0x68 extra, +0x70 flags, +0x74 next handle). At the body swap the Drive task (`exe+0x40D000`) hands the old body's things to the new one: `exe+0x2C4480(old, new)` moves every effect whose owner is the old body **unless its definition has flag 0x200**; those stay with the old body, which is then ended (class slot +0x38). The copy never swaps bodies, so effects of that kind, started while it plays the peer's Drive motion, stay. Effect instance: +0xC flags, bit 20 = finished (`exe+0x2BE7A0`); `exe+0x2BE8A0(instance)` stops it (sets 0x10001, ends its sound +0x30); the cleanup pass `exe+0x2C4660` unlinks and frees finished ones. The other hand-overs: `exe+0x3D06E0` (list `exe+0x2A24B40`, +0x10 actor handle, +0xC4 next), `exe+0x3E49B0` (actors with +0x5D8 = 2: +0x600), `exe+0x53DAE0` (actors whose +0x6A0 handle is the old body: `exe+0x3DA210`).
- **Effect stops, hard and gentle; the Keyblade's motions (Ghidra 2026-10-21, TODO 1.24):** `exe+0x2BE8A0(instance)` is the hard stop (instance +0xC |= 0x10001, deletes the particle system at +0x30 with `exe+0x2C1F50`, ends its sound): the copy's Drive shine vanished at once with it (your look, bench 22:38). `exe+0x2BE8E0(instance)` = `exe+0x2C2830(*(instance+0x30))` only sets byte +0x91 = 1 on the particle system (no null check): the gentle stop, used by the Keyblade update for its trail (`weapon+0xC00`). Keyblade update `exe+0x3EFA60(weapon)`: owner actor at `weapon+0xBF8`, motion controller at `weapon+0x158` (same layout as a body), shows / puts away through the class handler +0x88 / +0x80. A form object's weapons: `exe+0x3E0CC0(entry, 0/1)`: entry +0x4E = weapon object (u16), +0x4C = kind (1 or 0xE with index 1: from the table `exe+0x9ABDA0` by the form byte +0x57), then `exe+0x3EF8B0(weapon object)`. The forms' weapon motion files (Valor: `W_EX010_BTLF_R.mset` alone in group +1; `W_EX010_E0.mdlx`, `.a.us`, `W_EX010_BTLF_L.mset` in group +2, bench logs 22:23) are moved by a Drive's layout like the body's.
- **How a body's Keyblades are built and ended (Ghidra 2026-10-21, TODO 1.24 second Keyblade):** the player constructor `exe+0x3A7A40(actor, entry, form, ...)` builds weapon 0 with `exe+0x3D7C70(actor, exe+0x3E0CC0(entry, 0), 0)` and, only for form 1-6 or 10, weapon 1 with `exe+0x3E0CC0(entry, 1)` and slot 1 (the party constructor `exe+0x3C2DB0` builds both always). `exe+0x3D7C70(owner, weaponObj, slot)`: group = owner class handler +0x70 (`*decode(owner+0)` vtable) + slot + 1 (slot 0 of a player with `+0x6C8` bit 7 and `exe+0x3AA4F0()`: 0x8FD); allocates 0xC10 bytes (`exe+0x152430`), `exe+0x3EF750(mem, exe+0x3DFEB0(weaponObj), owner, u16 owner-entry+0x4E (entry from the owner's handle at owner+8), slot, group)`, stores it at `owner+0xD60 + 8 x slot`, then `exe+0x3C8440(weapon+0x158, owner+0x188)` and `exe+0x3C86A0(weapon+0x158, owner+0x180, 0, owner+0x19C)`. `exe+0x3EF750`: base init `exe+0x3DA940`, `weapon+0xC08` slot, `+0xBF8` owner, `+0xC0C` group, class handler at `exe+0x74E4A8` (vtable `exe+0x5C9408`; +0x38 = `exe+0x3EF4D0` ends it: hard-stops its trail `+0xC00`, then `exe+0x3B4700` (actor end: motion `exe+0x3C7300`, flag `+0x120` bit 28)), `exe+0x3E6370(weapon+0x910, group)` (the weapon's set takes its files from that group: the form's weapon 1 = group 5002), and, when the name table `*(exe+0x2AE5A40)` has a name for (kind + 2 x ... slot) (0x20-byte rows), finds that motion file among the loaded ones (`exe+0x39CC40(exe+0x2AE5A48, -1)`), puts it on its controller (`exe+0x3C8460`) and **links its controller to the owner's** (`exe+0x3C8E90(owner+0x158, weapon+0x158)`): the owner's motion changes restart the Keyblade. **Live (bench 22:52, Valor + Wisdom):** the copy's Keyblade on Valor's `W_EX010_BTLF_R.mset`, the friend's Drive moved that file, and putting the copy's body motion on again (before the Keyblade's file) ended the friend's game: fixed by setting the Keyblade's file first and moving every controller pointer into a moved file (+0 .. +0x58) with it.
- **File move fields; the motion controller link list; ending a weapon (Ghidra + benches 2026-10-21 23:00-23:15, TODO 1.24 second Keyblade):** `exe+0x39E690(entry)` runs with **+0x58 = the new place, +0x60 = the old one** (data still at the old place until it copies it; `+0x44` bit 0 = done this layout, bit 1 = to free); equal = no move. It fixes some pointers by the file's name (`exe+0x12DF90(entry+4)`: object +0x10 and +0x658 = new place, the list at +0x670 (next +8): each `+0x70` object's +0x80 shifted), then `exe+0x3A6120(new place)`. Motion controller links (`exe+0x3C8E90(owner ctl, child ctl)`): owner ctl +0xD8 = first child, +0xE0 = last, child +0xE8 = handle of the next (`exe+0x4AD240` / `exe+0x4AD270`); a weapon's end (class handler +0x38, `exe+0x3EF4D0` -> `exe+0x3B4700`) **doesn't unlink it at once**; its teardown when the actor is deleted (`exe+0x3EF520`, from the actor list `exe+0x3BFD87`) does, through `exe+0x3C8F20(owner+0x158, weapon+0x158)` (the game's unlink; it assumes the child is listed and reads through null otherwise: bench 23:20:57, `exe+0x3C8F7E`, after our early unlink), but only while `weapon+0x158` (its motion file) is non-zero and `exe+0x3DA520(weapon)`; it then **clears the owner's slot** `owner+0xD60 + 8 x weapon+0xC08` and frees (`exe+0x3B45C0`). Before that: the copy's next motion change walked into our ended second Keyblade's controller (`exe+0x3C8A0D` -> ... -> `exe+0x3A6425`, bench 23:13:52). **Correction (debugger dump 23:19):** the effect that read the moved file *is* owned by the old second Keyblade (+0x50) and its definition (+0x48) and particle system (instance +0x30: +0x70, +0x98, +0xA0) point into the old place of `W_EX010_E0.a.us`; it was already fading (instance +0xC has 0x10001), which our stop skipped. Earlier text: after our second Keyblade's files moved, an effect still read the old place of `W_EX010_E0.a.us` (`exe+0x2C42F6` -> `exe+0x2C5B48` -> `exe+0x2BE6EE` -> `exe+0x4B0242`, r8 = the old Keyblade; benches 23:03, 23:09, 23:15). Building it: the files of a form's weapons aren't all in when `exe+0x3E0160` says loaded (Master 23:07: a garbage model in the weapon's base init, `exe+0x3B399D`); waiting for all of groups +0..+2 fixed it (Master's second Keyblade `W_EX010_P0.mdlx` + `W_EX010_TRIF_L.mset` built, 23:13).
- **The file request's group (Ghidra decompile of `exe+0x3E0660(objId, group, p3, p4)`, 2026-10-21, TODO 1.24 step 1):** group -1 = the object's default, `u32 exe+0x5C8500[entry type byte +4]` (type 0 = 0x8FC = 2300, 1 = 0x960, 2 = 0x9C4, 3 = 0xA28, 4 = 0xA8C, ...), or -200 for a cutscene id (bit 30) whose type isn't 7, 22 or 24. Only group 0x960 (type 1, party members), and only for ids other than 0x319/0x31A/0x3EE and without bit 29, goes through the two player slots `exe+0x2A24FA8`/`+0x2A24FAC`: the slot already holding the entry's first int, else the first empty one, gives group 0x96A or 0x974 (and is written). Then `exe+0x39E000(exe+0x2A24FB0 name buffer, group, 0)` adds or regroups the model file, `exe+0x39DF10(exe+0x2A24FD8, group, p3, 0)` queues it, the motion set name (`exe+0x3E0BD0`) is added the same way, the two weapons (`exe+0x3E0CC0(entry, 0/1)`) are requested recursively with group+1, and the 4 linked objects (u16 at entry+0x58) with that group too. The form objects (0x55-0x58, 0x95D) are type 0 (player; bench log 2026-10-21 21:08), so -1 would put them in 2300 (the player area; not used, see the file heap entry below).
- **The file heap and how a request gets loaded (Ghidra 2026-10-21 + bench `formlook --one` 21:14 / 21:21, TODO 1.24 step 1):** asking for files (`exe+0x3E0660`) only queues them: state stays 1 (bench 21:08: nothing loaded in 6 s) until the requester calls **`exe+0x39D460(task or 0)`**, which with a task first waits for pending reads (`exe+0x39E340`, yields the task each frame), then lays the heap out (`exe+0x39D590`) and starts the reads (`exe+0x39E690` -> async read `exe+0x3A0BD0`, callback `exe+0x39DEE0`; state byte +1: 0/1 queued, 3 loaded, `exe+0x39DBB0`) and frees entries flagged +0x44 bit 1. With 0 it doesn't wait: **called from the copy's spawn step, the form's model and motions were in 63-94 ms** (bench 21:21, 5 forms). The heap is a fixed 30 MB static block `exe+0xBF33C0`-`exe+0x29F33C0` (end pointer at `exe+0x749740`); groups >= 0 fill from the bottom by area, negative groups from the top (bottom of those at `exe+0x29F33F0`). Areas (`exe+0x39D590`): 0x3F2 (0xE0000), 2300-2399 player (size set per room by `exe+0x39E270` from `exe+0x3E2EB0`, with the two party slot areas 0x96A-0x973 / 0x974-0x97D), **5000-5099 Drive (fixed 0x300000, `exe+0x39DC50`)**, 5100-5199 (0xC0000), the rest general; an area that outgrows its size pushes the top up, and a layout moves loaded files whose place changed (memmove + pointer fix-ups, `exe+0x39E690`). Entry fields: +1 state, +2 u16 users, +4 name, +0x44 flags, +0x48 group, +0x50 size, +0x58 place, +0x60 old place, +0x68 first group, +0x70 next (handle, `exe+0x4AD270`). **A drop is group + 10000** (`exe+0x39CCA0(g)` does it to every group >= g; groups 10000+ are freed by the next layout); `exe+0x39E000` on a name already listed keeps its group (unless 10000+: revived with the new group). **Measured (save 59, HB 04/06, friend not in a form):** one form's files are 2.3-3.1 MB (Valor 2.28, Wisdom 2.34, Limit 3.12, Master 2.47, Final 2.69); with one of them loaded 7.3-8.1 MB of the heap was free. Not freeing them crashed (bench 21:14: all five forms kept, 11.7 MB, crash at `exe+0x19C366` while laying out Final's); letting each go after use (group + 10000, then `exe+0x39D460(0)`) passed. Note: `exe+0x3E0160` says 'loaded' when the model and motions are in; the weapons' files can still be on their way.
  **Corrections and additions (benches 2026-10-21 21:32-21:55):** a drop doesn't free at once: `exe+0x39CCA0(g)` makes groups under 10000 +10000 and ages groups 10000-29999 by +50 a call; those stay as a cache (a request for the same name revives them, `exe+0x39E000`) until the layout needs their room. So 'let go' = put in that cache. The layout must only run with no read in flight: the game's wait `exe+0x39E340` loops while `exe+0x2A0D628` != 0 (`exe+0x3A0A30`), `exe+0x1DC340()` != 0, or a listed file of a group under 10000 has state 1 or 2; form_look checks the same before asking and before letting go. Measured: our layout calls moved no loaded file of a group under 10000 (only cached 15100 ones). **Form objects** (`exe+0x405190(1..10)`, read in game): 0x55 0x56 0x95D 0x57 0x58 0x59 (Anti), 0x318, 0x108, 0xC8, 0x5F9. **Both players in different forms (host Valor, friend Wisdom):** the friend's game ends with exit code **0xC0000409** (fast-fail, past our crash logger; no Windows error event) ~0.2 s after its copy took Valor's look, when its own Wisdom Drive drops the Drive area and builds its body; with the files loaded but the look not put on (`KH2COOP_FORM_LOOK_FILES_ONLY=1`) both games lived through two rounds. Not the stack cookie (a hook on `__report_gsfailure` `exe+0x43A0D4` never fired); `_guard_dispatch_icall` `exe+0x5669F0` is a plain `jmp rax` (no CFG). **Cause (own debugger attached to the friend's game, bench 2026-10-21 22:10):** the resource list is **kept sorted by group, ascending** (insert `exe+0x39DD70`; negative groups first), and the layout `exe+0x39D590` places the files of an area in list order. The friend's own Wisdom Drive asked for its files in group 5000, so they went in front of the copy's 5050 Valor files, and the Drive's layout moved those (e.g. `P_EX100_BTLF.mdlx` up by 0x23A800). The move (`exe+0x39E690(entry)`: memmove, `exe+0x3A6120` rebuild and the game's own fix-ups) doesn't reach the copy: its motion controller (`copy+0x158`; +0 = the motion set's place, +0x18 = the playing motion object, inside the motion set) kept the old addresses, and the copy's next motion step `exe+0x3C8520` made a virtual call through +0x18 into the moved-away memory: `_purecall` (VCRUNTIME140+0x5BFA) -> abort -> fast-fail code 7, exit 0xC0000409, which no VEH, WER dump or TerminateProcess hook sees. The game has its own crash handler too (`exe+0x1435D0`, writes `KINGDOM HEARTS II FINAL MIX.dmp` in the game folder), not reached on this path. **The fix (form_look.cpp, bench `formlook --auto --mixed --pick 2` 2026-10-21 22:23 PASS, 4 rounds, the look put on again once in each game):** note the list entries the copy's look points into (`copy+0x920` / `+0x928` / `+0x930` = an entry's +0x58), hook each file move (`exe+0x39E690`, first bytes `40 53 48 83 EC 20 F6 41 44 01`) to see when one of them moves, and put the look on again at the game's load wait (`exe+0x39E340(task)`, first bytes `40 53 48 83 EC 20 48 8B D9`), which `exe+0x39D460(task)` calls after all its moves; putting it on again from inside the move loop threw (bench 22:16). **The set must first get the new places:** the set at `actor+0x910` holds the files by place (+0x10 / +0x18 / +0x20 = `actor+0x920` model file, `+0x928`, `+0x930` motions) and its refill `exe+0x3E63D0` lets each go with `exe+0x39DA10(place)`, which looks for the list entry whose +0x58 is that place and subtracts 1 from its u16 users (+2), **writing through null when none matches** (exception at `exe+0x39DA55`, bench 22:20, after a move); with the three places written to the new ones first, the refill worked (22:23).
- **Resource list (Ghidra 2026-10-21):** head `exe+0x29F33C8`, entries linked by a handle at +0x70; +0x48 group / priority (5000 = the Drive Form's files; `exe+0x39CCA0(g)` re-ranks or frees a group), +0x68 object id, +0x44 bit 1 = to free.

- **How a Game Over ends (Ghidra decompile 2026-10-18, for TODO 1.3 / Q6; nothing run yet):** the ctor `exe+0x3FC7A0(obj, ?, kind)` rolls Mickey, sets `exe+0x2A11400` |= 2, pauses the world (`exe+0x155800`: saves `exe+0xABAD18` into +0xABAD1A, clears it), sets bit 2 of `exe+0xABB878` and starts a task (`exe+0x1506B0(1, 15000, exe+0x3FCA90)`; +0x68 = cleanup `exe+0x3FCD60`). The task runs the update `exe+0x3FCEC0` every frame and ends when it returns 0; the cleanup then clears the 0x2A11400 bit 2 and the 0xABB878 bit 2, frees the object and sets `exe+0x2AE8050` = 0.
  - **No Mickey = one way:** the ctor calls `exe+0x3A3460`, which ends the room's mission (`exe+0x2A0FF68` = 0); update state 2 calls `exe+0x3AC2F0` → `exe+0x3BF8F0(2000)` (clears many systems and removes the field's actors below priority 2000) and `exe+0x3DE010` (a party 'leave' task, then RestoreParty); state 4 loads `obj/GAMEOVER.mset` (`exe+0x3FD260`); state 5 opens the Continue / Load menu (`exe+0x2E4C20`, menu `exe+0x3AD480(1)` at obj+0x18, callback obj+0x78 = `exe+0x3FCBE0`). Nothing there returns to the field: only a reload.
  - **Mickey branch keeps the field and the mission:** state 2 calls `exe+0x3DC6D0` only, state 4 `exe+0x400A30(0)` (Donald and Goofy leave), `exe+0x415880`, state 5 opens the white choice menu (`exe+0x3AD480(10)`, callback obj+0x48 = `exe+0x3FCC50`). Choice 9 = 'I won't give up': state 7 = `exe+0x155790` (resumes the world: restores 0xABAD18), `exe+0x4158C0` (spawns Mickey), return 0 → cleanup → gameplay. Choice 8 = 'It's all over': loads GAMEOVER.mset and switches to the Continue / Load menu (`exe+0x2E4910`) without clearing the field, state 8 (the task ends).
  - **Kind 2 ('reraise', likely Auto-Life):** state 2 sets up the reraise effect, clears 0x2A11400 bit 2, `exe+0x155790`, return 0: back to gameplay with no menu.

Motion triggers (`exe+0x40D810(trigger, motion state)`, actor = state+0x10; trigger bytes: +4 type, +5 param count, +6 first param): type 0x14 offers the command into the menu of the player pointer `exe+0x2A105D0` (+0xDD0, slot 1, via `exe+0x3B2850` or, when the actor is that player, `exe+0x3B25C0`, the same without the +0x6C8 bit 25 check), aimed at the actor or, for a player in a grab (+0x6C8 bit 7, +0x18C bit 14), at its grabber +0x2B0, and sets player+0x18C bit 17 and +0x638; type 0x19 / 0x1A set state+0x34 bit 20 / 21 and actor+0x638 = the param. A menu slot is menu + (slot*9 + 0x26C)*4, command at +2 (`exe+0x402130`). In this PC's `B_EX140.mset` (Xigbar; also the grab motions his script plays on the held Sora) range triggers type 0x1A: entry 252 (AP40) frames 34-end chain 491, entry 254 (AP42) frames 50-70 chain 492, entry 255 (AP43) frames 42-62 chain 491: so 252 (after 491) opens the first 598 and 255 (after 598) the second, matching the host's own 491 / 598 / 598 (2026-10-16). A reaction command's target info (`exe+0x3BDA50` / `exe+0x3BEE60(info, actor, part)`) is {actor handle, part, handle, -1}; reaction_sync.cpp's host run used part 0 for every friend's press, while the friend's game names the part that offered it; `exe+0x432800` (the script call that faults for 492) turns its handle into an object and reads that object's handle at +0x114.
- **Attacks can carry a reaction command: the source of Data Xigbar's original 598 and the 492 fault (Ghidra, session 38, 2026-10-17; for TODO 1.83 / 1.85):** after the per-enemy loop, `exe+0x4081C0` calls `exe+0x407B40(state)`, which walks a list of attacks (hit shapes): head `exe+0x2A24B40`, next = the handle at +0xC4 (`exe+0x3CFE90`), only those with +8 bit 8 set. An attack with a command at +0x11C (non-zero) is offered when the command's table entry passes (flag bit 6 = a chain: player+0x18C bit 21 set and player+0x638 = the entry's +0x22) and the player is in reach (`exe+0x3C94C0`: a shape from the radius +0x120 and height +0x124 around the attack's place +0xE0 plus the offset +0x128). Its target info is made by `exe+0x3BEE00(info, attack, owner)`: actor = the owner (handle at +0x118), part 0, second handle = the attack itself. That matches the host's own 598s (actor Xigbar, part 0, a non-actor second handle). The script calls: `trap_attack_set_rc` (2/51, `exe+0x432790` -> `exe+0x437B70(attack, receiver, command, radius, height)`) writes the owner (the attack's +0x10 owner) to +0x118, the receiver to +0x114, the command to +0x11C, radius +0x120, height +0x124 (height = radius when 0 or less); `trap_attack_rc_receiver` (2/52, `exe+0x432800`, the call that faults for 492) returns the receiver of the attack named by its handle; `trap_attack_set_rc_owner` (7/34, `exe+0x53FAC0`) sets +0x118; `trap_attack_set_reaction_offset` (7/14, `exe+0x53F300`) sets the offset +0x128. An older census log of another boss shows the use: `trap_attack_set_rc(attack, B_EX170_LAST, 635, 800, 0)` by B_EX170_LAST on several attacks, the boss itself as the receiver. The command event `exe+0x3B5FA0(target, user, command, info)` hands the boss's script a handle to the carried-out command's target info (player+0xDD0, +0x10; command at +8), so the script can read the attack from the second handle and ask for its receiver. So, not yet checked live: the friend's game has no such attacks for Xigbar (his script, which calls 2/51, doesn't run there, and world_mirror.cpp doesn't send 2/51), so it can't offer the second 598 in motion 255 (its first 598 came from Xigbar's part 6 instead); and the host's run of a friend's press with no second handle makes Xigbar's script call 2/52 on nothing (the 492 fault at `exe+0x432810`). The checks in `exe+0x407B40`, in order (used by reaction_sync.cpp's OfferAttackCommands, built 2026-10-17, not run yet): the command's table entry `exe+0x3B68B0(cmd)` (a 0x30-byte row: +0xC u32 flags, +0x1E priority byte, +0x22 u16 chain command, +0x28 u16 distance); while the u32 at `exe+0x2A11404` is non-zero (`exe+0x3ABB20`) only entries with flag 0x10; flag 0x40 = a chain (above); `exe+0x3B6A10(entry, player)` (usable: reads player +0xDE0, +0x6CB, +0xE14 and its state `exe+0x3B5C10`, against entry flags 0x400 / 0x800 / 0x1000 / 0x2000 / 0x8000); then reach, `exe+0x3C94C0(attack shape, player shape)`. A shape is 0x28 bytes: +0 u32 kind, +4 radius, +8 height, +0x10 body pointer, +0x18 place (4 floats). The attack's: `exe+0x3C9B60(shape, 0, radius, height)` and the place = the attack's 4 floats from +0xE4 plus its offset +0x128 (`exe+0x13FCE0` adds 4 floats, `exe+0x13FD20` subtracts, `exe+0x1A8E60` copies). The player's: `exe+0x3C9C00(shape, player, body)` with body = `exe+0x3B5650(player, 1, -1)` (the first body shape of kind 1 from the list at +0xAB8; `exe+0x4081C0` offers nothing from these lists without one). The offer then goes through `exe+0x408080` (priority, `exe+0x3B6A10`, skips a dead target) and `exe+0x408BD0` (the entry's +0x28 distance only marks it, bit 30 of the slot state's +0x28).

The stack walk also listed `kh2coop.dll+0x17B4B`, which is right after a stack-cookie check: probably a stale slot, not a caller. Not reproduced.
- **Which Limits the command menu lists (Ghidra, session 37, 2026-10-16; for TODO 1.78, Trinity without Donald and Goofy):** the field command menu build `exe+0x3F29E0` fills the Limit list with `exe+0x3E7B30`: for each `limt` entry (table `*(exe+0x2AE5690)`, count at +4, 0x40-byte entries from +8; entry +0 a group byte (one Limit per group), +1..+3 the characters it needs, +0x28 the command id, +0x2A an ability, +0x2C a world byte) it lists the command when `exe+0x3E81A0(entry)` says so, greyed (state 5) unless `exe+0x3E75D0()` and `exe+0x3E7E10(entry)`. `exe+0x3E81A0` (and the same check in `exe+0x3E7800(command)`, which the menu state `exe+0x3D88E0` and the Limit start `exe+0x3D8B40` use): every nonzero character of +1..+3 must be found by `exe+0x3D5C70(char)` (walks the player, GetFriend(0), GetFriend(1), matching `exe+0x3B5B20`; for character 1 it falls back to `*(exe+0x2AE9A28)`) and be live (`exe+0x3BA720`); the ability +0x2A (type 0x13) must be on the first character's ability list. Our GetFriend(1) hook gives the Sora copy to `exe+0x3D5C98` (inside `exe+0x3D5C70`) when party slot 1 is empty. Command type 0x51 = a Limit (`exe+0x401D60` -> `exe+0x3D88E0`). The drive-form gate `exe+0x4005E0` (command type 4, argument 1..6) counts party slots by character: 1 needs character 3, 2 needs character 2, 4-6 every slot (fits Valor = Goofy leaves, Wisdom = Donald leaves, Master / Final = both), so likely 1 = Sora, 2 = Donald, 3 = Goofy. Not checked live: which entry 0x26C is and what character the copy answers (probe 'limit gate:' in puppet.cpp, waiting for a bench).
- **How the game decides a hit lands (Ghidra, session 37, 2026-10-16; for the friend hit during its Limit, TODO 1.75):** the hit pass `exe+0x3CFF40` walks the victims (`exe+0x3D2E50`), skips invalid ones (`exe+0x3BA900`), and for each live attack asks `exe+0x3D2060(attack, victim)`: not while `exe+0x3FCAC0` says so, not if the victim's bit set at `victim+0xC98` (+4, bit = attack +0x20, `exe+0x3CF7D0`) already has this attack, not the attack's own owners (attack +0x10, +0x14), and only if attack +0x39 has the bit of the victim's team (`victim+0x4DC`); attack kinds 5-6 skip the rest. Then the victim's body shapes `*(victim+0x640)` (count, 40-byte shapes from +0x18) are tested against the attack (`exe+0x40B050` -> `exe+0x3C94C0`) and a touch runs ResolveHit `exe+0x3D1730`. No 'invincible' flag seen on this path yet: a Limit's protection is likely in the body shapes or in `exe+0x3C94C0`. **Next:** read the friend's own Sora's body shapes during its Limit on the bench.
- **How a landed hit becomes damage, and the player's protection and survive rules (Ghidra, session 37, 2026-10-16; for TODO 1.75-1.77):** the shared OnHit `exe+0x3D60C0(victim, hit)` first asks the victim's class (handle at victim+0, `exe+0x4AD270` turns a handle into a pointer) whether it is protected: skipped when victim+0x9B8 bit 2 is set; the player class (`exe+0x74A518`, vtable `exe+0x5C4A80`) answers at vtable +0xB8 = `exe+0x3A7360`, true when an object from `exe+0x3F6DF0(0x10002, 0xC)` exists or `exe+0x3D65A0(victim)`: `exe+0x3D3570(victim)` (victim+0x120 bit 8, +0x18C bit 22, `exe+0x40A900(victim+0x64C)`, +0x120 & 0x10080000, +0x9B8 & 0x210, `exe+0x152940()`, `exe+0x3A57F0(exe+0x2A10500, 0)`), victim+0x126 bit 0, float victim+0xD70 > 0, `exe+0x2A11400` bit 1, `exe+0x3AC260()` (`*(exe+0x2A11478)+0x48` -> byte +2 bit 0). Protected (or +0x9B8 bit 2) = the hit is dropped unless the attack kind (attack object from handle hit+0x20, byte +4) is 5-6. Then `exe+0x3EE9B0(status, hit, victim)` remaps the reaction hit+0x26 by the status word (victim+0xD10 | +0xD14) & ~+0xD18 through the table at `*(exe+0x2AE59D0)` (count +4, 0x20-byte rows from +8: status bit, then a reaction per hit kind hit+0x27). Then `exe+0x3D3BA0(victim, hit)` applies it once (hit+0x18 bit 1): damage = hit+0x28 on stat hit+0x25 (stats victim+0x5C0, 12 bytes per stat: current, max, minimum at +8). A lethal hit (current - damage <= minimum) asks the class's vtable +0xA8 (player: `exe+0x3A7320` -> `exe+0x3D6670(victim, hit)`); true = damage becomes current - minimum - 1 (at least 0), the victim keeps 1 HP. `exe+0x3D6670` is true when stats+0x1DC has its top bit, the minimum is 0 and current > 1 (likely **Second Chance**); or stats+0x1E0 bit 0 and `exe+0x3D4880(victim)` (victim+0x158 set and victim+0x180 in 10..39, likely already in a hit reaction: **Once More**); or `exe+0x3D35F0(victim, hit)`: status bit 9; or bit 10 with (bit 19 or stat 0) and the attack's +0x12 bit 5 clear (`exe+0x3CEDE0`); or (bit 11 or stat != 0) with that bit clear and the attacker's (handle hit+0x10) +0x6C8 bit 7 clear; or victim+0x6C8 bit 7 with +0x18C bit 14 and reaction 0. After that, damage > 1 becomes 1 when status bit 15 is set, the hit kind is 0/1/3/4/6/7/0xF/0x10 (`exe+0x3CEE00`) and the attacker's +0x6C8 bit 7 is clear (`exe+0x3CEE50`). The change goes through `exe+0x3D3790` -> class vtable +0xE8 (player `exe+0x3A75F0` -> `exe+0x3D5E50`: Drive gain from damage taken, stats+0x22C x damage / max HP, via `exe+0x3D3CF0`) -> `exe+0x3D2EB0` (our ApplyStatDelta hook; its 4th argument is 1 only for kinds 5-6, not the reaction). The reaction plays after in `exe+0x3D60C0` by `exe+0x3CE980(hit+0x26)` (3 -> `exe+0x1688F0`, 4 -> `exe+0x1688B0`, other -> `exe+0x168930`). **What it means for co-op:** a forwarded hit on the friend calls `exe+0x3D2EB0` directly, so none of these rules use the friend's own Sora (no protection during its Limit, no Second Chance / Once More); and in the host's game the copy shares the host's stats, so the host's game clamps the copy's damage with the host's HP and abilities. Not yet seen live: which bit a Limit, Reflect and Guard set. **Confirmed live 2026-10-16 (table rows):** the hit apply and its raw damage, the Second Chance / Once More bits, +0x126 bit 0 during a Limit; Reflect sets none of these; Guard not tried.
- **The player's hit reaction (Ghidra, session 37, 2026-10-16; for the friend's missing flinches, TODO 1.79):** after the damage, `exe+0x3D60C0` calls `exe+0x1688F0` / `exe+0x1688B0` / `exe+0x168930` (reaction class 3 / 4 / other), which only set a flag (4 / 8 / 2) for the actor in the 3-entry table `exe+0xABCFE8` (flags at `exe+0xABD000`; likely the party HUD portrait), then the class's vtable +0xC8 (player `exe+0x3A73D0`: unless +0x9B8 bit 2, `exe+0x3D2FE0(actor, hit)`), then `exe+0x3F47B0(actor, hit)` (sends an event to the 8 listeners at `exe+0x2AE5D88`). `exe+0x3D2FE0` is the body's reaction: skipped when hit+0x18 bit 0 is set or `exe+0x40A8E0(actor+0x64C)` is false; by `exe+0x3CE980(hit+0x26)`: 1 -> `exe+0x3D4960`; 2 -> `exe+0x3D43B0`; 3 (flinch) -> `exe+0x3D43B0` + `exe+0x3D4960` (or `exe+0x3D3840` / `exe+0x3D4800`); 4 (knockback) -> motion 0x12 or 0x10 on actor+0x158 by the sign of the hit's direction against the actor's facing, a push `exe+0x3B6420`; 0xE (big knockback) -> motion 0x18 / 0x17; 5 -> `exe+0x40FBF0` with hit+0x1C. The hit's first 16 bytes are its direction vector; hit kinds 0xF-0x12 rebuild it from the camera. `exe+0x3CEAB0(hit)` and `exe+0x3CEA00(hit, ...)` (not read yet) give further values. Calling it for a forwarded hit needs a hit record with these fields.
- **Who the yellow target (auto target) measures from (Ghidra, session 37, 2026-10-16; for your question whether the friend's yellow target takes the host's enemy):** the lock-on record is the field command menu +0xBE8 (table row 'Lock-on state'); its player is **menu +0xC20** (`exe+0x3DD280` reads `*(lock+0x38)`), not +0xC38. The auto target is SetTarget `exe+0x3BE2B0` mode 6 (mode 4 = R1), run from `exe+0x3DCD70`: `exe+0x3BE4B0(slot, player, range)` walks the enemy list (`exe+0x3BF4F0`), skips invalid ones (`exe+0x3BA260`, `exe+0x3BA900`), and per target point scores distance from the player plus a height window and the angle off the player's facing (`exe+0x3B5B40`, `exe+0x3B56E0`, tuning at `*(player+0x150)` +0x5C..0x70); the lowest score wins. **The player is set by every player-class actor that is built:** `exe+0x3AA850(actor)` (from the constructor `exe+0x3A7A40` at `0x3A7BBE`, also `0x3A819F`, `0x3AAB75`) sets `actor+0xDC0 = exe+0x2A10620` and calls `exe+0x3FD830(menu, actor)`, which sets menu +0xC20 (`exe+0x3DD5B0`), +0xC38 and +0xAE0 to that actor. So the Sora built last is the one the yellow target measures from; the extra copy is built before our Sora at room loads (solo_copy.cpp, every 'extra copy' line of the 2026-10-16 friend log says so), so a copy built later in a room would take it. `exe+0x3FD610` (from the player removals `0x3A7151`, `0x3A8F61`, also `0x3A8116`, `0x3A9FBC`, `0x3AAB1C`) clears +0xC38 and the lock's player; `exe+0x3FD760` resets the menu. aggro.cpp's SetTarget hook only changes modes 0-2, never 6. Parked unmatched enemies (world_sync.cpp, 5000 below their spawn) should fall outside the height window. **Not checked live yet:** read menu +0xC20 against our Sora in both games during a fight.
- **The effect system's mode and a bound effect started in mode 3 (Ghidra 2026-10-16, session 36; from the friend's crash in the two-PC run 2026-10-15 23:31:10):** `exe+0xB63628` is the mode (1 when the current scene is the field one, `exe+0x150820`; 2 for the other, `exe+0x151B00`; set by `exe+0x2C4330` / `exe+0x2C4540`). `exe+0x2C4440(mode)` saves it to `exe+0xB6362C` and switches; `exe+0x2C4420` puts it back. Switched to 3 by `exe+0x29B590`, `exe+0x29B600` (which puts it back at its end) and `exe+0x1512B0` (callers not found by direct calls; what scene that is: not known). 1/87 `trap_obj_effect_start_bind` = `exe+0x42D540` -> `exe+0x3B6670(actor, id, a, b, bind actor or 0)` -> `exe+0x2C6B30(actor+0x80, id, 1, a, b, owner)`. `exe+0x2C6B30` makes the effect, allocates a 0x78 record (+0x40 the effect, +0x48 its table entry, +0x50 the owner, +0x74 the record's handle), links it into the actor's list, and only then, for a bound effect (entry +0x10 bit 0), runs `exe+0x2C5F70(record)`. That one branches on the mode: 1 treats the owner as a battle actor; 3 calls `exe+0x2FF110(owner, out, bone)`, which reads owner+0x30 as a model for `exe+0x1A7AF0(model, bone, out)` (bone matrices at model+0x50, 0x40 each). Run on a field actor in mode 3, owner+0x30 holds floats: the friend's run faulted at `exe+0x1A7B05` with rcx = 0x3F8000003F800000 (return address `exe+0x2C5FB8`, right after the mode-3 call), world mirror caught it ('call 1/87 on F_MU060 failed'), the record stayed half made, and 30 s later the room unload crashed at `exe+0x4B01D5` with rcx = 0xEFACCAFEEFACCAFE (stack `exe+0x2BE8BE` <- `0x2C652D` <- `0x3DAC0F` <- `0x3B4EAA`); just before, world sync logged that `F_MU060` as 'removed but still here ... fx 0xEFACCAFE'. The friend had entered that room 3 s before (23:30:38, 'frozen 0x3').

- **Motion playing, more from Ghidra (session 35, 2026-10-14, for TODO 1.62 / 1.65):** `exe+0x3C64A0(src, data, effects, target, blend, users)` (the play both `exe+0x3C63E0` and `exe+0x3C6CC0` use) plays the entry on `target+0x158` and, when `target+0x6C8` bit 2 is set, also on the actors at `target+0xD60` and `+0xD68`; then `target+0x184` = a new negative id. `exe+0x3C7190(motCtrl, n, blend)` plays entry n of the actor's second motion file (`+0x160`, else `exe+0x3C86A0`) and sets `+0x2B8` to that file, so `+0x2B8` != 0 doesn't always mean a grabber's or a Limit's file. `trap_obj_motion_is_capture` (`exe+0x42D360`) reads `+0x18C` bit 14 after calling `exe+0x3BA720`. Where the game clears bit 14 was not found (no AND / BTR on `+0x18C` / `+0x18D` with that bit; `+0x18C` is written whole in many places).

Armored Xemnas II's shield (0x12/0x17 programs 73): the player presses `F_EH060`'s commands for 're_f_eh060-00' (menu path) and '-10' (from `0x1B16D1`), `F_EH060` hooks that Sora (trap_obj_hook), and about 5 s later it offers 646 in slot 1 by `trap_command_override` (1/165) with itself as the source; the host's player pointer was the Sora copy at that moment (one brain's nearer-player swap), not the hooked Sora.
