- **How a Limit sends the allies away and brings them back (Ghidra session 35, 2026-10-14; for the Limit rule in docs/DECISIONS.md L1):** the Limit start `exe+0x3D8B40` makes the Limit object (0x128 bytes, `exe+0x3D7FA0`, stored at `exe+0x2A24CC0`), then for party members 0 and 1 (`exe+0x3C3270` = GetFriend, the function our puppet.cpp hook wraps) calls `exe+0x400990(actor)` on each one that `exe+0x3BA720` says is live (in the actor list and `+0x120` & 0x10080000 clear) unless `+0x6C8` bit 22 is set or `+0xDD4` != 0 (probably how the Limit's partner stays; not checked). `exe+0x400990`: looks the member up by its character id (`exe+0x3B5B20`, `exe+0x3C3280`), starts the fade (`exe+0x3B7810(actor + 0xA08, ...)`) and sets `+0x9B8` |= 0x10 (the removal the table row 'Fade-out removal of the Sora copy' shows), and appends the member's id (`exe+0x3E31C0`) to a 'bring back' list: u32 ids at `exe+0x2AE9788`, count at `exe+0x2AE9790`. `exe+0x400A30` does the same for both members at once (callers `exe+0x3DE460`, summons by the summon state code nearby, and `exe+0x3FCEC0`); `exe+0x400BB0(id, ...)` for one id. The Limit end `exe+0x3D8F40(limit)` calls `exe+0x400440()` (unless the Limit's `+0x54` bit 4 is set: then `exe+0x400830()` just empties the list): every id in the list is built again with `exe+0x3C2FC0` and faded in, which fits 'Goofy comes back ~20 s later at a new address without the actor factory' (table row 'Limits in a fight'). `exe+0x400880(id)` = is that id in the list; `exe+0x400330()` = none of them exists any more. Other callers of `exe+0x400440`: `exe+0x40CC00`, `0x40CF20`, `0x414DD0`, `0x4157D0`, `0x3DE080`; of `exe+0x400830`: `exe+0x3C3390`, `exe+0x3A5650` (room loads?). The end's last step sets the current Limit's `+0x54` |= 4 (an 'ended' mark) and leaves `exe+0x2A24CC0` set; the pointer is cleared later. Confirmed live (table row 'A Limit's leave and bring-back work when called from the other game'): the mark comes about 1 s before the pointer clears.

- **The per-enemy reaction list the player's menu reads every frame (Ghidra, session 35, for 1.26):** `exe+0x4081C0(slot state)` runs for the player each frame: unless `exe+0x3BA4C0(player)` or `exe+0x2A11400` bit 14, it tries the player's own reaction slot (`exe+0x3B2660(menu, 1)`, priority 0xCB), the command at `player+0x638` with the actor at `player+0x2B0` when `player+0x18C` bit 20 (0xCC), then walks every actor (`exe+0x3BF4F0` iterator) through `exe+0x407DE0(state, actor)` and `exe+0x407B40`. `exe+0x408080(state, command, target info, priority, n)` checks `exe+0x3B6A10(row, player)` and fills the slot through `exe+0x408BD0` -> `exe+0x402130`. Enemies add entries to their list with `exe+0x40A590(list, a, b)` (8 entries of 12 bytes: a, b, active byte): script call 1/49 `method_obj_enable_reaction_command` (`actor+0x540`) and motion trigger type 6 (`exe+0x40D810`, list at `+0x4E0` of the trigger's actor; type 7 = `exe+0x40A500` removes). Motion trigger type 0x14 offers a command straight to the player (`exe+0x3B2850`, or `exe+0x3B25C0` when the motion is the player's own) and sets `player+0x18C` bit 17 / `+0x638`. Not yet checked: which of these Xaldin's 649 and Luxord's 458 use (list entry by script on the host, or by a mirrored motion in both games), so the host can tell that its boss offers the command to anyone near it.
- **Grab calls: what the script calls do (Ghidra, session 34, for grab_sync.cpp; table 1 = `exe+0x755370`, 16 bytes an entry, table 0 = `exe+0x752E00`):** An actor argument is a handle to an object whose +4 is the actor's handle. 1/29 `trap_obj_motion_capture(grabber, actor, motion, blend)` = `exe+0x42D160` -> `exe+0x3C6CC0(grabber + 0x158, actor, motion, blend)` (blend is a float: `movss xmm6,[rcx+0x18]` at `exe+0x42D17A`, passed in xmm3) (returns an int; sets `actor+0x2B8` = the grabber's motion file and `+0x180` = motion; the same call our puppet uses for reaction-command motions). 1/101 `trap_obj_motion_is_capture(actor)` = `exe+0x42D360`: `actor+0x18C` bit 14. 1/74 `trap_obj_idle(actor)` = `exe+0x42D0E0` -> `exe+0x3BFB70(actor)`, `exe+0x3B4DA0(actor, 0)`. 1/18 `trap_obj_attach(parent, actor, a, b, cb, cbarg)` = `exe+0x42C550` -> `exe+0x3D9FE0(parent, actor, a, b)` (correction, session 35: the first one is the one attached, to the second; see the table row 'What an actor is attached to') (a, b whole numbers in r8d / r9d; when cb != 0 the returned record gets +0x10 = `*(exe+0x2A25048)`, +0x18 = cb, +0x20 = the actor of cbarg). 1/102 `trap_obj_detach(actor)` = `exe+0x42C5F0` -> `exe+0x3DA250(actor)`: the parent is the handle at `actor+0x6A0`; it unlinks the actor and puts back the action saved at `+0x6A8` (`exe+0x3B4DA0`). 1/153 `trap_obj_reset_pos(actor, vec)` = `exe+0x42B880` -> `exe+0x3B6100(actor, float[4])`; 1/79 `trap_obj_set_dir(actor, vec)` = `exe+0x42F540` -> `exe+0x3B6420(actor, float[4])` (the vector is a pointer into the running script's memory, NaNs zeroed). 1/220 `trap_obj_move_to_space(actor, f, flag)` = `exe+0x42BBB0` -> `exe+0x3B9AC0(actor, float f, flag != 0)` (f a float, 250 in Data Roxas's grab; flag = only the low 32 bits of the script's value: grab sync sent all 64 bits and the friend got flag 1 where the host's was 0, bench 01:43:43). 1/157 and 1/219 `trap_obj_camera_start(owner, name, target)` = `exe+0x42E070`, 1/164 `trap_obj_camera_start_global(owner, name)` = `exe+0x42E0D0` -> `exe+0x3DB2F0(owner, name, target or 0)`: name = up to 4 letters packed into a u32, looked up in the owner's file (`owner+0x928`, entry type 0x16) and started as an in-fight scene (`exe+0x3ABDC0(obj, 0xB, data)`; Data Roxas's grab: 're_b_ex390-00', after the steal '-10'). 1/112 `trap_obj_hook(actor, pc, owner)` = `exe+0x42CA20`: starts a script thread at pc in the running script (`exe+0x3E1970(*(exe+0x2A25048), 0, pc, {actor handle, owner}, n)`) and sets `actor+0xD48` to it (`exe+0x3D76D0`); in Data Roxas's steal that thread makes the grabbed Sora hit Roxas (`trap_attack_new(copy, 1839, ...)`). Same function as the Final Xemnas host-crash lead below. 0/31 `func_screen_whiteout(t)` = `exe+0x41DD70`, 0/32 `func_screen_whitein(t)` = `exe+0x41DDB0` (t a float).
- **The controller reads in Final Xemnas's Riku part (Ghidra, session 35):** 0/81 `trap_pad_is_trigger(n)` = `exe+0x41DAE0`: bit n of the u64 at `exe+0xBF31A0 + pad * 0x68 + 8` for pad 0 (`exe+0x39B780(0)`), i.e. a button pressed this frame on the first controller, whoever the script is about. 1/113 `trap_player_get_movement(actor)` = `exe+0x42FE30`: copies the vector at `*(actor + 0xDD0) + 0xB50` (`exe+0x3AA4C0`) into `exe+0x756B48` and returns it (the actor's stick movement, presumably). So in the host's game both reads follow the host's controller when the held Sora is the host's; with the copy held, `player_get_movement(copy)` reads the copy's +0xDD0 record (what the puppet leaves there not checked). Both run through our trap thunk (world_census.cpp), so their answers could be replaced (the friend's stick and button for option 2, or a steering rule for option 1). Not tried.
- **Grab motions and motion triggers (Ghidra, session 34):** 1/151 `trap_obj_motion_check_trigger(actor, n)` = `exe+0x42D230`: bit n-1 of the byte `actor+0x2AD` (the grab trace saw it 0 every 6 frames, so not seen set yet). `exe+0x3C7190(ctrl, motion, blend)`: while `ctrl+0x160` (= `actor+0x2B8`, the grabber's motion file) is set it plays the motion from that file, else the normal set `exe+0x3C86A0`; `exe+0x3C63E0(model, file, x, actor, motion, blend, y)` is another capture (sets `actor+0x2B8` = file, `+0x180` = motion). Nothing found yet that clears `+0x2B8` by itself; a grabbed Sora frees itself when the grab motion ends (table). Also seen once: during Data Roxas's motion-256 grab of the host's own Sora, command 675 was in the friend's menu (the friend's bench presses triangle; the host refused it, 'not offered to the friend lately'); where the friend's game got it from is not known
- **Motion lookup and the second motion file (Ghidra, session 34, for Data Saïx's T-pose):** script call 1/11 `trap_sysobj_motion_start` = `exe+0x42B320` -> `exe+0x3C86A0(actor+0x158, motion, a, 0)` (the same set-motion world sync and the puppet use); 1/12 `trap_sysobj_motion_change` = `exe+0x42B3B0` -> `exe+0x3C6DC0(actor+0x158, motion, a, 0)`, which (when the motion exists) keeps the phase between motions 1 and 2, then calls `exe+0x3C86A0` too (table 1 entries read from the exe: +11 x 16 = `exe+0x755420`, +12 x 16 = `exe+0x755430`). The lookup `exe+0x3C7C50(motCtrl, motion)` turns the id into an entry index `table exe+0x5C5D38[variant x 4 + k] + motion x 4` (variant from `exe+0x3BA460(actor)` and `actor+0x6C8` bit 2 with `exe+0x3D7640`; k = 0..3 tried in turn) and looks it up in the second file `motCtrl+8` (= `actor+0x160`) first, then in the actor's own `motCtrl+0` (= `actor+0x158`), through `exe+0x40D7A0(file, index)`. Lead: Saïx's Berserk motions may come from a file the host's script loads that the friend's Saïx (brain off) never gets; to check: compare both games' Saïx `+0x158..+0x2C0` at Berserk (scratchpad probe ready).
- **Companions' emergency pick (Ghidra, session 34, for TODO 1.58; used and confirmed on the bench, see the row 'The host's companions heal the friend's copy'):** `exe+0x1B3A30(companion)` (callers: the think `exe+0x1B53B0` right after it sets `+0xF78`, and the normal pick `exe+0x1B1D40`) runs only while `+0xF78` is set and `+0x4A8` is 0; it clears the current action (`+0xED0..+0xEF0`), runs every action entry of the companion's helper (`+0xDF8`) flagged 0x10 (then 0x20) in check mode, picks one of those that said yes at random and runs it at once (Cure `exe+0x1CC1A0`; HP item `exe+0x1CC970` -> `exe+0x1B7D90(companion, item, target, 1)`, which sets `+0xBF8` = the target). Both heal actions aim at `+0xF78` first (`exe+0x3BEE60(info, +0xF78, 0)`) and only add itself, the player or the other companion when that left no target. 'Needs help' `exe+0x3C1D30(stats)` = HP <= max / 4 (max rounded toward 0). So putting another actor into `+0xF78` before the pick aims both heals at it (companion_heal.cpp).
- **Reaction commands: where the offer goes and how a press could reach the boss (Ghidra, session 34, for a 'one player at a time' reaction command):** `trap_command_override_top` (script call 1/237) = `exe+0x4300A0`: it writes the four commands into the menu of the global player, `exe+0x3B2910(*(*(exe+0x2A105D0) + 0xDD0), ids, source)` (the shared field command menu `exe+0x2A10620`), not into the menu of the Sora the boss grabbed: four slots at menu+0xA28 + n x 0x24 (the command from `exe+0x3B68B0(id)`, the source actor's handle; skipped while the menu's actor `+0xAE0` has `+0x6C8` bit 25; slot filler `exe+0x402130`). `trap_obj_can_decide_command` (1/221) = `exe+0x42BBF0` -> `exe+0x3B6950(command, actor, source)`. `method_obj_enable_reaction_command` (1/49) = `exe+0x42ECF0` -> `exe+0x40A590(actor + 0x540, a, b)` (a per-actor list; for objects like chests and the chandelier). `trap_obj_reaction_callback` (1/325) = `exe+0x42C0D0` -> `exe+0x3B5FA0`, which posts script event 0xB to the actor's script (`actor+0x5B0`) through `exe+0x3E1840(script, event, args)`. Event 10 with two numbers is posted by `exe+0x3B3030` (a vtable entry at `exe+0x5C4F70`, also reached from class thunks `exe+0x55E600` ... `exe+0x561DB0`), `exe+0x3B4C90` and `exe+0x3F4980` (to the 8 scripts at `exe+0x2AE5D88`): a candidate, but see next. **How a press reaches the source:** the menu's execute step `exe+0x3B22D0(menu, command row, target info)` checks `exe+0x3B6950` and stores the pick (`exe+0x3D7F30(menu+8, id, target info)`: menu+8 = u16 command id, menu+0x10 = the target's handle); the player's update carries it out through `exe+0x405440`, which sends every type it doesn't know (a reaction command's type is its own id, e.g. 672; command table `*(exe+0x2A161E8)`, 0x30-byte rows sorted by id, `exe+0x3B68B0(id)`) to `exe+0x3A8B10(player, row)`: special cases 0x1E / 0x1F (Talk) / 0x20 / 0x37 / 0x7F; else, if `exe+0x3BFB70(target)`, `exe+0x3D7C60(player)`, the row's action byte +0x25 (1: `exe+0x3B4DA0(player, 0)`, 2: player+0xC object vtable +0x18), and last **`exe+0x3B5FA0(target, player, id, menu+0x10)`: script event 0xB to the target's script with the user, the command id and the target** (the same function as `trap_obj_reaction_callback`). Command rows (03system.bin 'cmd', 751 rows): 672 = type 672, flags 0xB951, priority 100, message 52862; 104 = type 0, flags 0x11 (the empty slot the scripts put around it). **The menu's slots and the 'can use' check (Ghidra, session 34, after the Data Roxas bench):** `exe+0x3B2910(menu, ids, info)` fills slot n at menu + 0xA1C + n x 0x24 through `exe+0x402130`: +0 u16 = 0, +2 u16 = the command id (row +0), +4 u8 = 1, +5 u8 = `exe+0x401D60(row, menu actor, info)`, +8 u32 = handle; +0xC..+0x1F = the 20-byte target info; +0x20 u8 = 100 (a slot is refilled only while that byte is < 0x65 or its +2 is 0). The menu's execute step `exe+0x3B22D0(menu, row, info)` returns at once unless `exe+0x3B6950(row, *(menu+0xAE0), info)`: row +0x1F = 0 checks the menu's actor, 1 the source actor (from the info's handle), 2 both, through `exe+0x3BA070(actor, row)`: 1 if `actor+0x18C` bit 17 and `actor+0x638` = the id (or bit 21 and `+0x638` = row +0x22); else, unless `+0x18C` bit 30, if (row flags bit 8 and `+0x18C` bit 19) or (`actor+0xB94` <= 0 and (`+0x158` = 0, or `+0x18C` & 0x1C, or (row flags bit 7 clear and (`+0x18C` bit 10 or `+0x194` bit 5)), or row flags bit 2)); row flags bit 4 clear also needs `exe+0x3ABB20()` = 0. Rows from 03system.bin 'cmd': 672 = `a0 02 a0 02 a0 02 ff 00 7e ce 00 00 51 b9 ...`, +0x1F = 0 (the menu's actor: the friend's own Sora, which no script holds in the friend's game), +0x22 = 672, +0x24 = 4; 104 = flags 0x11, +0x1F = 0.
- **Friend crash in Data Roxas: the effect pool full, a new site `exe+0x4D3A84` (two-PC run 2026-10-13 20:47:58 and 20:48:39, session 33, friend logs; crash + Ghidra):** the same fault twice (writing address 0x4, rbx 0), stack `0x4D3CD1 <- 0x4AD3FD <- 0x4B115D <- 0x4D3B30 <- 0x2C2A6C <- [kh2coop effect step hook] <- 0x2BD3B0 <- 0x14FDFE`. `exe+0x4D3A20` (called by the particle operator `exe+0x4D3B30`, which spawns child particles at a model's vertices) writes the new particle's +4 through the result of the particle allocator `exe+0x4B02F0` without a null check; `exe+0x4B02F0` returns null when the effect pool allocator `exe+0x4AF240` does. `exe+0x4AF240` is a first-fit free list over the pool; when nothing fits it calls the function pointer at `exe+0xB60280` (if set) and returns null. `exe+0x4B02F0` has 19 call sites in 17 functions (`exe+0x4BF720`, `exe+0x4D36E0`-`0x4D5A20`, the particle operators run per particle by `exe+0x4B0FD0`); the ones read check `exe+0x2B0EFC0 == 0` and the system's +0xC1 before spawning, not the result. `exe+0x4AF240` has 22 call sites. Room 0x12/0x15, Data Roxas `B_EX390` with two brains (ONE_BRAIN_TYPES was M_EX020 only): the first crash came 85 ms after the friend's own scene `re_b_ex390-00` started for the second time in 3 s (the host's game never played it), the second 1.2 s after the friend's Sora went down. What filled the pool isn't known (no pool sampling in the friend's log).
- **Where the effect pool is made, and its size (Ghidra, session 33, for a bigger pool; the pool setup `exe+0x4AF380` and a buffer elsewhere are confirmed on the bench, see the table, session 34):** `exe+0x2C4330(heap, size1, size2)` -> `exe+0x2BDB50` allocates two buffers through the heap's vtable +8 (`(*heap)[1](heap, size, 0)`; kept at `exe+0xB1DA28` / `exe+0xB1DA30`, heap at `exe+0xB1DA20`) and passes them to the effect system setup `exe+0x2C2480(buf1, size1, buf2, size2, ...)`, which makes the effect pool with `exe+0x4AF380(&exe+0xB60808, buf1, size1)` (pool struct: +0 rover, +8 start, +0x10 end = start + size - 0x10, +0x18 bytes in use) and sets `exe+0x2B0EC08 = exe+0xB60808`. Callers of `exe+0x2C4330`: `exe+0x152768` and `exe+0x1E1568` pass the heap `*(exe+0x9BA920)` (or another) with size1 = 0x80000 (512 KB; matches the 521008 B of in use + free) and size2 = 0x20000; `exe+0x152D8A` and `exe+0x152FF5` pass the u32s at `exe+0x2A0F7D4` / `exe+0x2A0F7D8`. So the setup runs again on loads, and size1 is one number. The handle encoder `exe+0x4AD390` adds a new 32 MB base to the 64-slot table `exe+0x2B0D720` for an address it hasn't seen, so a buffer anywhere in memory gets valid handles. More (Ghidra, session 33/34): `exe+0x2C2480` also keeps size1 at `exe+0xB60288`, read only by `exe+0x2C3020` (a debug text: in use `exe+0xB60820` / size x 100); `exe+0xB60808` itself is referenced only by `exe+0x2C2480`. The base table is filled with -1 once (`exe+0x4AD2C0`, a static init) and never cleared, so its slots only grow (a handle is `0x80000000 | slot << 25 | address & 0x1FFFFFF`; past 64 slots it gives a wrong handle). The allocator `exe+0x4AF240` walks the blocks by their handles and works out sizes from addresses, with no fixed pool size in it. Not checked: when the game's own buffers are freed (`exe+0x2BD980` reads `exe+0xB1DA28`).
- **Twin Lords: a short no-damage moment at HP 344 (bench, session 32):** in 4 of 6 runs of `test_census.py twins` the host applied the friend's claims on a Lord at HP 344 with no HP change for 0.2-0.7 s ('HP 344 -> 344'; the Volcanic Lord in some runs, the Blizzard Lord in others, both in one); in another run the same Lord went 349 -> 344 -> 339 without stopping. Starting HP 422-432, so 344 is about 80 %: likely a phase point of the fight in the game itself (claims go through the game's own HP change). Not checked in Ghidra.
- **Script call table 1 entry 27 is a getter here (Ghidra bytes, session 32):** OpenKH's name list (trap_names.inc) calls {1, 27} `trap_area_event_set`, but its function `exe+0x42E3B0` (table `*(exe+0x753490 + 8)` = `exe+0x755370`, entry +27 x 16) is `movzx eax, word [exe+0x717010]` (the current event program, `kNow`+8), stores it in args[0] with the tag '@INT' and returns. So the names near it may be off for this build; how an object's reaction command starts a story event is still to find.
- **Changing a player's Keyblade at run time (Ghidra 2026-10-13, session 31; for TODO 3.3):** `exe+0x3D7C70(actor, weapon object, slot)` builds a weapon actor (0xC10 bytes, ctor `exe+0x3EF750(mem, object row, owner, *(decode(owner+8))+0x4E, slot, n)`) into `actor+0xD60 + 8 x slot`; callers: the party member ctor `exe+0x3C2DB0` and `exe+0x3A7A40` (twice; the player's set-up, likely). `exe+0x3D59C0(?, character, slot, Keyblade item)` (one caller at `exe+0x36AF8B`, likely the menu's equip) is the game's swap: finds the party actor with that character id (`exe+0x3B5B20`), destroys its weapon (`exe+0x3EFF20`), computes the new weapon object with `exe+0x3EF8B0(kind = *(decode(actor+8))+0x4E, item)` (table `*(exe+0x2AE5A38)`: per kind an offset, + the item row's u16 at +4), asks for its file through `exe+0x3E0A20(kind, slot, object, ...)` (`exe+0x3E0660` load request, `exe+0x3EF910` / `exe+0x39E000` the kind's weapon file slot), builds it like `exe+0x3D7C70` and shows it (vtable +0x88) if the old one was shown. For the copy (character 1, like our own Sora) the game's swap would pick our own Sora, and the weapon file slot is per kind, so loading the other player's Keyblade there may replace our own Sora's: to research before building 3.3.
- **The stat change's twin `exe+0x3D2C50(self, actor, delta, channel, flag)` (Ghidra + trace, session 31):** same body as ApplyStatDelta `exe+0x3D2EB0` (also tells the enemy HP bar), slot +0xE8 of `YS::BTLOBJ`'s function table (`exe+0x5C6250`, RTTI `VTABLE<BTLOBJ>`); prologue `48 89 5C 24 10 48 89 6C 24 18 56 48 83 EC 20 48 8B DA`. A hit through it would skip our ApplyStatDelta hook (claims, god mode). Trace hook (`KH2COOP_TRACE_STAT2=1`, puppet.cpp) in six bench fights (melee, Wisdom Form, a Limit, a summon, spells): **never called**. Who calls it is still unknown.
- **Script syscalls, still open (session 29):** table 11 of `exe+0x753490` points to `exe+0x2B047D0` (empty in the file, filled at run time?), not in OpenKH's list, not looked at. The rest of the old lead (tables, names, call site) is now the table row 'Script system calls ('traps'): the tables, live'. Function addresses from the file scan (not each hooked by itself): 1/0 `trap_sysobj_appear` `exe+0x42B130`, 1/28 `trap_obj_leave` `exe+0x42C810`, 1/19 / 1/20 `trap_sysobj_fadeout` / `fadein` `exe+0x42B4B0` / `0x42B500`, 1/133 / 1/134 `trap_obj_hide` / `show` `exe+0x42B640` / `0x42B680`, 1/108 / 1/109 `trap_obj_show_part` / `hide_part` `exe+0x42FD10` / `0x42FD40`, 1/49 / 1/50 `method_obj_enable_reaction_command` / `disable` `exe+0x42ECF0` / `0x42ED30`, 1/262 `trap_obj_voice` `exe+0x42BDE0`, 1/263 `trap_player_set_exec_rc` `exe+0x42FEE0`, 1/93 `trap_sysobj_party` `exe+0x42FA10`.
- **Party member constructor and the two companion spots (Ghidra session 28, 2026-10-12; not checked live):** `exe+0x3C2DB0` (the constructor for object type 1, party-member class: Donald, Goofy, world allies) puts the new actor in the first free entry of `exe+0x2A239B0` (u64[2], what GetFriend reads) and stores the index at `actor+0xDD0`; `exe+0x3C2870` / `exe+0x3C3470` clear that entry on removal; `exe+0x3C3390` clears both. Player-class actors (type 0, Sora and our copy) never go there (fits the old row 'friend slots' with the copy in the party: {Goofy, empty}). The spawn factory `exe+0x3DF930`: object 0x236 -> the player's object (`exe+0x405190(0)`, the form-aware table at `exe+0x3E2E40()+0x10`), then by the object row's type: 0 -> `exe+0x3A7CB0` (player class), 1 -> `exe+0x3C2F00` (party member), others to their own constructors. Placeholder resolver `exe+0x3DFEB0`: 0x236 -> `exe+0x2A24FA0` or `exe+0x405190`, 0x237/0x238 -> `exe+0x3C32E0(1/2)`, 0x319/0x31A -> `exe+0x3C3320(1/2)`, 0x3EE -> `exe+0x3C3320(3)`.
- **Final Xemnas's grab (the Riku takeover): when it comes (two-PC runs 2026-10-11/12, session 28; logs only; bench facts now in the table row 'Final Xemnas's grab: low HP alone doesn't start it', the rule itself still unknown):** both crashes at the grab came with `B_EX170_LAST` (1500 max HP) at 230 HP (host log kh2coop_33228, 00:14:13) and 187 HP (host log kh2coop_16828, 01:43:41; Sora motion 261 'other set' just before). Two points only: an HP threshold (about 15%) is a guess; it may also be on a timer or random. Way to check: set his HP in a test and wait. Volpestyle's control tool warps with the same RequestTransition and makes Sora untouchable with team 0 (`actor+0x4DC`, table row); for a grab test that is no use, since an untouchable Sora can't be grabbed either.
- **Soft reset while a room change is starting = crash (two-PC run 2026-10-11 23:49:11, session 28, friend log kh2coop_17396; crash only):** the host's game had been closed; on the friend's 'Connection lost' screen (over the eh29 cutscene) 'Title Screen' was picked at 11.203; at 11.420 the friend's own game asked for 0x12/0x16 program 72 (the scene's end move, chain `0x3A49D9 <- 0x3ACC94 <- 0x3AC01E`), and at 11.569 our soft reset (`exe+0x154DB0`) went in. 100 ms later: the same fault as a double room request, `exe+0x39DA55` (release list `*(exe+0x29F33C8)` empty), stack `0x156EF1 <- 0x1DF2FE <- 0x39C827 <- 0x152509 <- 0x150728 <- 0x1503E5 <- 0x14F65B`. Reading: the soft reset's teardown and the room change's teardown both ran. Why the cutscene ended 33 s after it started (it runs about 5 min) is not known. Ghidra: `exe+0x39DA10(x)` walks that list for the entry whose `+0x58` is x and lowers its count `+2`; with an empty list it writes to address 2. **Fix built (session 28, TODO 6.11):** 'Title Screen' now holds every room change (ours and the game's own, follow.cpp) and the soft reset waits until no room change is asked for or loading. Bench 2026-10-12 01:05:22 and 01:08:34 (friend copy, host copy closed, Continue Alone, then a room request and Title Screen in one frame): waited 34 frames for that load, then reset, title screen, no crash. The crash itself wasn't reproduced on the bench (no run without the fix).
- **RequestTransition internals (Ghidra session 28, `exe+0x152990` and its task `exe+0x152A90`):** with byte `exe+0x9BA8D1` at 0 it makes a new load task (`exe+0x14F770`, callback `exe+0x152A90`) and keeps it in `exe+0x9BA928` (table row); with the byte set it only copies the packet to the second staging `exe+0x717120` (a redirect). The task counts down `task+0x18` (the mode argument) frames, waits until `exe+0x1515C0()` and `exe+0x19BA60()` return 0, then sets in-field `exe+0x9BA8D0` = 0, tears the room down (`exe+0x39B640`, `exe+0x39C800`, ...), clears `exe+0x9BA8D1`, commits the location (`exe+0x152BE0`), schedules LoadRoom `exe+0x152680` and clears `exe+0x9BA928`. Reading: two requests before the first task runs = two tasks = the room torn down twice = the `exe+0x39DA55` crash (fits all three sightings). In old scene logs the game's own second request within a frame or two of the first always came after the room had switched (load already started), e.g. Twilight Town 02/09 asked twice 1 ms apart (log kh2coop_6484).
- **Boss in-fight scenes ('re_b_...', Ghidra 2026-10-12 06:00, session 28; for TODO 1.20 boss special moves):** `exe+0x3AD2D0(event)` plays a built event object: StartCutscene `exe+0x2CC4B0(event[2], event+0x60, ...)` (the hooked one; the scene log's 'from ? <- 0x3AD31C' is this call), makes it the current event `exe+0x2A11478` (an older one is stopped first). Callers: `exe+0x3FB0DD` (mission complete, 're_mscomp'), `exe+0x3FC9B0`, `exe+0x3D8E75`, `exe+0x3DB3B2`, a data ref at `exe+0x2BB5F4C`. **`exe+0x3DB2F0(actor, char* name, target)`** = play the actor's own event by name: packs up to 4 chars of `name` into a u32, looks it up as entry type 0x16 in the actor's file (`exe+0x3A6480(*(actor+0x928), 0x16, packed)`), builds a 0xC0 event (`exe+0x152430` alloc, `exe+0x3ABDC0(obj, 0xB, data)`), sets event+0x10 = actor+0x80, binds `target` if given (`exe+0x3AD270`), plays it (`exe+0x3AD2D0`). `exe+0x3D8CF0(handles*, name, float, byte)` is the script-command form (handles decoded with `exe+0x4AD270`; [0] owner, [1] actor whose file holds the event, [3] the actor placed and bound to it) and ends in a tail call to `exe+0x3AD2D0`, which is why the logged chain goes straight from `0x3AD31C` to the interpreter `0x41C4FC`. Idea for syncing boss moves: on the host, see `exe+0x3AD2D0` with event+0x10 = a synced boss + 0x80, send the event name; the friend calls `exe+0x3DB2F0` on its bound copy of that boss (and drops its own boss's scenes). Not tried.
- **Host crash in Final Xemnas: enemy-script command `exe+0x42CA20` on a missing actor (two-PC run 2026-10-12 00:14:13, session 28, host log kh2coop_33228; crash + Ghidra; 'needs Riku' is now verified on the bench, see the table row 'Final Xemnas's grab crashes the game when Riku is missing'; the Ghidra parts below are still leads):** access violation at `exe+0x3D76DF` reading `[0 + 0xD48]`. `exe+0x3D76D0(actor, obj)` stores `obj` at `actor+0xD48` (if one is already there it is released first through `exe+0x3E1410` and `exe+0x3E1510`). Its callers (Ghidra): `exe+0x42CA20` and `exe+0x42CAB0`, script commands. `exe+0x42CA20(args)`: arg 0 is a handle, decoded (`exe+0x4AD270`) to an object whose `+4` is another handle, decoded to the actor; it makes something with `exe+0x3E1970(*(exe+0x2A25048), 0, args[2], {id, 0x52444140}, n)` (`exe+0x2A25048` = the running AI thread's second argument, lead 'Current AI script thread') and hands it to `exe+0x3D76D0`. The actor decoded to 0. Stack: the AI interpreter's command call (`0x41C4FC`) from an actor update (`0x3BF519`, `0x408514`, `0x3B68A0`) with two of our hooks in the chain (kh2coop.dll+0x80DE, +0xE191). Final Xemnas (`B_EX170_LAST`) was at 230/1500 HP; in the host's game Riku didn't exist (the Sora copy had his slot, table row 'W_FRIEND_ONLY rooms take whoever is in active slot 1'). Guess, not checked: Xemnas's script refers to Riku at that point. **Seen again on both PCs at once (2026-10-12 01:43:41, host log kh2coop_16828, friend kh2coop_25812):** same fault, at the moment Xemnas grabbed a Sora (Sora motion 261 'other set' just before); in both games the party list had the Sora copy and no Riku (both had gone through the ship fight's NO_FRIEND). Friend's stack `0x4AD3FD 0x42CA9E 0x4AD25B 0x41C4FC 0x4AD3FD 0x408514 0x3B68A0`. Strongly fits 'the script asks for Riku when Xemnas grabs Sora'; the actor itself not checked in memory.
- **Pause menu (menu 8): item lists, text and custom items (TODO 6.8, session 27, 2026-10-11; Ghidra + `14mission.bar` and `msg/us/sys.bar` read from the OpenKH-extracted files; opening with our list, the callback, the message hook, the line text and the closing are now verified, see the table row 'Pause menu with our own item list'):** `exe+0x2E4C20(src, def)` opens the pause menu with an item list `src` and an optional definition `def` (stores them via `exe+0x31D860` in `exe+0xBEF4A0`/`exe+0xBEF4A8`, then opens menu 8 through `exe+0x2E4950(8)`; if `src+0x25` bit 0 is set it also zeroes `exe+0x156DC0()+0x108`). Callers: the Start-button watcher `exe+0x1572B0` (opens only when `exe+0xABB878 == 0`, `exe+0x9BA8D0 != 0`, the task `exe+0x156DC0()+0x108 == 0` and Start is pressed; list from `exe+0x3AD540`), the game-over flow `exe+0x3FCEC0` and `exe+0x3A3540`. `exe+0x3AD480(id)` finds list `id` (bsearch, 0x40-byte entries) in the table at `*(exe+0x2A11498)` = the `slct` entry of `14mission.bar` (set by `exe+0x3F0050` → `exe+0x3AD5D0`). `exe+0x3AD540` returns the current list (the mission's pause list from the 6 slots at `exe+0x2A114A0`, else list 6). List entry (0x40 bytes): +0 u16 id, +2 u8 item count, +3 u8 cursor, +4 up to 4 items {u16 kind, u16 message id (0x8000 bit stripped)}, +0x14 s16 title sequence (layout 0x7531; -1 none), +0x16 s16 sprite in layout 0x7532 (-1 none), +0x18 u32 32-bit game pointer to a text shown in sprite 0xE of layout 0x7532 (0 none), +0x24 u8, +0x25 u8 flags. Lists on this build: 6 = combat pause (no items), 4 = cutscene pause (6 Continue, 5 Skip Scene), 2 = mission pause (6 Continue, 3 Retry, 0xC Help, 4 Quit), 1 = game over (2 Continue, 1 Load Game), 10 = game over 'I won't give up!' (9) / 'It's all over...' (8), 0x1C = (6 Continue, 0xA Jiminy's Journal, 0x11 Save), 0x24/0x25 = Return to Menu (0x13). Item kinds are handled in the input handlers `exe+0x31DD70` / `exe+0x31E280` (cursor kinds in `exe+0xBEF538[cursor]`): 1 → next 3, 3/4 → `exe+0x3A3DD0(kind)`, 5/0x13 → Skip Scene (`exe+0x2CFCD0`; these are the 'not identified' callers `0x31DECA`/`0x31E331` in the Skip Scene row), 6 → close (`exe+0x2EB790`), 10 → next 4, 0xC → yes/no window `exe+0xBEF550`, 0x11 → next 1, **any other kind → `(*(def+0x28))(kind, task)`** when `def` and that pointer are set. Start/cancel (cursor -2/-4) closes the menu. `def`: +0 u32 id and +8 pointer for an extra picture (game over's boss picture; skipped when +0 is 0), +0x28 the callback above. The item fill `exe+0x31F1F0` makes one text sprite per item (`exe+0x2F74A0(0xC,0,0,layout,msgId)`), whose text pointer (+0x1F8) comes from the message lookup `exe+0x3AD9A0(msgId)` (434 callers: bsearch in 3 message tables at `exe+0x2A11668` (stride 0x10), {u32 id, u32 offset}; not found → message 0xADC). The +0x18 text sprite is `*(exe+0xBEF518)+0x220`, its text pointer at `+0x418`. 32-bit game pointers: `exe+0x4AD270(p)` = 0 for 0, else `exe+0x4AD3F0`: `base[(p & 0x7FFFFFFF) >> 25] | (p & 0x1FFFFFF)`, 64 bases at `exe+0x2B0D720`. Text format (sys.bar, us): 0 ends, 0x01 space, 0x02 new line, 'A'-'Z' = 0x2E-0x47, 'a'-'z' = 0x9A-0xB3, '.' 0x4F, ':' 0x52, '?' 0x49, '!' 0x48, apostrophe 0xEC (e.g. 0xAB0 'Continue' = `30 A8 A7 AD A2 A7 AE 9E`). sys.bar message ids run 0x1BA-0x562D.

Not watched directly. No longer matters to our code: after the two-PC test (session 27) the reload is wanted in fights and boss fights too, so link_watch.cpp has no fight check any more.
- **Forced-solo rooms: the area script's `Party` command (TODO 5.5, session 27, 2026-10-11; room files + Ghidra; the templates, the rewrite by `exe+0x3E31F0` and the Bailey fight are now verified, see the table rows 'Party templates', '`Party` command rewrites the world party entry' and 'Forced-solo fight: HB Bailey'; still files/Ghidra only: the room scan and the interpreter's other cases):** room scripts (`btl`/`map`/`evt.script` in `ard/us/<room>.ard`) set the party per program with command **0x0F `Party` {u32 value}**: 0 NO_FRIEND, 1 DEFAULT, 2 W_FRIEND, 3 W_FRIEND_IN, 4 W_FRIEND_FIX, 5 W_FRIEND_ONLY (OpenKH SpawnScript names; opcode and value read from the bytes of `hb08` btl program 0x34 = Bailey: `0f000100 00000000`). Bailey (HB 04/08 program 52) = btl `Party NO_FRIEND` + `Mission 0x8D "HB08_MS102"`; the evt program 52 says `Party DEFAULT` (the scene after). Scan of all 300+ `ard/us` rooms: 406 DEFAULT, 238 W_FRIEND, **187 NO_FRIEND** (90 btl, 97 evt), 44 W_FRIEND_FIX, 8 W_FRIEND_ONLY, 6 W_FRIEND_IN. NO_FRIEND btl outside the Roxas prologue (tt*): dc00 51/64/66, eh14 58/101, eh19 59/97, eh21 65/99, eh22 72/90, eh24 80, hb01 75, hb08 52/91, hb16 62-65/87-90, hb17 66, he00 140/141/174, he09 190/191/194/195, mu07 25/73/76/99/106, mu08 104, tr02 61/62 (list made by a scratch script, `party.tsv`). Code: the area script interpreter **`exe+0x3A24C0`(ctx, command)** switches on the u16 opcode (0 Spawn → `exe+0x3A4E80`, 4 Capacity → f32 `exe+0x2A0F7DC`, 0x15 Mission → `exe+0x3A3FA0`, 0x1C a nested block run only when its value == `exe+0x71700A`...); **case 0x0F calls `exe+0x3E31F0(exe+0x3E2E20(-1), value)`**: `exe+0x3E2E20(world)` returns `exe+0x9ACDE4 + 4*world` (world -1 = the current one, u8 `exe+0x717008`) = **the world party entry save+0x3534** if the save starts at `exe+0x9A98B0`; `exe+0x3E31F0(entry, value)` rebuilds that 4-byte entry from the template u32 `*(u64 exe+0x2A252E8) + 4*value` (byte 0 the player; member bytes with bits 0xC0 set are taken from the template, the entry's current members are kept where they match the template; empty = 0x12). Session 26 host log (kh2coop_24112) has no 'party entry ... leaving it' line during Bailey, so the entry wasn't simply emptied to `00 12 12 12`: read live in session 27 (`00 00 01 02` after Bailey, table row above).

- **Close-ups hide the body with SeqPart; the crown is part 1 and stays (session 26, 2026-10-11, script + Ghidra):** cutscene command 0x0D SeqPart {s16 start frame, s16 end frame, s16 actor id, s16 parts[] ending -1} → handler `exe+0x2D1310` (walker case 0xD) → `exe+0x1A8800(*(actor+0x7B0), &parts)` → virtual call `(*(obj+0x20))->vtable[0x60](*(obj+0x20), *(obj+0x340), parts)`. Scene `mu_event_112`: body Sora (actor 0) gets parts {1..31} at frame 621 and {0..31} at frame 781; the close-up head (actor 1) leaves at 690. Reading: the list = parts shown; the close-up hides part 0 (the body) and leaves part 1 (the crown) on, which fits the crown you saw floating during a close-up with our gold crown on. Other cutscene handlers: case 0xE SeqAlpha `exe+0x2D0780`, 0x19 SeqTextureAnim `exe+0x2D0DB0` (u16 cmd+8 value, byte +0xA flag: 1 → `exe+0x3CD8A0`), 0x1A SeqActorLeave `exe+0x2D0A20`, 0x4D SeqHideObject `exe+0x2DA660`. Not seen live: that removing part 1 from such a list stops the floating crown.
- **Sora's crown (session 26, 2026-10-11, wikis only):** owning Proofs (Connection: Lingering Will; Nonexistence: all Data Organization; Peace: Mushroom XIII) puts a crown on Sora's head on its own (not an equipped item): 1 Proof = copper, 2 = silver, 3 = gold. Shown in every Drive Form, **not in cutscenes and not in the world costumes** (Halloween Town, Atlantica, Space Paranoids, Timeless River, Christmas Town). How the game decides it (which key items it checks, whether per actor or for every Sora model, mesh in P_EX100 vs. an attached piece) is not known yet. Item ids 593/594/595 (0x251-0x253) for the three Proofs: the crown code below counts exactly these three (static, not yet seen live). Sources: khwiki.com/Crown, kingdomhearts.fandom.com/wiki/Crown.
- **Crown code (session 26, 2026-10-11, disassembly + Ghidra; the setter, the pick and the values are now verified, see the table row 'Sora's crown'; the cutscene Sora showing it is verified too; still not seen live: the callers of the pick, the cutscene command):** `exe+0x3A9E20(actor)` = the crown pick: counts how many of the items in the u16 table at `exe+0x5C4C98` = {0x251, 0x252, 0x253} the player owns (item count `exe+0x3C4380(item, 100)` > 0 for each; 100 = the player's own inventory, it then jumps to `exe+0x3C4720`), takes byte `exe+0x5C4CA0[count]` = {0 Proofs: 0xA4, 1: 0x9A, 2: 0x9B, 3: 0x9C} and tail-calls **`exe+0x3CD8A0(actor, value)`** = `exe+0x1A87A0(*(actor+0x7B0), value)`: walks the list at `*(actor+0x7B0)+0x68` (next = `exe+0x1C13B0(item)`), asks each item's vtable +0x10 `(item, 1, 0xFFFF, value)` and on yes calls `exe+0x1C06B0(item, value)`, which maps `value` through a range table (min u16 +8, max u16 +10, index table at +0x18) of the item's resource and stores the chosen entry pointer at `item+0x288` (looks like picking a motion/pattern by number, not an effect: Sora's `P_EX100.a.us` PAX has 57 effects numbered 0x00-0x27). `exe+0x3CD8B0(actor, value)` is the matching 'off' (→ `exe+0x1A8570`). The pick runs **per actor**: tail-called from `exe+0x3A7040` and `exe+0x3A8E70` (after `exe+0x3D6470(actor)`) and from `exe+0x3A7F30` on the player pointer `exe+0x2A105D0` (if `exe+0x3BA720(player)`). **A cutscene command uses the same setter:** the handler ending at `exe+0x2D0E1B` looks up a scene actor (`exe+0x2CB300(id)`), reads u16 cmd+8 (value) and byte cmd+0xA (1 = `exe+0x3CD8A0` on, else `exe+0x3CD8B0` off). The cutscene Sora files have no `.a.us` (P_EX100 and P_EX120 do). Also: the three Proofs are checked together by `exe+0x3E6A60`, a difficulty-based condition (save+0x2498 via `exe+0x152350`; difficulty 3 needs all three), so 0x251-0x253 = the Proofs is near certain. Inventory per Volpestyle: save+0x3580 (lead).
- **Telling the players apart: Volpestyle's notes (read 2026-10-11, their main c2c76e6):** no mention of the crown anywhere in their repo, and no cutscene appearance work (they don't edit cutscenes; clients wait behind an overlay during the host's). Their plan (not built) is **recoloured Soras**: recolour variants shipped as puppet assets in an OpenKH mod with extra objentries, each PC picking the variant for each remote player. Techniques they list (ONLINE_COOP_PLAN.md 'Appearance', pointer_map_v1.md): the GoA ROM swaps party costumes by **rewriting objentry model-name strings before a room loads** (`obj0` = pointer at OBJENTRY_POINTER; `obj0+0x16F0` = Donald's model name, `obj0+0x1750` = Goofy's); or replace a companion's model + moveset files (Master-Trio). Warning from Expert595 via them: **don't remap a loaded actor's resources at runtime**, it crashed during room teardown. Their player kits (PlayerKits.hpp, PLAYER_KITS.md) make the local player Roxas (objentry 0x5A `P_EX110`) or Mickey (0x5B `P_EX200`) through objentry descriptors, qualified live in Hollow Bastion only.
- **Game's Steam ID read `exe+0x14D1E0` (crash on the friend's PC 2026-10-10, disassembly; session 25):** returns the player's CSteamID: uses a cached one at `exe+0x8CC44C` when its account bits are valid, else calls the import at `exe+0x57BC78` (likely SteamInternal_ContextInit) with the context at `exe+0x713360`, takes the ISteamUser* from it and calls vtable +0x10 (GetSteamID). The save path builder `exe+0x103E40` calls it at `exe+0x104045` (the `\%llu` folder). When the game's Steam didn't start (the friend ran `KINGDOM HEARTS II FINAL MIX.exe` directly, not through Steam / Mods Manager), the ISteamUser* is null and the game crashes at `exe+0x14D275` (`mov rax,[rcx]`, rcx 0) while opening the save at boot (stack 0x10404A <- 0xFF080), before the first frame; those runs never asked Steam for the user-stats interface either. Not caused by the mod: our Steam code only starts at the first frame.
- **Steam networking (session 25, 2026-10-10; read from files; the exports we use, the game's own Steam session, NetworkingMessages send/receive, rich presence and Join Game are now verified, see the table row 'Steam link'; still unverified: Matchmaking/lobbies, InviteUserToGame, the overlay invite dialog and Volpestyle's notes below):** the game folder's `steam_api64.dll` (298856 bytes) is a recent SDK (accessors `SteamAPI_SteamFriends_v017`, `SteamAPI_SteamUser_v023`, `SteamAPI_SteamMatchmaking_v009`, `SteamAPI_SteamNetworking_v006`) and has the flat-API names for `SteamAPI_SteamNetworkingMessages_SteamAPI*`, `SteamAPI_ISteamNetworkingMessages_SendMessageToUser` / `_ReceiveMessagesOnChannel`, `SteamAPI_SteamNetworkingSockets_SteamAPI*`, `SteamAPI_SteamNetworkingUtils_SteamAPI*` + `_InitRelayNetworkAccess`, `SteamAPI_ISteamFriends_SetRichPresence` / `_InviteUserToGame` / `_ActivateGameOverlayInviteDialog`, `SteamAPI_ISteamMatchmaking_CreateLobby`, `SteamAPI_ISteamUser_GetSteamID`, `SteamAPI_ManualDispatch_Init` (strings found in the file; the ones in the 'Steam link' row resolve with GetProcAddress, the others weren't tried). The exe initializes Steam itself (table row 'Steam achievements': SteamAPI_Init, RunCallbacks, Register/UnregisterCallback). **Volpestyle (docs/STEAM_TRANSPORT.md, docs/FRIEND_PLAYTEST_STEAM.md, read 2026-10-10):** ISteamNetworkingSockets (CreateListenSocketP2P / ConnectP2P / AcceptConnection), relay-only (ICE 0), using the game's own Steam session for app 2552430 with no second SteamAPI_Init and no app 480; a broker DLL in the game pumps `SteamAPI_ISteamNetworkingSockets_RunCallbacks` every 16 ms and talks to their separate runtime over a local pipe; players connect by typed SteamID64 or an invitation text `kh2coop:steam:<SteamID64>` (host allowlist), from their external launcher; no lobbies, overlay invites, friends picker or Steam 'Join game'; a launcher may create `steam_appid.txt` (2552430) if missing. Their two-PC transport test passed 2026-10-08; host/join through their package UI not yet tested live; known: a shutdown race with the game's SteamAPI_Shutdown, 30 s connect timeout. One Steam account can't test this alone: two copies on one PC share one account. **Their in-game code (inject/src/SteamBrokerNative.cpp, read 2026-10-10):** `GetModuleHandleW(L"steam_api64.dll")` + `GetProcAddress` of flat exports only ("never vtable calls"): `SteamAPI_GetHSteamUser`, `SteamAPI_SteamUser_v023`, `SteamAPI_SteamUtils_v010`, `SteamAPI_SteamNetworkingSockets_SteamAPI_v012`, `SteamAPI_SteamNetworkingUtils_SteamAPI_v004`, then per-method exports; no SteamAPI_Init: retries once a second for up to 60 s until the game's Steam is up (user + pipe handle nonzero, expected app id, logged on, valid SteamID); connection events through socket config value 201 (a plain function pointer: the connection-status-changed callback) instead of CCallbackBase/RegisterCallback, pumped by calling `SteamAPI_ISteamNetworkingSockets_RunCallbacks` itself every 16 ms on its own thread; config 104 = 0 (ICE off, relay only), 9 = send-queue bound, 24 = connect timeout; `InitRelayNetworkAccess` + `GetRelayNetworkStatus` / `GetAuthenticationStatus` checked before use; the DLL is pinned; a known race with the game's SteamAPI_Shutdown at exit. Their transport layer (common/src/SteamTransport.cpp) only forwards frames over a pipe to that broker (their separate runtime exe): tied to their design, not reusable as is.
- **Save container (Ghidra, session 25, 2026-10-10; the opening, path, swap and writer parts are now verified, see the table row 'Save container: second container'):** `exe+0xFEF80(container, slot size, slot count, path or null)` opens a save file: null path = the string at `exe+0x7164E0` (the Steam save), reads the whole PNG with _sopen/_read (a file that exists but is empty is deleted with DeleteFileA), pulls the save chunk out of the PNG (exe+0x144A40 with callback exe+0x100FB0), decrypts it with a key from the player's Steam ID (exe+0x145930) and copies it into the container's buffer (`+0x10` = data: 0x10 header, slot count × 0x158 slot info, then the slots); if the result is empty it writes a fresh file (exe+0x100810). The KH2 container pointer lives at `exe+0x79CB10` (made at boot by exe+0x101000); a second one at `exe+0x79CD38` (exe+0xFF760, likely the system file). The writer `exe+0x100810(container)` saves to the path kept inside the container (`+0x40`), creating the folder if needed. **Live (bench 2026-10-10, save guard log):** the save file is read once at start (KHIIFM_WW.png, then KH1.5+2.5_system_WW.png); the title's LOAD screen ('Checking PC for save data') reads nothing from disk, also the second time: the save list comes from memory.
- **Title menu code (Ghidra, session 25, 2026-10-10; the Theater check, menu input and LOAD path are now in the table above):** `exe+0x2B4590` title main loop (makes the layouts, plays `vagstream/Title.win32.scd`, runs the menu each frame, then starts New Game / Load / Theater); `exe+0x2B4C60` title start (loads `title.2ld` or `titlejf.2ld`, sets the row count); `exe+0x2B35A0` menu input (up 0x10 / down 0x20 move the cursor and wrap, confirm 0x4 maps the row to the action: 3-row menu row 0 New Game, 1 Load, else Back; 4-row: 0, 1, 2 Theater, else Back); `exe+0x2B4480` creates the rows layout with the group of the current row; `exe+0x2B3C10` closes the menu (exit animation). **Load:** action 1 calls `exe+0x2B70D0` (waits for the menu system) then `exe+0x2E4950(3, -1)` = opens generic menu 3 (the save list); when a save is picked, `exe+0xBEDF60` (u8, read by exe+0x2E78D0, cleared by exe+0x2E7680) becomes nonzero and the title starts the load. Theater = action 2 loads `menu/eventviewer.2ld`. `exe+0x14EAD0` = bit 25 of `exe+0x749804` picks `titlejf.2ld` and a 2-row menu (read 0 here). The Title Screen Skip mod's patch at `exe+0x2B3732` sits in this input function.
- **Save file reading and writing (Volpestyle docs/SCENARIOS.md 'How the game writes saves', their headless Ghidra 2026-10-02; read session 24 for TODO 5.7/5.19; the opening `exe+0xFEF80` and the writer `exe+0x100810` are now verified on our build, see the table row 'Save container: second container'; the rest not checked):** the save path comes from the string `KINGDOM HEARTS HD 1.5+2.5 ReMIX\Steam\KHIIFM_WW.png` at `exe+0x7164E0`; `exe+0xFEF80` opens the container read-only (and calls `DeleteFileA` on it if it reads as empty); `exe+0x100810` creates the folder, checks free space and writes the PNG through `exe+0x145170` (`_wfopen_s(path, "r+b")`, then `"wb"`); `exe+0x101000` (boot, also `_chdir`) can call the deleting helper `exe+0x1007A0` after init errors or an account/header mismatch. Their party notes (docs/PARTY_SETUP.md): `exe+0x3E38D0` swaps two selector bytes of the SAVE party row; `exe+0x3C2120` commits status bytes into a SAVE character record. Save layout (OpenKH `SaveDataFinalMix.cs`, via their OPENKH_REFERENCE.md): +0x0C world, +0x0D room, +0x0E spawn, +0x24F0 characters[13] × 0x114, +0x32F4 drive forms[10], +0x36E0 EXP. Their design (ONLINE_COOP_PLAN.md D6): one shared co-op save, clients never save, host progress is canonical; cross-account saves: import raw entries into the target account's own container keeping its header/footer/XOR key (Kingdom Save Editor's `PcSaveArchive` format). Nothing about the title menu beyond navigating NEW GAME / LOAD / BACK.
- **Mission end commands (TODO 5.14, Ghidra + exe bytes + scan of all 303 `msn/us/*.bar` scripts, 2026-10-10 session 24; the command call at `exe+0x41C4FA` and both endings (mission complete through `exe+0x3A41E0`, a trigger fired straight to the area settings runner) are now seen live in the Pete fight, see the table row 'Story fight end: mission scripts'; which command number ran wasn't logged, the numbers and the scan are Ghidra/files only):** mission scripts are AI-style scripts (bar entry type 3) run by the interpreter `exe+0x41B400`: 16-bit words, opcode = word & 0xF, **opcode 10 = call command**, trap = word >> 6, command index = next word; the command entry is `table[trap] + 16 × index` = {fn(u64 args[]), u32 arg count / flags (0x40000000 = returns a value)} (`call [rdi]` at `exe+0x41C4FA`). Mission scripts use the table set `exe+0x753490` (11 pointers, trap 3 and 9 empty); **trap 4 (`exe+0x757250`) = mission commands**. Three end the fight: **#3** `exe+0x4334C0` → `exe+0x3A33C0(arg)` → `exe+0x3A41E0(mission, 7, arg)` (0x9C instead in one case) = Mission Complete (293 missions); **#16** `exe+0x4334D0` → `exe+0x3A3440` → `exe+0x3A41E0(mission, 0x10, arg)` (282 missions, likely Mission Failed); **#22** `exe+0x4334F0` → `exe+0x3A37A0(arg)`: sets mission+4 \| 0x14 and other end flags, then fires trigger **0xF** (282 missions; the Pete fight's 30% HP ending). `exe+0x3A41E0` also sets mission+4 \| 0x14 and later fires its kind as the trigger (area settings 7 / 0xF in tt25 fight program 149 both play 803 then move to 02/1A). Other trap-4 commands tie triggers to info-bar counters/timers (#5, #6, #8, #9, #20, #41). Missions with none of the three: AL00_SKATE_02, DC02_MS102, EH25_MS109, EH27_MS106, EH28_MS107, HB02_KINOKO_ROX, HB09_SKATE_02, NM09_SKATE_02, TT04_MS106, WORLDMAP; Atlantica (LM*) only #3. Current mission object `exe+0x2A0FF68`; mission task `exe+0x3F4860` (registered by `exe+0x3F4910`).
- **Party HUD (TODO 3.11, Ghidra 2026-10-09; the list, set/clear, room-change clear, bit 4 and the update hook are now verified, see the table row `exe+0xABCFE8`; the rest below is still Ghidra only):** the HUD classes are `dk::GAUGE_PLAYER` (vtable `exe+0x5B2100`), `dk::GAUGE_FRIEND` (`exe+0x5B2158`, ctor `exe+0x16B5A0`, size 0x1710), `dk::FACE`, `dk::GAUGE_ENEMY`. HUD init `exe+0x16C3A0` makes the player gauge (ptr `exe+0xABD248`), friend gauge 0 (`exe+0xABD250`) and friend gauge 1 (`exe+0xABD258`) and sets each up with `exe+0x181D20(gauge, actor, index)`. **The gauges read their actor from a 3-entry array `exe+0xABCFE8`** (u64[3]: [0] player, [1] friend gauge 0, [2] friend gauge 1; getter `exe+0x1689A0(i)`), with a u32 flag word per entry at `exe+0xABD000` (bit 0 = slot changed, 2/4/8/0x10 = events set by actor at `exe+0x168930/0x1688F0/0x1688B0/0x168870`). Set slot `exe+0x168850(i, actor)`, clear slot `exe+0x168970(i)`, clear all `exe+0x1689E0`. Callers of the setter: `exe+0x3FD830` (player command setup: slot 0 = the player) and `exe+0x3C27F0` / `exe+0x3C35C0` (an AI party member puts itself in slot `[actor+0xDD0] + 1` when `[actor+0xDD0] < 2` and `actor+0x6C8` bit 22 is clear). A player-class actor (our Sora copy) never goes through those, and its +0xDD0 is the field-command pointer, so it never gets a slot. Friend gauge refresh `exe+0x182400` re-reads array[index+1]; per-frame update `exe+0x182730`: HP fill = `exe+0x18E8A0(actor)` = stat[0]/stat[1] of `*(actor+0x5C0)`, MP fill = `exe+0x18E8D0(actor)` = `+0x180 / +0x184` of the same block; gauge state (`gauge+0x20`) from `exe+0x17EC50`: 2 when `actor+0x9B8` bit 2 (dead), else 1 if `exe+0x3C1D30(stat block)` (likely low HP), else 0. Face picture `exe+0x17EB30(face, actor, state)` → `exe+0x17E980` → `exe+0x3B57B0(actor, &pic, ...)` (picks it from the actor). Face: `exe+0x3B57B0` looks up the entries named "face" of types 0x18 and 0x19 in the actor's file at `actor+0x928`; this PC's extraction has them in `obj/P_EX100.a.us` (Sora), `P_EX030.a.us`, `P_EX020.a.us` and the form files, so a Sora copy probably gets Sora's face. (Confirmed on the bench 2026-10-09: it does, at the player gauge's size, bigger than the ring.) In BAR terms 0x18 = image (IMGD) and 0x19 = 2D sequence/layout (SEQD), so the face is a picture plus a layout that sets its size and place; `exe+0x17E980` loads the picture into the texture object at `face+0x200` (`exe+0x1909E0`) and only sets base coordinates there (`exe+0x191870` → +0x10, `exe+0x191800` → +0x2C; that family just rebuilds the texture registers in `exe+0x191010`, no scale), then the face is drawn by `exe+0x16B160(face, layout, face+0x200, anim 0..3)`. Lead for shrinking the copy's face: hand the copy's widget a patched copy of the layout (scale/offset) from `exe+0x3B57B0`. Only HUD code reads the array: the getter's 7 callers are all in the HUD (`0x16C5xx`, `0x18091F`, `0x182412`, `0x182650`, `0x1827E3`), and the only direct references are the small functions above. Clear-all is reached through the tail call `exe+0x157B10`. A party member's removal `exe+0x3C2890` clears its slot, and so does the player command teardown `exe+0x3FD610` for slot 0. Note: the copy's `+0x5C0` points at our own Sora's stat slot (table above), so a gauge for the copy would show our own HP until the copy has its own block (3.4). The gauge also reads `**(actor+0x5C0)` directly into its damage trail (`gauge+0x2C/+0x30`, in `exe+0x182400` and `exe+0x182360`) and the low-HP state through `exe+0x3C1D30`, so swapping only the two fill readers wouldn't be enough.
- **Sora's death and revive (disasm 2026-10-18, session 38; for the Mickey loser that can't jump / floats):** death = player class vtable +0xB0 `exe+0x3A7330`: `exe+0x3FD5A0(move = *(actor+0xDC0))` = movement off (`move+0xB3C` bit 0 cleared, `+0x24 |= 1`, `+0xC30` bit 0 cleared, `+0xC40` = 0, plus `exe+0x3DD510(move+0xBE8)`, `exe+0x408B90(move+0xB10)`), `actor+0xDD8` = 0, `exe+0x3D5DC0` (class +0xA0(actor, 2, 0), `exe+0x3D2E80`). **Revive `exe+0x3AA8D0(actor)`** (confirmed live 2026-10-18 02:10, now in the table) (only caller: the script-call wrapper `exe+0x42E1E0`, handle -> actor -> +4 handle -> actor; Healing Light's, presumably): `+0x9B8 &= ~4`, `exe+0x3B9D20(actor)`, `exe+0x3B4DA0(actor, 0)`, `*(actor+0xDD0)+0xAE0 = actor`, **`exe+0x3FD5F0(move)` = movement on** (`+0xB3C |= 1`, `+0x24 &= ~1`, `+0xC30 |= 1`, `+0xC40` = 0; also called from `exe+0x42FFB2` and the script call `exe+0x430150`), then the class's +0xE8(actor, max HP, 0, 0) = HP to max. The Mickey loser's dump after our old revive (bench 2026-10-18 02:04, `mcoop_dump_loser_vs_winner.txt`): `move+0xB3C` 1 -> 0, `move+0xC30` 3 -> 2 (= movement still off), entries at `move+0x64`, `+0x1C0..+0x1F0`, `+0x3C0..+0x3E4` 1 -> 0x501, `move+0x34C` 1 -> 0x101, `actor+0x1A8` / `+0x1AC` 0 -> 1.
- **The player's own gauge, its KO face and the low-HP beep (disasm 2026-10-18, session 38; the hook's effect is confirmed live, see the table row 'Our gauge's KO face and no low-HP beep while down'):** built by the HUD build `exe+0x16C3A0` through the constructor `exe+0x16B6F0`, pointer `exe+0xABD248` (friend gauges `exe+0xABD250` / `exe+0xABD258`); vtable `exe+0x5B2100`, +0x08 update **`exe+0x181270`** (the friend gauge's vtable `exe+0x5B2158`, update `exe+0x182730`); gauge+0x40 = the actor. The update first calls the state dispatcher `exe+0x17EE60` (KO state from `exe+0x18E940` = actor+0x9B8 bit 2), then the beep: `exe+0x1DCB30` = 'beep on' (byte `exe+0xAD8040`); if the actor is dead (`exe+0x18E940`) and the beep is on -> stop (`exe+0x1DCC30`: byte 0, `exe+0x1DF740`); else low HP (`exe+0x18E920`) and off -> start (`exe+0x1DCC60`: byte 1, sound 2 through `exe+0x1DF790`), on and not low -> stop; a hidden HUD (`exe+0x166E80`) also stops it. So the dead bit set only for this update shows the KO face and stops the beep, nothing else in the game sees it.
- **Party widget events and KO state (Ghidra 2026-10-09, session 20, not checked live):** `dk::GAUGE_FRIEND` vtable `exe+0x5B2158`: +0x08 update `0x182730`, +0x28 refresh `0x182400` (event bit 0), +0x30 `0x1823D0` / +0x38 `0x1823C0` / +0x40 `0x1823B0` (bits 1/2/3) = hit reactions, each `exe+0x182530(gauge, anim 0x46/0x45/0x44)` (damage-trail update + ring animation); +0x48 `0x182360` (bit 4, HP changed). Bits 1-3 are raised by the hit handlers `exe+0x3D60C0` / `exe+0x3D5370` through `0x168930` (bit 1, normal hit), `0x1688F0` (bit 2, hit type 3), `0x1688B0` (bit 3, hit type 4 or the target already dead); no separate KO event. The event dispatcher `exe+0x17EE60` (start of every update) first sets the widget state `gauge+0x20`: **2 (KO) when `exe+0x18E940(actor)` = `actor+0x9B8` bit 2**, else 1 when `exe+0x18E920` → `exe+0x3C1D30(*(actor+0x5C0))` (low HP), else 0; ring animation 0x43 for state 1, 0x48 otherwise; face via `exe+0x17EBA0(face, state)` (group 2 = KO face). So the KO face shows when bit 2 is set during the update and the HP-changed event fires.
- **Group spawning pace (Ghidra 2026-10-09, session 20; the delay unit fits the two-PC logs, the rest Ghidra only):** per group each frame `exe+0x3FF000(group, player pos)` (from SpawnTask `exe+0x3A4F90`) clears group flag bits 3/4 and, unless SpawnBlocked `exe+0x3ABC80`, counts the group's cooldown float `group+0x20` down by `exe+0x717480` (frame step) and, for each trigger box that contains the position (box vtable +8), calls GroupTrigger `exe+0x3FE320` (sets flag 8). So **the spawner only runs on frames when a position is inside a box.** Spawner `exe+0x3FE6F0`: returns at once while the cooldown > 0; for each record of spawn type 2 it makes the enemy only if no actor with its serial exists (`exe+0x3B41E0`, room-wide, no group check), the serial isn't in the defeated list `exe+0x2AE5E60` (`exe+0x3F5900`, 256 u16 at `[+0x820]+8`; likely the 'cleared until the World Map' list) and the record's stage byte (record+0x30, spawnparse 'level') <= `group+0x2C`; after a successful spawn the cooldown = the record's u16 delay (record+0x2A, spawnparse 'delay') and the loop returns on the next record. Spawn type 1 spawns at the player (`exe+0x3B5B40(player pointer exe+0x2A105D0)` + offset), type 2 at the record's spot. Stage advance `exe+0x3FEB50`: sums objentry byte +0x54 (a 'weight': Soldier M_EX010 12, Shadow M_EX020 8) over the group's records still alive or not yet defeated; if the sum <= group header byte +0x22 the stage goes up. Live check: HB 04/09 group 248 (4 Soldiers, delay 15 each) spawned all four 0.25 s apart (= 15 frames) when the host's Sora triggered it (friend log 2026-10-09 20:34:30), but ~14-17 s apart when only the friend's position triggered it (TODO 1.44). Group 248's trigger box: center (-2044, -1126, 642), half-size (383, 139, 695) (spawn file `ard/us/hb09.ard` entry b_00).
- **Friend crash at `exe+0x3C795C` (2026-10-08 bench, first sighting; cause found in our code, see TODO 1.41: the host's enemy table matched actors by address only, and the game had reused a dead Shadow's memory for group 244's new enemy):** script call `exe+0x42F2D0` (reads arg handle -> actor -> `[+4]` handle -> actor, then `exe+0x3C7950(actor+0x158 motion controller, motion id)`) asks for a motion's data; `exe+0x3C7A00` returns the current motion object (`+0x18`) when the id is the current one (`+0x28`), else looks it up (`exe+0x3C7C50`, then `exe+0x40D7A0` in the set(s) at `+8` / `+0`) and returns 0 when the set lacks it; `exe+0x3C7950` then reads `[0]` -> crash. So an actor whose script asks for a motion its motion set doesn't have crashes (the user saw a T-pose just before). Context in the friend's log: "spawn: host enemy serial 79 (group id 245) spawned here at (-744, -1, -303)" right after group 244 was triggered, then "unmatched: host#4 M_EX020@(619,-100,-89) ours#5 M_EX020@(-744,0,-303)", a mirrored kill, and the crash 0.25 s later; the user saw a Shadow where group 244's Soldiers should be. TODO 1.41.

- **Cutscene runtime (Ghidra 2026-10-09, session 20, TODO 5.12; the start hook, the handler calling convention, the actor table and the slots are now verified, see the table rows 'Cutscene start', 'Cutscene command handlers', 'Cutscene actor table'; the rest below is still Ghidra only):** start cutscene `exe+0x2CC4B0(script, ...)` (callers `exe+0x3AD2D0`, `0x3AC350`; prologue `48 89 5C 24 08 48 89 6C 24 10 48 89 74 24 18 57`) stores the script pointer at `exe+0xB65218` with two cursors (`+8` walker, `+0xC` setup), copies the scene name (script+10, the SetProject command) to `exe+0xB65578` and starts three tasks: setup `exe+0x2CC180` (handles SetActor 0x01 → `exe+0x2D0E30` → table add `exe+0x2CB2A0`, SetupEvent 0x0F, EventStart 0x10, ReadAssets 0x24...; sets `exe+0xB64FA4` = 1 at the script's end), command walker `exe+0x2CB5D0` (waits for the setup, then one switch over all Seq commands; type 0x03 is in neither switch) and `exe+0x2CC8A0`. Scene actor table `*(exe+0xB653E0)`: 0x60 entries of 10 ints {object (-1 = free), actor id, handle, ...}, lookup `exe+0x2CB300(id)`; animation slots `*(exe+0xB653D8)`: 0x40 entries of 14 ints. SeqPlayAnimation `exe+0x2D2160(cmd)` (actor id cmd+0x0E, path cmd+0x12) creates the actor on its first animation with the factory `exe+0x3DF930(object | 0x40000000, pos)` (bit 30 = event actor: allocated 0xBB0 and built by `exe+0x419230`, no spawn budget check) and takes an animation slot (no null check: a full slot table would crash). SeqActorPosition `exe+0x2D0BD0` (actor id cmd+0x24, frame cmd+0x26, only SubId 0) looks the actor up and returns if missing; SeqActorLeave `exe+0x2D0A20` does not check. `exe+0x2C87F0(frame)` = wait for that scene frame (true when skipped). Object table types: ACTOR_SORA 571 / ACTOR_SORA_H 572 type 2, SORA_WEAPON_R 573 type 6 (not player class 0).
- **Cutscene twin doesn't move (bench 2026-10-10, session 21, TODO 5.12): cause found in session 22,** see the table row 'Cutscene twin froze: our puppet took it for the Sora copy'. Still Ghidra only: SeqPlayAnimation schedules task 0xA7EE (`exe+0x2C8850`, actor id at task+0x18), which frees (`exe+0x2CEE10`, motion data back to the heap at `exe+0xB65548`) every animation entry in state 4 whose +0xC is that actor id and +8 is 0; when entry+8 is 0 also task 0xA7F3 (`exe+0x2CEF40`, entry state = 4). Entries are tagged with Sora's actor id, not the twin's, so the twin's own 0xA7EE never frees anything; Sora's frees the entries both used, after both moved on to the next one (same frame).
- **TT 02/08 after-fight scene (game rule, user 2026-10-10):** event 707 starts after about 90 s of the Dusk fight or when a Sora drops to 30% HP; consistent with the bench (host's Sora at 7/24 HP, 707 started at once).
- **SetTarget modes (exe+0x3BE2B0, Ghidra 2026-10-08):** 0 = the player pointer `exe+0x2A105D0` (or `exe+0x40C870(exe+0x2A16370)` when actor+0x120 bit 11 is clear); 1 = the last party attacker `+0xD40` if valid and not dead (`+0x9B8` bit 2), else the player; 2 = `exe+0x3BEE60(slot, player pointer, 0)`; 3 = nearest target point over the list `exe+0x3DB9D0` (`exe+0x3BE7F0`); 4 = `exe+0x3BEA30`; 5 = like 3 with a bonus `exe+0x5B22E4` for actors with `+0x6C8` bit 10; 6 = sight search `exe+0x3BE4B0` (range = `exe+0x3ABA10()` × `[actor+0x150]+0x5C`, height window `+0x60/+0x64`, best score wins, nothing in range = empty slot). The mode is stored at slot `+0xC` (`enemy+0xC04`). Our logs' "mode 6: N / 0 / ..." tallies are enemies whose sight search found nobody.
- **Get-target call `exe+0x4319D0` (Ghidra 2026-10-08):** if the actor's target slot isn't valid (`exe+0x3BDE30`) and `+0x6C8` bit 4 is set and the mode isn't 3, it re-selects with SetTarget mode 2; then returns the slot. Fits the ~1000 re-selects a second seen with an emptied slot (table row "Enemy with an emptied target").

Seen in the friend's log (attacker log, first 150 hits): 52 by its own Sora, 29 by the Sora copy (the host's avatar), 8 by mirrored Goofy `P_EX030`, 25 by mirrored Hercules `P_HE000`, 25 by the HB defense `F_HB020` (team 3). - **Drive gauge (2026-10-08, live read, not confirmed by a change):** Sora's stat slot (exe+0x2A23598) +0x1B0 = 0x64 (100, fill of the current bar?), +0x1B1 = 3, +0x1B2 = 3 while the HUD showed "MAX 3"; +0x1AF = 1 (the state byte exe+0x3A8800 checks). - **Effect instances and Limit objects (Ghidra, session 29, 2026-10-12; for the Limit's particles on the copy):** `exe+0x2C4050(inst)` zeroes a PAX instance (0x30 bytes); `exe+0x2C6320(inst, pax, 0)` fills it (`+0` pax, `+8` pax + 0x10, `exe+0x2BEA90(inst + 0x10, pax + *(pax+0xC))`) and links it at the end of a game-wide list (head `exe+0xB63608`, tail `exe+0xB63610`, next as a handle at `+0x28`); `exe+0x2C5730(inst)` unlinks it and ends its effects (list at `+0x18`); `exe+0x2C4240` / `exe+0x2C4280` clear the whole list. The Limit object (0x128 bytes from `exe+0x152430`, the game's allocator, made by `exe+0x3D8B40` -> `exe+0x3D7FA0`) zeroes its instance at `+0x18`, and `exe+0x3D96D0` loads the Limit's file and fills it (`exe+0x2C6320(limit + 0x18, pax, 0)`); `exe+0x3D8650` passes motion triggers that use that instance to the Limit's script. An attack (`exe+0x3CF830`, `YS::ATTACK`) keeps its effect instance as a handle at `+0x28`: its owner's `+0x80`, or the running Limit's (attack data `+0x12` bit 1), or that of the actor at owner `+0x2B0` (bit 3). Handles: `exe+0x4AD240` -> `exe+0x4AD390` encodes any pointer, adding its 32 MB block to the 64-entry base table `exe+0x2B0D720` when it isn't there yet.
- **Playing a motion from another file (Ghidra + disassembly, session 29, 2026-10-12; for TODO 1.31):** `exe+0x3C63E0(owner, bar, paxSlot, target, int motion, float blend, int* users)`: only caller `exe+0x3D93E0` (`trap_limit_motion_start` 2/47 at `exe+0x432E20` passes `(0, decode(limit+8), limit+0x18, target, motion, blend, limit+0x50)`); gets entry `motion` of the BAR with `exe+0x40D7A0(bar, n)` (entry list from `+0x10`, 16 bytes each, data handle at `+8`), plays it with `exe+0x3C64A0`, then sets `target+0x2B8 = bar`, `+0x180 = motion`. `exe+0x3C6CC0(srcMotion = src+0x158, target, int motion, float blend in XMM3) -> int`: `trap_obj_motion_capture` 1/29 (`exe+0x42D160`) and 12 other callers; finds the entry with `exe+0x3C7C50` (4 entries per motion number, from the table at `exe+0x5C5D38`; the second file `+8` first), plays it with `exe+0x3C64A0(src, data, *(src+0x158+0x148), target, blend, src+0x5B8)`, sets `target+0x2B8 = src+0x158` file, `+0x180 = motion`. `exe+0x3C64A0(owner, data, paxSlot, target, float blend, int* users)`: `exe+0x3C65E0` on `target+0x158` (and on the weapon actors `+0xD60`/`+0xD68` when `+0x6C8` bit 2), which stores paxSlot at `+0x158+0x148`, owner at `+0x158+0x158`, sets `+0x158+0x38` bit 2, counts `*users` up (`+0x158+0x1B0`, counted down when the next motion starts, `exe+0x3C7300`); then the new id from `exe+0x74B980` (counts down) goes to `target+0x184`. An actor's own motion (`exe+0x3C8CD0`, under the chain set `exe+0x3C88C0`) puts `+0x158+0x148` back to `actor+0x80` (its own effects) and clears the owner. Limit files: the room load preloads every party Limit (`exe+0x3E7650`, table `*(exe+0x2AE5690)`: count `+4`, 0x40-byte entries from `+8`); `exe+0x3E7C80(command)` loads one (0x261 = the list at `exe+0x74DA18`); the path is built by `exe+0x3E80E0` into `exe+0x2AE5698` from 'limit/%s/%s' (`exe+0x5C9020`) or 'limit/fm/%s' (`exe+0x5C9010`) and queued with `exe+0x39DF10(path, 5000, 7)`. `trap_limit_player` 2/48 reads the limit's user handle at `+0xC`, `trap_limit_friend` 2/49 the partner `i` at `+0x10 + 4i`.
- **Script threads and magic threads (Ghidra, session 29, 2026-10-12; the running thread and its actor are the table row 'The running script's actor'):** `exe+0x3E1C80(thread, parent)` runs a thread and then its children (list at `+0x48` first / `+0x50` last, next sibling as a handle at `+0x44`), with `exe+0x2A25048` = the parent (the thread itself for a root) while it runs. `exe+0x3E1970(parent, 0, pc, args, n)` makes a 0x70-byte thread (allocator object at `exe+0x2A250A0`) from the parent's program `+0`, globals `+0x58` (shared) and owner actor `+0x60`; `+8` = pc, `+0x38` flags (bit 1 = ended), the n arguments go on its stack (`exe+0x41B320`). `trap_magic_start_thread` (2/20, `exe+0x432CD0`) = `exe+0x3C5EC0(*(exe+0x2A25048), pc, &args[1], 1)`: a new thread at `pc` of the running script with one argument, put on a global list (`exe+0x3C4E90`: head `exe+0x2A23A70`, tail `exe+0x2A23A78`), so it runs apart from its owner's brain. Its argument in `F_BB090`'s script (OpenKH `Bdxio decode` of `obj/F_BB090.mdlx`, static): a block from `trap_memory_alloc` (64 or 68 bytes) holding a 16-byte copy of the caller's work header (whose `+4` is the actor's handle, so the call log names the block as `F_BB090`), `+16` an effect handle, `+32` a position, `+48` a target (`trap_target_dup(trap_btlobj_target(block))`), `+52` the thread; the thread frees the target and the block at its end.
- **Casting a spell (Ghidra, session 29, 2026-10-12; for TODO 1.31; the cast on the copy, its list and its trigger are now the table row 'The other game can cast a player's spell on its copy'):** `exe+0x3C5170(actor, spell id, flag) -> magic list entry`: finds the spell's slot in the table at `exe+0x2A23A80` (6 slots of 0x50 bytes: `+8` the spell's script program, `+0x18` its effect instance, `+0x48` its data, whose `+0x26` is the spell id, `+0x28`/`+0x2C`/`+0x30` cast motions, `+0x34`.. ), sets the actor's cast motion (`exe+0x3C86A0(actor+0x158, motion)`), makes a thread of the spell's program for the actor (`exe+0x3E1730`), puts it on the magic thread list (`exe+0x3C4E90`, run by `exe+0x3C55F0` from the head `exe+0x2A23A70`) and calls the script's entry 0x14 with (actor, the slot's instance, level) (`exe+0x3E1790`). Only caller `exe+0x3A9DD0(actor, spell)` (also sets `actor+0xDA0` = that entry), called by `exe+0x3D6FC0` for a type-2 command (after `exe+0x3D6CA0`). `exe+0x3E2000(root, pc, args, n, ..)` and `exe+0x3E1840` run a script function on a temporary thread at once; `exe+0x3E1970(parent, 0, pc, args, n)` makes a lasting thread (`trap_magic_start_thread` uses it).
- **Attacks (Ghidra, session 29, 2026-10-12):** `YS::ATTACK` (`exe+0x3CF830`): `+8` flags (bit 6 = no hits in the hit pass, bit 11 = to delete, `0x400` = id given), `+0xC` / `+0x10` / `+0x94` the owner's handle, `+0x14` a second actor's, `+0x18`/`+0x1C` ids, `+0x20` group, `+0x28` effect instance handle, `+0x30` its data (`+4` type: 5-6 hit any team), `+0x39` the teams it can hit (bit = team). `exe+0x3D2060` also skips the attack's owner and second actor. The game's attack list starts at `exe+0x2A24B40` (next as a handle at `+0xC4`).
- **Effect instance fields:** now the table row 'Effect instance flags `+0xC` and its part `+0x30`'.
- **(Partly confirmed 2026-10-08: for the HB Shadows the removal goes through the fade route slot `+0x38` → exe+0x3B4700, see "Enemy fade-out removal" in the table; the `+0x40` predicate path below wasn't seen.)** **From Volpestyle/kh2-multiplayer (read 2026-10-08, `docs/ENEMY_PARITY.md`), relevant to TODO 1.4:** an enemy that's alive gets removed via its class handler. The generic actor update `exe+0x3BFD30` calls handler slot `+0x40` (Shadow: thunk `0x419B90` → `exe+0x3DAC30`) only when actor `+0x120` bit 28 is set. If that returns true, slot `+0x48` (`0x419BA0` → `0x411800`) disposes of the actor. `0x3DAC30` steps the AI script (`0x3B4420` → `0x3E1C80`) and returns true once the script-thread count `+0x5B8` is 0 (gated by `+0x80`/`+0x98` and the `+0xBB4` child). So "too far, vanish" lives in the enemy's AI script data, not in an exe distance constant. Slot `+0x38` is a second disposal route (fade done). Their copies vanished ~50 frames after release, ~3000 u from the player. They hook the `+0x40` predicate to hold removal.
- **From Volpestyle (`docs/NATIVE_PRIVATE_STATUS.md`), relevant to 1.36 / 3.4:** a native Sora clone shares the local Sora's status record because the pool lookup `exe+0x3C04E0` shares records on an exact key match (Sora key 1, Roxas key 14). Fresh-record init is `exe+0x3C03F0` (binds SAVE data for keys 1 and 14) and the allocator is `exe+0x3C0620`. They give the clone a private record through the init. Roxas clone = object 90, key 14, `P_EX110`.
- **From Volpestyle (`docs/ENEMY_TARGET_REMOTE.md`):** enemy target choice is bdscript syscall `target_search` (bank 1, index 121, code `0x4303A0`), called through its `.data` table entry `exe+0x755B00` (table `0x755370` + 16×121). The chosen target goes into the enemy's `+0xBF8` as {actor handle, part}. Shadow attack ranges come from that target: < 300 `btl_attack`, < 600 `btl_short`, else `btl_long`.

- **From Volpestyle (`common/include/kh2coop/ProgressAllowList.hpp`, read 2026-10-08), relevant to 5.1 / 4.6:** story parts of the live save (offsets from `exe+0x9A98B0`, confirmed above): `+0x10` size `0x1C80` per-room program table (19 worlds × 64 rooms × 3 u16: map, battle, event; `+0x10 + 0x180×world + 6×room`), `+0x1C90` size `0x260` story tables (19 × 0x20, extra state from `+0x1EF0`), `+0x22F8` size `0x98` rooms-visited flags (8 bytes × 19 worlds), `+0x23AC` size `0x34` chest flags 1..411 (bit 0 of `+0x23AC` and the top 4 bits of `+0x23DF` unverified). Per player, never synced: characters/stats from `+0x24F0`, inventory from `+0x3580`, forms, munny, EXP. Their host sends these ranges and the client writes them before its room loads.
- **From Volpestyle (`docs/ENEMY_PARITY.md`), relevant to 2.5 / 6.4:** the actor factory `exe+0x3DF930(objectId, pos4, yaw)` checks its own admission at `exe+0x3A1F00`: objentry `+0x54` weight ≤ limit `exe+0x2A0F7DC` − used `exe+0x2A0F830`. A null result with admission left means the 0xD50 allocation failed. **Disasm on this build (2026-10-18, `tools/disasm.py`):** `exe+0x3DF9FB` reads the objentry byte `+0x54`, converts it to float and calls `exe+0x3A1F00`, which returns `float[exe+0x2A0F7DC] - float[exe+0x2A0F830] >= weight`; on false the factory returns 0 (`exe+0x3DFA0D` -> `exe+0x3DFE2A`). Not yet read live (lead for the Land of Dragons swarm failures). **More from Volpestyle (2026-10-18 read, `docs/ENEMY_PARITY.md` 'Static findings', `docs/ROUTE_SPAWNS.md`):** the weight goes back to the budget when the actor's shutdown starts, not at disposal: slot +0x38 (fade complete, `+0xA08` / `+0xAAC` zero) calls the base `exe+0x3B4700`, which stops scripts and motion, returns the actor's weight and sets `+0x120` bit 28; disposal comes later (`+0x40` -> `+0x48`). They observed limit 120, used 118, weight 9 in one room (room not named) and never change the limit. Our lingering Land of Dragons copies had `+0x120` = 0 (bit 28 not set), so by this lead their shutdown never started and they still hold their weight. **Shutdown start, disasm on this build 2026-10-18:** `exe+0x3B4700(actor)`: if `+0x120` bit 28 is set, only sets it again (no second refund); else, when a root script thread `+0x5B0` exists, stops the scripts (`exe+0x3CA530(actor+0x390)`, `exe+0x3E1A00(root)`), stops the motion (`exe+0x3C7300(actor+0x158)`), gives the weight back (`exe+0x3A1EE0(-weight)`: `float[exe+0x2A0F830] += x`) and sets bit 28. **Called by us live (bench 2026-10-18 03:50, friend kh2coop_14812):** on removed `M_EX660_RAW` copies: no crash, budget used 61 -> 56 (the weight 5 given back); about 1 s later the same copies showed `+0x120` = 0 and script threads 1 again (cause not found).

The enemy's revenge total and its limit aren't documented there (not in enmp). Effect setters: the second argument is a script vector (handle -> 4 floats, NaN made 0) copied into a field of the effect's part (effect+0x30) found by a getter: place `exe+0x2BE970`, scale `0x2BEA80`, rotation `0x2BE1B0`, colour `0x2BE1E0` (the copy is `exe+0x1A8E60`); set_dir turns the direction into a rotation first (`exe+0x3B7710`: x = atan2(-dir.y, horizontal length), y = atan2(dir.x, dir.z)). Pause `exe+0x2BED00(effect, on)` = effect+0xC bit 21 (0x200000). Texture animation start / stop = `exe+0x3CD8A0` / `0x3CD8B0` on the render object actor+0x7B0 (the crown's switch is `exe+0x3CD8A0` too). `obj_effect_start_pos(actor, id, place, a, b)` = `exe+0x3B66C0(actor, id, place pointer, a, b)` -> effect, encoded with `exe+0x4AD240`. `bg_hide` / `bg_show` are 16-byte stubs (no Ghidra function), one number.
