- **Mission object, values and timer (Ghidra, session 36, 2026-10-15, for mission_sync.cpp):** the mission `M = *(exe+0x2A0FF68)` (`exe+0x3A3970` returns it; `exe+0x3A3B50` = M != 0; `exe+0x3A39F0` = setters allowed = M and not `M+4` bit 2). `M+4` flags: bit 0 HUD (2D) shown, bit 1 hide pending, `| 0x14` once ended or failed. `M+0x40` = the mission file's name (`exe+0x3FB3A0` runs `strstr` on it for "PO01_MS501", "TT12_WORK_BOX", "HB32_FM_VEX", "TR04_MS104" / "TR07_MS203", "EH15_MS104" and scales the added amount). Timer slot `M+0x128` (1, 0x38 bytes; flags `+0x140`), **3 counters** at `M+0x160 + i*0x50`, **3 gauges** at `M+0x250 + i*0x48`, watches at `M+0x328 + i*0x48`; in a counter or gauge: `+0` vtable (`+0x30` = the param setter), `+0x18` flags (bit 0 in use, bit 2 signals on, bit 3 warning shown, bit 4 turned off by `exe+0x3FA070`), `+0x1C` (-1 = no signals), `+0x20`, `+0x24` maximum (int), `+0x28` warning level, `+0x38` value (counter int, gauge float). HUD parts list `M+0x3D0`, count `M+0x418`; `M+0x6A0` nonzero = `set_gauge` (`exe+0x3FBCB0`) does nothing. **Setters:** counter `exe+0x3FA230(counter, int)`: clamps to 0..max, signal **0xE** on reaching 0 and **0x7B** on reaching the maximum (`exe+0x3AABE0`, only with flags bit 2 and `+0x1C` != -1); gauge `exe+0x3FA310(gauge, float)`: signal **0x88** at 0, **0x89** at the maximum. Callers of the counter setter: `exe+0x3FBAE0` (set_count), `0x3FB2B0` (add; also called by 20 engine functions, e.g. `0x410100`, `0x543730`), `0x3FB330`, `0x3FBB30` (one digit), `0x3FB950` (to `+0x20`), `0x3FAD00` / `0x3FAD10` / `0x3FAD70` / `0x3FAD80` / `0x3FADF0` (engine adds with a difficulty factor). Callers of the gauge setter: `exe+0x3FBCB0` (set_gauge), `0x3FC230` (the gauge's set-up), `0x3D96D0`, `0x3D9130`, `0x3D9B90`. Param setters `exe+0x3FBBF0` (count), `0x3FBD10` (gauge), `0x3FBE40` (timer) call the slot's vtable `+0x30` when the slot is in use. **Mission timer globals:** `exe+0xABB850` clock (+1 a frame in `exe+0x1570F0` while `0xABB854` is 0), `0xABB854` pause bits (`exe+0x157130(bits)` sets, `0x157110` clears; the mission's pause_timer uses 2), `0xABB858` start, `0xABB85C` duration (frames), `0xABB860` value, `0xABB864` state (1 start, 2 running, 0 stopped), u8 `0xABB868` mode (1 counts down). `exe+0x1571B0` each frame unless paused: state 1 -> 2; counting down value = start + duration - clock, at 0: value 0, state 0, signal **0x4A**; counting up value = clock - start (at most 0x34BBF), at the duration the same end. `exe+0x157140(s)` count-down set-up (mode 1, value = duration = s*60), `0x157170(s)` count-up, `0x157190` start (start = clock, state 1), `0x3FA5D0(timer, s)` restart = set-up + start + unpause 2, `0x3FA0A0` returns the value. **Mission calls (trap 4, all `mov ecx,[rcx]...; jmp` stubs):** 4 information(id) `0x3A3F60`; 7 restart_timer `0x3FB9A0`; 13 pause_timer = `0x157130(2)`; 14 activate2d `0x3FB1C0` (shows the HUD parts, `M+4 |= 1`); 15 deactivate2d `0x3FB4D0`; 17 / 18 / 19 the param setters above (4 numbers: value, max, warning, slot); 24 cancel_pause_timer = `0x157110(2)`; 39 set_watch `0x3FBEB0`; 44 disable_count `0x3FB510`; 45 disable_watch `0x3FB5B0`; 46 set_warning_se `0x3FBC60`; 47 warning_timer `0x3FC030`; 49 disable_timer `0x3FB560`; 50 / 58 warning_count / reset `0x3FBF50(slot, 1 / 0)`; 57 warning_gauge `0x3FBFD0`; values: 5 set_count `0x3FBAE0(v, slot)`, 6 / 20 / 41 inc / dec / add `0x3FB2B0(n, slot)`, 8 set_gauge `0x3FBCB0(f, slot)`, 9 add_gauge `0x3FB3A0`, 10 set_gauge_ratio `0x3FBD90`, 48 set_count_figure_num `0x3FBB30`; 11 mission_failed = `jmp 0x3A3800` (`M+0x120` = 2, `M+4 |= 0x14`, then Game Over or the mission's failure flow). Flags of 13, 14, 15, 24: no arguments.
- **Script prizes and damage sheets (Ghidra, session 36, 2026-10-15, for world_mirror.cpp packet v4):** prize calls: 1/92 `trap_obj_scatter_prize` `exe+0x42C960(obj, id)` -> `exe+0x3B6170(actor, id)`; 1/226 `_mu` `exe+0x42C990` -> `exe+0x3B5CB0(actor, buf)` (its place) then `exe+0x414220(place, n)`; 1/278 `_tr` `exe+0x42C9E0` -> `exe+0x42A980(place, n)`; 1/240 `_tt` returns at once; place calls (argument 0 a script vector, NaN parts set to 0 first): 6/1 `trap_prize_appear` `exe+0x53DBA0` -> `exe+0x3DC170(place, id)`, 1/279 `_tr` `exe+0x4313A0` -> `exe+0x42A980(place, n)`, 1/342 `_num` `exe+0x431590` -> `exe+0x3DC3D0(place, id, n)`, 1/363 `_xaldin` `exe+0x431610` -> `exe+0x42AD40(place, n)`. Sheets: an actor's damage sheet `*(actor+0x5C0)` (sheets sit in an exe array near `exe+0x2A21A70`, 0x278 apart; HP = `+0`, max `+4`, `+0x268` the owner's handle, `+0x270` the base table's handle); 1/84 `trap_obj_sheet` `exe+0x42F7D0` returns the sheet's handle; 1/311 `trap_sheet_set_element_rate(sheet, element, rate)` `exe+0x431200` -> `exe+0x3C2650`: byte `sheet+0x1A8+element` = base (u16 at table `+0x48 + element*2`) * rate / 100, rounded up; 2/9 `trap_btlobj_set_sheet(obj, id, x)` `exe+0x431A70` -> `exe+0x411350(actor, id)` (frees the old one with `exe+0x3C07E0`, new from `exe+0x3C0280(id)`, owner set); 2/95 `trap_btlobj_dup_sheet` `exe+0x431AA0` -> `exe+0x4112B0`.

- **The removal test of actor class `exe+0x7528E8` (`B_AL110`, `B_AL120`, `M_AL020_ICEE2`; Ghidra, session 36, 2026-10-15):** vtable `exe+0x5D2D68`: `+0x38` `exe+0x419B40` (-> `exe+0x3D36D0` -> `exe+0x3B4700`, the fade route's slot), `+0x40` `exe+0x419B90` -> `exe+0x3DAC30` (the removal test), `+0x48` `exe+0x419BA0` -> `exe+0x411800` -> `exe+0x3B45C0` (disposal). The per-entity update `exe+0x3BFD30` calls `+0x40`, and `+0x48` when it says yes, every frame while `+0x120` bit 28 is set. `exe+0x3DAC30(actor)` = `exe+0x3B4420` (runs the root thread `+0x5B0` once with `exe+0x3E1C80`; yes when the script threads `+0x5B8` are 0) and (`actor+0x80` == 0 or `actor+0x98` == 0: no effect records) and (the slot at handle `+0xBB4` gone or its `+0x14` == 0 after `exe+0x3CE260` updates it; `exe+0x3CE550` reads `+0x14`). `trap_obj_leave` (1/28, `exe+0x42C810`) is the same route as world sync's removal: unless `+0x9B8` bit 4 is set it starts the fade (`exe+0x3B7810(actor+0xA08, *(exe+0x623A80), 0, 0)`), then sets bit 4.
- **How a killed effect is finished, and an actor's effect records (Ghidra, session 36, 2026-10-15):** (the part about an effect killed without bit 0 is confirmed live: table row 'An effect that runs out of particles before its kill') the effect update sets `0x10000` (killed) **without bit 0** when an effect runs out of particles (`exe+0x2BD3BD`: the particle step `exe+0x2C28E0` returned 0; the part is freed and `+0x30` zeroed). The kill `exe+0x2BE8A0` (0/51) sets `0x10001` and frees the part; `exe+0x2BE8F0` (loop end) sets bit 0. The sweep `exe+0x2BD730` (live list `*(exe+0xB1D988)`) destroys a killed effect (bit 16, not bit 26 or 22) through its vtable `[0](fx, 1)` when bit 20 is set, and sets bit 20 when bit 0 is set: an effect killed without bit 0 is never destroyed. Records: holders in a list (head `exe+0xB63608`, tail `exe+0xB63610`, next holder's handle at `+0x28`, newest record `+0x18`, oldest `+0x20`); an actor's holder is `actor+0x80`. `exe+0x2C6320(holder, owner, flag)` adds one, `exe+0x2C5730(holder)` takes it out and frees its records; the per-frame task `exe+0x2C4280` updates the records whose effect still has a part (`exe+0x2C58F0(record)`), and `exe+0x2C4660` drops a record once its effect has bit 20 (`exe+0x2BE7A0`). `exe+0x2C6490(holder)` kills every record's effect and frees the records; `exe+0x3DABE0(actor)` (that plus `exe+0x3C7300(actor+0x158)` and `exe+0x3B43C0(actor)`) is where 28 class handlers end with a `jmp`.

# Verified offsets and formats (as of 2026-11-10, session 66, before the second restructuring)

The dictionary of what we know about the game's Steam build: addresses, structures, files and rules, one line per fact. Only things confirmed **on this PC's build** go here; leads from other projects stay in the Leads section until re-verified. The full text of every entry, with each run, bench, cause and correction, is in [docs/VERIFIED_OFFSETS_HISTORY.md](docs/VERIFIED_OFFSETS_HISTORY.md): look the key up there.

Build: Steam buildid 15194255, exe SHA-256 `9002b2de6a1f91a790bd0673de125d1cf833f7942bfec827cdcf6ba64d5849ed`.

**How to read:** `exe+0x...` is an offset from the game executable's base; `actor+0x...` and the like are fields of the game's structures. 'Name in code' is the constant the DLL uses (`src/dll/`). A cell ending in … is shortened; the history file has the rest, under the same key. Dates are the last verification.

**State of this file (2026-10-22):** restructured mechanically from the old single table. Sections 1 to 4 are keyed and sorted; section 5 is an index of the findings in their old order and section 6 the old leads, both still to be rewritten into short keyed entries (see 'Gaps' at the end).

## 3. Structures
Only the rows that were keyed by a field are here. Field facts stated inside other entries are not yet folded in: mentions in the old text per base: actor 190, player 19, save 17, weapon 15, owner 13, enemy 11, move 11, entity 9, copy 8, sheet 4, entry 2, instance 1.

- **The damage of a hit is computed from the victim's sheet (Ghidra 2026-10-23, session 41):** (L126): the hit builders `exe+0x3CEE90` / `exe+0x3CE740` set hit+0x28 = `exe+0x3C12F0(victim sheet, attacker, attack, stat)`: the attacker's strength (+0x188) or magic (+0x18A) by attack +5, minus the victim's defense (+0x18C) and, at 1/4 HP or less, its +0x204 (Defender's lead); clamped; x the victim's resistance for attack +9 x +0x1AE x attack +6 / 10^6; difficulty factors for player-class victims (`exe+0x9ABD48`); the attacker's element boosts (+0x218..+0x222); at low HP x a factor (sheet+0x248, Damage Control's lead). So in the host's game a hit on the copy uses the host's defense, armor and HP. To verify on a bench.

- **A file asked for again joins the old entry, except in groups 0x8FD and 5002 (Ghidra 2026-10-26, session 45; for TODO 3.3, 1.110, the review's 5.2.6):** (L138): `exe+0x39E000(name, group, size)` (entry bytes `40 55 56 57 48 81 EC 30 01 00 00`) first returns an entry of that name already in that group (`exe+0x39E1E0(name, group)`, -1 any); else it returns the listed file of that name whose group is under 10000 as it is (its group unchanged), unless the group asked for is 0x8FD or 0x138A (5002): then a second entry with the same name is made. A dropped one (group >= 10000) takes the new group. So the copy's Keyblade files asked for in a group of ours are shared with our Sora's when both carry the same Keyblade. To verify live.
- **The pause menu's Keyblade swap frees the old weapon's files at once (Ghidra 2026-10-25, session 44; for TODO 1.110):** (L139): `exe+0x3D59C0` calls `exe+0x39D010(group)` (group = owner vtable +0x70 + slot + 1), which walks the file list and, for every entry of exactly that group (`+0x48`) that is loaded (`+1` = 3) or flagged `+0x44` bit 1, releases its resource (`exe+0x3A6A10(+0x58, 1)`), unlinks it (`exe+0x39E2A0`) and frees the entry, in the same call (no later pass). The weapon object for the new item is `exe+0x3EF8B0(kind, item)` = u32 table `*(exe+0x2AE5A38)`[table[kind] + item row u16 +4], so the look follows the body's kind (world variants of Sora's body have their own kind). `exe+0x3E0CC0`: for character 1 or 14, slot 1 reads the form record chosen by the entry's byte +0x57. The free and the object pick verified 2026-10-25 (section 1 `exe+0x39D010`, `exe+0x3EF8B0`, F333); the rest to verify.
- **The game's immediate Keyblade end (Ghidra 2026-10-25, session 44; for TODO 1.110, 3.3):** (L140): `exe+0x3EFF20(weapon)` = weapon vtable (`exe+0x5C9408`) +0x80 `exe+0x3EF610` (hides: `+0x124 |= 0x700`), +0x48 `exe+0x3EF520` (unlinks its controller from the owner's, clears the owner's `+0xD60 + 8 x slot`, `exe+0x3B45C0` ends it and marks it for the delete pass), then `exe+0x3B4E80` (frees its motion state and model instance now, sets `+0x9B8` bit 0x40 so the delete pass doesn't again). The menu swap runs it just before freeing the old files (L139). Verified 2026-10-25: section 1 `exe+0x3EFF20`.
- **The file heap layout (Ghidra 2026-10-25, session 44; for TODO 3.3):** (L141): the file list is kept sorted by group (`exe+0x39DD70`: groups 0 and up ascending, then the negative ones ascending). `exe+0x39D460` first runs `exe+0x39D590`, which only works out places: entries of group >= 10000 go to the end of the list, then each entry in list order gets the next place in its area (0x3F2: 0xE0000 bytes; 0x8FC-0x95F the player area, size `exe+0x29FB430`; 0x96A-0x973; 0x974-0x97D; 5000-5099 the Drive area, 0x300000; 5100-5199 0xC0000 when set; any other group the general area), each area starting where the last one ended (its fixed size reserved), negative groups from the heap's end down; old place stays at +0x60. Then each entry is moved or read (`exe+0x39E690`) in list order. So a file's place moves whenever an earlier file of its area changes. The place step verified 2026-10-25 (section 1 `exe+0x39D590`); the areas' sizes: to verify.

- **The draw packets are a PS2-style DMA chain; a crash there right after the copy's look goes on again from moved files (Ghidra + two PCs 2026-10-26, session 45; for TODO 1.127):** (L146): `exe+0x1ADED0` walks a chain of 16-byte commands: tag id bits 28-30 (1 cnt, 2 next, 3/4 ref, 5 call, 6 ret, as PS2 DMA tags; next and call decode a handle with `exe+0x4AD270`), opcode bits 16-25 picks a handler. Opcode 0x328 (`exe+0x1BA750`) decodes the handle at command `+8` as a texture (type magic `0x2F02EB5` at `+0x10`, `+0xA8` a byte kept in `exe+0xAD7750`) and sets the texture registers (`exe+0x19E930`). The friend's crash 20:18:09.494 (Data Larxene, the copy in Valor, the friend's own Drive into Wisdom, DLL `29d76147986e`) was there (`exe+0x1BA7A3`, the handle decoded to unmapped memory), 14 ms after copy_blade's end while moving (F343's fix ran), form_look's moves of the copy's 7 files and 'looks again (files moved)', and the second Keyblade's end and rebuild. Which draw packet still held the old handle: not known.
- **Where the place step puts each group, and what can move a file (Ghidra 2026-10-26, session 45, the list order corrected from the bench; for the review's 5.2.6):** (L147): `exe+0x39DD70` inserts an entry after the files already in its group (the list seen live is in insert order across groups: our negative entries after 2421 and before the 5000 ones asked later, F348). `exe+0x39D590` then gives places in list order: negative groups from the heap's end `*(exe+0x749740)` down, each after the negative files before it in the list (their bottom kept at `exe+0x29F33F0`); every other area starts where the files before it in group order end and reserves its fixed size (0x3F2 `exe+0x29FB42C`, 0x8FC-0x95F `+0x430`, 0x96A-0x973 `+0x434`, 0x974-0x97D `+0x438`, 5000-5099 `+0x43C`, 5100-5199 `+0x440` only when above 0, the rest the general area, `exe+0x29FB428`); in the place step only entries let go (group >= 10000) are checked against the negative files' bottom (or past 0x7F entries) and freed; **corrected 2026-10-29 (Ghidra, session 46):** right after it the file pass `exe+0x39D460` (and the move-only pass `exe+0x39D910`) walks the list and frees, instead of moving or reading, every entry of group 0 or more whose new place ends above that bottom (`exe+0x39E2A0` plus the list's release, its data let go by `exe+0x3A6A10(data, 1)` when loaded): a game area that runs into the negative files loses its own files there, silently, with any pointer to them left dangling. So a negative-group file moves when an earlier negative one changes, and a 5100-5199 file when a general-area file of a lower group changes.
- **A form's body object is per world (Ghidra 2026-10-29, session 46; the copy shows no Drive Form in the costume worlds):** (L158): `exe+0x405190(form)` reads u16 at the current character's object row (`exe+0x3E2E40`) +0x16 + 2 x form, so it gives each world's own form bodies (live: Timeless River's Limit 0x961 and Final 0x670 against 0x95D and 0x58; Sora's row 0x657 there). form_look caches forms 1-10 at its first call (`FormObject`), so in Halloween Town, Christmas Town, Space Paranoids and Timeless River the peer's form body matches no form and the copy keeps Sora's look (bench room sweep wi00, 2026-10-29). Not built: per-world forms on the copy.
- **A fault putting a boss's reaction offer into the menu during the soft reset to the title (bench room sweep 2026-10-29 02:40:42, session 46):** (L159): `exe+0x430067` (0xC0000005) inside reaction_sync's offer put, 0.45 s after 'the host's boss host#2 offers us command 112' and while the game took its HUD down; reaction_sync caught it and dropped the offer, and the game went on to the title. Not fixed: reaction_sync could drop offers once leaving for the title starts.
- **MOOSE, a DLL that enlarges KH2's file buffers (prior art, read 2026-10-29, session 46; not tried on this build):** (L160): Crazycatz00's KH2 MOOSE (https://crazycatz00.x10host.com/kh/kh2-moose.pc, v1.25033.2245, closed source, no license) sets the size of 'the cache buffer, where most files load' (our 30 MB file region) and of its 'preset blocks' such as `player` (the fixed areas, L141), plus a startup data buffer; its page says parts of the game keep signed 32-bit pointers from the PS2, so all buffers together must stay under 2 GiB (as the 64 x 32 MB handle bases, F3). Installed as dbghelp.dll or dinput8.dll (Panacea is our dbghelp). A risk if a player adds it: a region it moves to an address under our copy-files buffer would make the top take (L150) unload our files.
- **The drop lets go of negative groups too (Ghidra 2026-10-26, session 45; for the review's 5.2.6):** (L148): `exe+0x39CCA0(from)` with `from` >= 0 lets go of every entry of group >= `from`, with `from` < 0 of every entry of group <= `from`. A group under 10000 becomes 29000 if it is one of the two at `exe+0x749758`, 15400 + i if it is the i-th of the 13 at `exe+0x749760`, 30000 if negative, else group + 10000; one already let go (10000-29999) ages by +50. Only with `from` == 5000 (the Drive's) are the group-30000 entries freed at once (`exe+0x39E2A0` unlink, then the list's free). 31 callers (the room load task's `exe+0x1524E0`-`0x152A90` among them); which value the room's unload passes is to be seen live.
- **A set's file lookup wants the file in the group it asked for (Ghidra + bench 2026-10-26, session 45; for the review's 5.2.6):** (L149): `exe+0x39CC40(name, group)` takes the entry of that name in exactly that group (`exe+0x39E1E0`, -1 any), raises its users (+2) and returns its place (+0x58), with no check for none: no entry gives a read of address 4. The set fill `exe+0x3E6120` calls it with the set's group for the model and texture (-1 for the third file). A weapon's set group (`exe+0x3D7C70`) is its owner's group (+0x70 of the owner's class) + slot + 1, or 0x8FD for slot 0 of a player in some state; the crashes in bench `neg_group` (22:21:16, 22:25:11, 22:46:12, 22:52:47) were this lookup in our group + 2 (F350, F352), not the join.
- **The heap's end is a stack of blocks taken from the top (Ghidra 2026-10-26, session 45; for the review's 5.2.6):** (L150): `exe+0x39D300(size)` lowers `*(exe+0x749740)` by size (rounded up to 0x80), pushes that size at `exe+0x29FB400[--*(u32*)exe+0x749750]`, marks every file under 10000 whose data (+0x60, +0x50) reaches above the new end as unloaded (+1 = 0, +0x60 = 0), frees the let-go ones there, and clears the block (`memset`) at once; `exe+0x39D400(block)` raises the end by the size on top of that stack (by index, not by address). So a block is relative to the end as it is (not an absolute value), and negative-group files at the end are wiped the moment one is taken, before any layout. Callers: 22 (`exe+0x3E2470`: the room load's 0x80000, given back at its end; `exe+0x2CF090` / `0x2CF220`: the cutscene's 2 MB pool, L154, not the effect pool; the summon's 0x1C0000 is the cutscene work block, F353).
- **No pin flag at +0x44 (Ghidra 2026-10-26, session 45):** (L151): the place pass `exe+0x39D590` moves the let-go entries (>= 10000) to the list's end, re-inserts every entry (`exe+0x39DD70`), then sets +0x44 = 0 on every entry and gives each a place (negative groups from the end in list order, the others by their area's cursor). +0x44 bit 0 = 'placed in this pass' (set by `exe+0x39E690` when it starts on an entry), bit 1 = a let-go entry with no data (no read); both last one pass. `exe+0x39E690` also re-points a moved file's resource object (+0x658, +0x10 and the +0x670 chain's +0x80) itself.
- **A cutscene's work block size comes from its script, up to 4 MB (Ghidra 2026-10-26, session 45; for the review's 5.2.6):** (L153): the cutscene start `exe+0x2CC4B0(script, ...)` sets `DAT_140b64f80` = (float)script+4 + a constant from `exe+0x5B8F7C`, then if not 0 adds 0x40000 and caps it at 0x400000; a script with 0 there takes no block. The setup task `exe+0x2CC180` takes the block (`exe+0x2CCAB0`), the task's end `exe+0x2C85B0` gives it back. Other writers of the size: `exe+0x2C86C0`, `exe+0x2CB380`, `exe+0x2D9AA0`. So any event played mid-room (summon entries, boss in-fight scenes L65) can lower the heap's end by up to 4 MB for its length.
- **The cutscene work block's life (Ghidra 2026-10-26, session 45; the review's 5.2.6 step 4, its Ghidra hour):** (L154): (a) `exe+0x2CCAB0` keeps the block at `exe+0xB64F88` (full 64-bit) and runs a sub-allocator over it at `exe+0xB65548` (init `exe+0x2CF830`, take `exe+0x2CF680`, give `exe+0x2CF780`; list arithmetic inside the block only, its one 32-bit sum correct modulo 2^32); the event's files get slots there (`exe+0x2CED10`) while its `.win32.scd` files already go to `_aligned_malloc` memory; no read of the block against the heap's bounds found. (b) One give-back path: the task's end `exe+0x2C85B0` -> `exe+0x2CE470`, which passes `[exe+0xB64F88]` to `exe+0x39D400` and clears it; the reset `exe+0x2C86C0` that zeroes it without a give-back runs only in the boot init `exe+0x14ED40`. `exe+0x39D400` first tears down every resource object (list `exe+0x8AEF30`, data +0x658, size +0x640) whose data overlaps the block (`exe+0x12EF30(block, block + size, 1)`; a block starting 'BAR': `exe+0x3A6A10(block, 1)`), so a buffer of ours needs the same teardown at its give-back. An event at the 4 MB cap also takes a 2 MB pool (`exe+0x2CF090` / `exe+0x2CF220`, kept at `exe+0xB655F0`, its take returning to `exe+0x2CF0E9` / `exe+0x2CF28A`; set up by the cutscene's own sub-allocator init `exe+0x2CF830` on its own struct `exe+0xB655E8`, not the effect pool's `exe+0x4AF380`), given back first in `exe+0x2CEFD0`. (c) The script runner `exe+0x2CC180` has one script slot (`exe+0xB65218`, 16 bytes); `exe+0x2CC4B0` stores a script only in a free slot, so no second event takes a block while one is out.
- **Who runs the Limit preload (Ghidra 2026-10-27, session 45, corrected session 46; for TODO 1.127):** (L155): `exe+0x3D8830` (registered through `exe+0x3D8A90`) runs `exe+0x3D9130` while a Limit runs (`*(exe+0x2A24CC0)` not 0: the running Limit's object, as limit_sync.cpp reads it; not a Drive); otherwise, when the player actor's class check (`+0x78`) passes and `exe+0x3A57F0(exe+0x2A10508, 0)` is 0, `exe+0x3E7650`: for each `limt` entry (`*(exe+0x2AE5690)`) usable by `exe+0x3E81A0` whose files aren't all in (`exe+0x39DBB0`, `exe+0x3E0160`), it drops from 5000, asks each Limit's files in 5000 (`exe+0x39DF10`, `exe+0x3E0460`) and calls `exe+0x39D460(0)`. So it reloads the Limits whenever a usable Limit's files are out: after a Revert (F357, bench), a summon's end. `exe+0x39D460(task)` waits (`exe+0x39E340()`) before the place step and `exe+0x39E340(task)` after the moves; with 0 it returns with every file moved.
- **A boss's scene camera is a small cutscene aimed through one anchor (Ghidra 2026-10-28, session 46; for TODO 1.62):** (L156): `exe+0x3DB2F0(owner, name, target)` finds the 4-letter shot in `owner+0x928` (type 0x16, `exe+0x3A6480`), makes a camera of kind 0xB (`exe+0x3ABDC0`), sets the aim (`camera+0x50`: mode, actor) to the target (mode 3, 2 for a shot kind 2; `exe+0x3AD270`), makes it the event camera `*(exe+0x2A11478)` and plays the shot as a cutscene (`exe+0x2CC4B0`, L153) with `owner+0x80` as its effect context (`exe+0xB653F8`, read only by the event's effect commands `exe+0x2DAC90`). The shot's callback `exe+0x3AC630` re-aims on its events: 1 = the player `*(exe+0x2A105D0)`, 2 = mode 4: the boss anchor `*(exe+0x2AE7D30)` / `exe+0x2AE7D38`, set to the boss itself by `BTLOBJ::VTABLE<YS::BOSS>` (`exe+0x3F8C60`) and `<YS::LARGEBOSS>` (`exe+0x3F8EA0`), cleared by `exe+0x3F8E80`. The aim update `exe+0x406A90` copies the actor's +0x808 (mode 2) or +0x40 (mode 3), then follows its place each frame (`exe+0x3B5B40`, mode 7). No path reads another Sora: the host's copy reaches the friend's grab camera only if it is the target, the player, or where the friend's Sora is put.

Shots: five functions (`exe+0x21EE20`, `exe+0x21FA00`, `exe+0x220390`, `exe+0x220B70`, `exe+0x221650`, likely the five `gb::ENEMY_*` shot classes) test `exe+0x200560` and `exe+0x1FF2D0`, then damage with `exe+0x1FF340(player, shot+0x44)` (+0x18 the damage). `exe+0x21EE20` tries the friends first (`exe+0x22B690`): a friend absorbs the shot and `exe+0x22B770` adds 100 score. Contact: `exe+0x1FF4F0(player, amount)` from `exe+0x1F6900`, `exe+0x1F6B60`, `exe+0x1F6BD0`, `exe+0x213D70`. The score (section 2) is added by `exe+0x207A50` (guard byte `exe+0x72B134`). - **How a ship's place across the rail moves (Ghidra, 2026-11-08, TODO 5.2 stage 2):** (L231): the place itself is confirmed (section 2, `exe+0xAEA1A0 +0x80`). `exe+0x1E8ED0()` returns that object (143 call sites; a matrix at +0, a mode at +0x94). `exe+0x1FFE80(player)` turns the stick into a step and `exe+0x1E9B00(obj, &step)` adds it, stopping at x +-180, y +-140 for modes 0-3. - **How flight objects update, get hit and die (Ghidra, 2026-11-08, TODO 5.2 stage 3):** (L232): `exe+0x1E4D30` goes through every `gb::OBJ` by type 0-6 and calls the step (section 1), which uses vtable +8 and +0x10. `exe+0x1E2250(id, pos, rot)` builds one from a 0x60-byte row found by id (row kind 8 = type 1, 3 = type 2, 4 = type 3, 0xD = `exe+0x1F66D0`, type 4) with `exe+0x212F80`. A `gb::ENEMY`: +0x448 its param row (+6 contact damage, +8 score), +0x450 i32 HP, +0x470 flags (bit 0 going down). `exe+0x214190(enemy, attack)` takes a hit (attack +0x18 damage; +10 score for one that doesn't kill). `exe+0x213D70(enemy)` is its contact with the player; it and the friend's absorb `exe+0x22B770` are hooked but no run showed them running. Enemy shots aren't `gb::OBJ`: each class's vtable holds its hit test (`exe+0x21EE20` at `exe+0x5B5BB0`, `exe+0x21FA00` at `exe+0x5B5C08`). Score adds: `exe+0x233920` (kill), `exe+0x233A10` (+10 hit), `exe+0x233980` (friend's absorb +100), `exe+0x2339B0` (prize, from `exe+0x20E5D0`, `exe+0x20E260`). `exe+0x207A50` stops at 99,999,990 and keeps the last digit. `exe+0x207A90` raises the score's last digit unless it's 9, from `exe+0x1FD1E0` and `exe+0x1FDF90` while PLAYER +0x140C bit 0 is set (`exe+0x1FF4F0` sets it); a run's last digit matched this count (section 2, `exe+0x72B130`). Mission 1's objects were types 0, 1, 3 and 6.

Every ship value comes through `exe+0x1FD150()` (`*(exe+0xAF0540)`): HP PLAYER +0x578 and max +0x57C (the ring shows 1 - HP / max); +0x140C bit 1 and `exe+0x225F00` pick the frame's colour; +0x688 / +0x690 (a friend exists) show a formation icon, sprite +0x148; PLAYER_STATE +0x54 bit 2 adds sprite +0x150. The pips read the `gb::LOCKON` at PLAYER +0xB90 (L235): pip count +0x71C (32 at most, sprites gauge +0x48), its state +0x708, and a sight whose +0x34 == 1 lights a pip (anim 0x1A, otherwise 0x1B; state 3 shows all as 0x19). Gauge +0x18C is its alpha: it falls while the ship's place (`exe+0x1E8ED0()` +0x80) is past two fixed x, y limits, likely the ship being behind the HUD.

- **The red boss's script (`obj/G_EX260_R.mdlx`, entry `g_ex` type Bdx, decoded with OpenKH's `OpenKh.Command.Bdxio`, 2026-11-10, TODO 5.2b):** (L246): named `g_ex260`. That first roll is the only one the attack order needs. The rest: six `range(10, 20)` at the start, a heading `getf(360)` when within 100 of its wander target (L661, `syscall 3, 69` reads the place), volley spreads only for `G_EX260_G`, and after death a burst of explosions every `range(4, 10)` frames (L2705). State 6 (L1222) ends an attack when `syscall 3, 39` reads 0. No branch reads HP, a hit or the ship; it sees only whether each arm is alive and the going-down flag. With no arm alive it goes to state 7, the only place with motion 232. The arms (`G_EX261_R`, `G_EX262_R`) have no script.

Called from `exe+0x1F0F00` (a beam, each tick), `exe+0x1F1110` (a fire, several shots), `exe+0x1F6480` / `exe+0x1F67A0` (a chaser's first step: gun at +0x488), `exe+0x231DB0`, `exe+0x232490`. `exe+0x1FD150` only ever returns our own ship (`*(exe+0xAF0540)`).
- **Enemy shot kinds and who reads the ship (Ghidra, 2026-11-10, TODO 5.2b):** (L253): `exe+0x1F0C50(gun, start, direction)` fires by gun kind (+0x92): 0x21 `exe+0x21E660`, 0x22 `exe+0x21F410`, 0x23 `exe+0x220060`, 0x24 `exe+0x220690`, 0x25 `exe+0x221120` (`gb::ENEMY_HOMING`, vtable `exe+0x5B5CE0`, 0xB0 bytes, target ship pointer at +0x98, filled from `exe+0x1FD150`), 0x26 / 0x27 / 0x28 `exe+0x1F65E0` (objects 0x356, 0x1FC, 0x352, the chasers, gun kept at +0x488), then 0x47-0x55. The red boss's script builds kinds 34, 35, 37, 39 (`G_EX260_R`). `exe+0x1E6BA0(ship, out)` reads ship +8, so a swap of the ship's place covers bits 6 and 13 of `exe+0x1F1B20`. `exe+0x21EE20`, `0x21FA00`, `0x220390`, `0x220B70`, `0x221650` are per-kind hit tests against this game's own ship (`exe+0x200560`, damage `exe+0x1FF340`), not homing; `exe+0x21DAA0` turns a shot toward the ship through `exe+0x1E6C20`. Homing (0x25): its update `exe+0x221450` steers each tick toward its target +0x98's place (`exe+0x1E6BA0`) unless +0xA4 is set; the newest homing shot is `*(exe+0xAF8410)` (confirmed, section 2). The update returns 'still flying' while +0x78 (f32, life left, counted down by the frame time; set to 60 once when +0x80 bit 10 is set) is 0 or more; with +0x80 bit 1 it steers through `exe+0x1E6C20`, which also reads the target's +8. +0xA4 is set by the hit test `exe+0x221650` when the missile is within 400 units (`exe+0x585F00`) of our ship, not by the update. The shot tick `exe+0x23DB00` (driver `exe+0x221DC0`: five pools of 0x108-byte slots from `*(exe+0xAF84F8)` to `*(exe+0xAF8518)`) runs slot 3 (the effect's place), then unless +0x80 & 0x1C40 slot 8, the hit test (shot, previous place, current place): on 1 it sets +0x80 bit 10 and runs slot 4; bit 2 tests the ground (bit 11, slot 5); then slot 7, the update. The ship's radius in `exe+0x200560` is half its widest side from `exe+0x1FB350(ship + 0x488)`: the blueprint by +0x90 (0 `exe+0xAEF488`, 1 and 2 the Teeny slots), whose ship data gives `exe+0x52E520`: +2 u16 three 4-bit sides, +4 +5 +6 s8, (side - |s8|) x 10 (`exe+0x623BB4`); the hit benches used it (radius 30 and 35). The finer touch test `exe+0x1FF2D0` -> `exe+0x1FA7E0(parts)`: spheres of radius 10 around the block centres at +0xA8 (count +0xB0), and +0xB8 / +0xC0 (radius `exe+0x623B98`), built once by `exe+0x1FACA0` from the parts' +8 matrix. `gb::PLAYER`'s parts are at +0x488, `gb::FRIEND`'s at +0x440 (`exe+0x22B500` calls `exe+0x1FAB30(friend + 0x440, slot + 1)`). The red boss fires them in one volley of 5 or 6 per fight, at boss age 1075 (bench runs kh2coop_22032 / 19020, 9840 / 28116). The chasers' scripts (`G_EX100`, `G_EX110`, `G_EX120`) call no command that reads the ship.
- **Flight script commands (Ghidra, 2026-11-10, TODO 5.2b):** (L248): table 3 is `exe+0x72F1A0`, 16 bytes a row (function, flags: low bits argument count, 0x40000000 returns a value), put in slot 3 by `exe+0x1F9AA0` and cleared by `exe+0x1F9A80`. `3,35` `exe+0x226AD0` play now (`exe+0x216DC0`); `3,36` `exe+0x226B10`; `3,37` `exe+0x226B50` queue (`exe+0x216AB0`); `3,38` `exe+0x226BA0` reads the ended bit (obj +0xD8 bit 1); `3,39` `exe+0x226BE0` reads obj +0xD0; `3,8` `exe+0x227EC0` fires a gun group (`exe+0x215830`, guns listed at obj +0x478, `exe+0x1F26A0`); `3,44` `exe+0x2289C0` returns our ship; `3,178` sets the object's motion speed (+0x3EC).
- **The motion player (`gb::OBJ +0xB8`; Ghidra, 2026-11-10, TODO 5.2b):** (L249): +0x18 script's motion, +0x1C setter's motion, +0x20 flags (bit 1 ended: a one-step pulse), +0x24 length, +0x28 time, +0x38 queue write, +0x3C queue read, 4 queue rows of 0x18 at +0x40 (motion, blend, start, stored function). `exe+0x217020`, inside the setter `exe+0x217390`, writes -1 over +0x18 and +0x1C and replaces length and time; the setter then writes only +0x1C. Play now (`exe+0x216DC0`) empties the queue and writes +0x18. The tick (`exe+0x216220`) pops when time reaches length and writes +0x18; with an empty queue it only sets the ended bit. Inside one object step: tick, then the script, then the mod's `FollowHost`. So calling the setter directly restarts the clock a queued pop is waiting on (confirmed, F469).

- **How a Limit motion reaches its motion object (Ghidra, 2026-11-10, TODO 1.119):** (L254): `exe+0x3C65E0` finds the motion's entry in the Limit's BAR by name (`exe+0x3A6480(bar, -1, tag, i)`, i 0-3), decodes the entry's +8 (a handle once the file is placed, not an offset) into the motion's inner BAR and calls `exe+0x3C8A40(state, inner, ...)`. That one takes the inner BAR's first type-9 entry (`exe+0x3A6420(bar, type, n)`: the n-th entry whose u16 at +0 is `type`), decodes its +8 into the motion object, keeps it at state +0x18, calls its vtable +8 (the frame count, to +0x40; return `exe+0x3C8B0C`, the 1.119 abort), and keeps type 0x10's object at +0x20.
- **The hit effect maker with any holder (Ghidra workflow 2026-11-10, 29 of 30 claims re-read; TODO 1.31):** (L256): `exe+0x3F6410` reads only the holder (+0 PAX, +8 entries of 0x50 bytes, +0x10 DPX) and writes its records (+0x18 / +0x20) and effect +0x10 = holder; owner 0 (`0x3F6454`), as the game's own hits (`0x3D1A7E`). No check of holder +0: the game checks before (`0x3D1A33`). a, b, c = effect +0xC flags, +8 kind, +0x18; the game passes (1, 1, 10000) for hits, (1, 1, 0) for triggers; b = 4 makes no part and crashes `0x2C6BE7`. Owner 0 is read unchecked when `exe+0xB63628` is 2.
- **A motion's effect triggers and its holder (Ghidra workflow 2026-11-10):** (L257): `exe+0x3C65E0` keeps the holder raw at state +0x148 (the actor's and, with actor +0x6C8 bit 2, its weapons' states) until the next ordinary motion start (`exe+0x3C8CD0` puts back actor +0x80). Trigger type 1 (`exe+0x40DD70`) makes the effect from it when holder +0 isn't 0 (`0x40DEDF`), owner the actor; then `exe+0x3D8650` tells the Limit's script (key 0x18) only if the holder is the running Limit's +0x18, else the actor's own script gets key 0x10. The game fills free-standing holders for motions itself (`exe+0x2D9340`, table `exe+0xB653C8`).
- **The game's own Limit holder's life (Ghidra workflow 2026-11-10):** (L258): `exe+0x3D96D0` fills limit +0x18 with the Limit BAR's type-0x12 entry (`exe+0x3A6420(bar, 0x12, 0)`), only for Limits where `exe+0x3E82B0(kind)` is false (the others' effects are the Limit actor's +0x80, `exe+0x3D8A40`). The end mark changes nothing; about 1 s later, once +0x18 has no records, `exe+0x3D8F40` (`exe+0x2C6490`), `exe+0x3D83E0` (`exe+0x2C5730`), the BAR released (`exe+0x39DA10`), `exe+0x2A24CC0` = 0, freed.
- **Holder list rules (Ghidra workflow 2026-11-10):** (L259): `exe+0x2C6320` links at the tail without checking that the holder is linked already (a refill mid-list cuts the rest off): `exe+0x2C40C0` (in-list check, then `exe+0x2C5730`), then `exe+0x2C4050`, then fill. `exe+0x2C6490` and `exe+0x2C5730` kill each record's effect at once (`exe+0x2BE8A0`, which reads the effect's file data, inferred), so a holder is ended before its file moves. A map teardown unlinks every holder (`exe+0x2C47D0`, +0 = 0) or resets the list (`exe+0x2C4240`). The handle table `exe+0x2B0D720` has 64 32-MB slots, never trimmed; when full the encode silently gives slot 0.
- **The Limit script's effects (Ghidra 2026-11-10, session 66; TODO 1.31):** (L260): `trap_limit_effect_start` (2/57, `exe+0x432FB0`), `_pos` (2/58, `exe+0x433000`, which then sets the place) and `_bind` (2/59, `exe+0x4330B0`, owner = the bound object's actor) are the only callers of `exe+0x3D9B60(limit, id, a, owner)` = `exe+0x2C6B30(limit+0x18, id, a, 1, 30000, owner)`: the holder entry whose +2 is `id`, the effect from `exe+0x2BD510`, effect +0x10 = holder, place / turn / size from the entry; a 0x78-byte record (+0x40 effect, +0x48 entry, +0x50 owner, +0x74 handle of the next older record) pushed at holder +0x18 (+0x20 the oldest); an entry with flags 0x21 in field mode calls `exe+0x3C8000(owner+0x158, effect)`. `trap_effect_set_pos` (0/38, `exe+0x41DF80`) copies a vec4 to `*(effect+0x30)` (`exe+0x2BE970`, `exe+0x1A8E60`); `trap_effect_kill` (0/51, `exe+0x41E250`) = `exe+0x2BE8A0`; `trap_effect_is_active` (0/50, `exe+0x41E220`) = `exe+0x2BE780`. Trap tables: `exe+0x753490` (table 0 `exe+0x752E00`, table 2 `exe+0x756B60`, 16 bytes an entry).

## Gaps (the second half of the restructuring)
- Rewrite section 5 into short keyed entries grouped by topic (Game Over and Mickey, Drive Forms and the file heap, reaction commands and grabs, hits and damage, companions and Limits, cutscenes, title and saves, world sync and one brain, missions, effects), at most four lines each, with the addresses linked to sections 1 to 3.
- Fold the field facts of the findings and leads into section 3 (counts above), one table per structure, one row per field.
- Sort section 6: promote what a bench confirmed into sections 1 to 5, keep only true leads with a 'to verify' line.
- Add the hooked functions' signature bytes to section 1, and give every function the name the code uses.
- `tools/check_docs.py` (built 2026-10-22) warns about code constants with no entry here, long cells, broken tables and the size budget; later, `offsets.hpp` generated from a table so the two cannot drift.
